### 2026-11-28 - M2 B6 Morph-hosted Fast Model rows for the direct provider

- Goal / must-haves: close the recorded B6 gap "the direct provider's hosted model rows". B6 requires "Morph-hosted open models" as the direct second option, but the `morph` preset names no model at all (`provider-setup` therefore writes a provider definition without `model`), and nothing in the product records which Morph-hosted models exist, what they cost, or which of them is safe to select: the two abliterated rows are approval-gated and return 403 for an account without privileged access, and `kimi-latest`/`deepseek-flash-latest` are floating aliases that silently move to a new model. This slice adds one validated, frozen data module that records the twelve chat rows from Morph's own machine-readable model feed, classifies them into the B3 cheap/standard/powerful tiers, refuses the gated and floating ids for automatic selection, and gives the `morph` preset a documented default model. Data only: no client, no credential, no network, no behaviour change to B1/B2/B3.
- Must-have IDs: B6 (direct provider with its Morph-hosted open models); consumes the B1 model grammar and the B3 tier list. B6 stays open afterwards for enabling the approval-gated abliterated rows and for live smoke tests; Bifrost supervision and the in-IDE history path also remain M2 work.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/morph-models.mjs` (new), `src/stable/gateway/provider-presets.mjs` (only the `morph` preset's `defaultModel` and its comment), `tests/vibe/morph-models.test.mjs` (new), `tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs` (only a morph default-model assertion), `docs/vibe/design/m2-morph-hosted-models.md` (new), `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs; every other gateway module and its tests (`provider-client`, `provider-fallback`, `provider-router`, `provider-setup`, `secret-store`, `morph-direct`, `morph-fast-apply`, `morph-warp-grep`, `morph-router` are consumed as-is); the M3 runtime modules and tests; extension host code and the panel; build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure; new dependencies or vendored data; live network, keys or processes in tests; the M0/M1 owner-parked work and every M3 item.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/morph-models.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `MORPH_HOSTED_MODELS` is frozen, holds exactly the twelve documented chat rows in a fixed order (`morph-glm53-744b`, `morph-glm52-744b`, `morph-glm53flash`, `morph-dsv41flash`, `morph-dsv41flash-1017`, `morph-dsv4flash`, `morph-dsv4flash-0731`, `morph-kimik3`, `morph-kimik31`, `morph-kimik3-fast`, `morph-dsv41flash-abliterated`, `morph-kimik3-abliterated`), and each row is frozen with only `id`, `label`, `contextTokens`, `inputNanoUsdPerMillionTokens`, `outputNanoUsdPerMillionTokens`, `cachedInputNanoUsdPerMillionTokens` and `gated`; every price is a non-negative safe integer, every `contextTokens` is 1048576, and the documented prices match exactly (for example `morph-kimik31` 2.5/14/0.29 USD per million tokens as 2500000000/14000000000/290000000 nanodollars, `morph-dsv41flash` 0.12/0.8/0.008 as 120000000/800000000/8000000, and the fractional `morph-dsv4flash` 0.141953125/0.399625/0.0359375 as 141953125/399625000/35937500); exactly the two abliterated rows carry `gated: true`; the apply models (`morph-v3-fast`, `morph-v3-large`), the decision model (`reflex`) and the floating aliases (`kimi-latest`, `deepseek-flash-latest`) are not rows. `MORPH_TIER_MODELS` is frozen and maps `cheap` to `morph-dsv41flash`, `standard` to `morph-glm53flash` and `powerful` to `morph-glm53-744b` (the 744B GLM flagship and the same model the build loop uses for `strong`/`review`); every tier id is an ungated row and passes the B1 `validateProviderModel`; `MORPH_DEFAULT_MODEL` equals the cheap tier model and equals the `morph` preset's `defaultModel`. `morphHostedModel(id)` returns the frozen row for a known chat id and `undefined` for an unknown id, a gated id, an apply id, the decision id and a floating alias. `morphHostedModelMap({ embeddingModel })` returns a frozen six-class model map for `createMorphDirectPolicy` (`plan` and `code-edit` the powerful row, `review` the standard row, `quick-edit` and `summarize` the cheap row, `embed` the caller's embedding model), and `createMorphDirectPolicy` accepts that map unchanged. A missing `embeddingModel` fails `missing-embedding-model`; a gated embedding model fails `gated-model-unavailable`; an invalid embedding model string fails `invalid-embedding-model`; a `tierModels` entry naming a gated row, an unknown id, a floating alias or a non-row model fails `invalid-tier-model`, while a `tierModels` override naming another ungated row is accepted; unknown option keys, a non-plain-object or accessor-backed options object fail `invalid-options` without invoking a getter, and no error message echoes a supplied model id. `describeMorphModels()` returns a frozen, JSON-safe projection of the version, the source URL and read date, the tier map and the rows, and no row, map, describe result or error carries a credential, client or endpoint. `node --test tests/vibe/morph-models.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs` passes, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` passes and `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` is 0 errors and 0 warnings.
- Out of scope: enabling or offering the approval-gated abliterated rows in a product flow (their privileged access is a provider-side approval; the rows are recorded and refused, not enabled); changing any B1 preset `capabilities` field, including whether the `morph` preset advertises `embeddings` while the feed lists no embedding row; per-model pricing in the budget ledger or a cost dashboard; changing B3 routing, budgets, tiers or the B6 direct policy; live provider calls, keys or smoke tests; the Morph apply, Compact, WarpGrep and Router APIs, which are already delivered; Bifrost supervision (its pin is not yet seven days old); the wired direct-provider selection UI; an offline model-list cache, refresh scheduler or openrouter feed; any dependency.

```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

1. Read AGENTS.md, vibe/goal/GOAL.md, docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md, docs/vibe/STATUS.md and docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md.
   If this file ends with a `## LANE SCOPE` section, that section is binding: work only on the milestones it names, do not start work outside it, and record any cross-scope work you notice under "Parked for owner" in STATUS.md instead of doing it.
2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Morph tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.
```

### 2026-11-28 - M2 B3 difficulty-tier route preference and classification charging

- Goal / must-haves: close the B3 follow-up the Morph Router slice recorded: map `cheap`, `standard` and `powerful` to provider/model routes per task class, decide whether a hint may override a class's fixed route, and put the billed classify call in the budget model. The delivered policy table fixes one ordered route chain per task class and the delivered Router adapter returns a `cheap`/`standard`/`powerful` tier that the policy never consumes. This slice decides the question the conservative way and wires it: a tier hint never overrides a class's fixed route, it only reorders the class's own chain - routes tagged for the hinted tier first, then untagged (tier-agnostic) routes, then routes tagged only for other tiers, each group in policy order - so every policy route stays reachable as fallback and no route outside the validated policy can ever be contacted. It also adds a bounded `classify()` path that charges a billed classification against the same task and UTC-day budgets before it dispatches, so a classify call cannot spend tokens the budget model never saw.
- Must-have IDs: B3 (task-class routing and budgets).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/provider-router.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-router.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/morph-direct.test.mjs` (only the two `routedBy` expectations, because the frozen routing metadata now always carries `tier`), `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-routing.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-morph-router.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, plus the living-doc reconciliations the chain's owners run (`docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` only if a row's facts need it).
- Files forbidden: locked docs; every other gateway module (`morph-router.mjs` is consumed as-is; `morph-direct`, `morph-fast-apply`, `morph-warp-grep`, `provider-client`, `provider-fallback`, `provider-presets`, `provider-setup`, `secret-store`) and their tests; the M3 runtime modules and tests; extension host wiring, build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure; new dependencies; live network, keys, processes or timers in tests beyond the existing loopback mock; M0/M1/M4/M5/M6 work and the open `vibe/m3/*` slices.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-router.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `DIFFICULTY_TIERS` is the frozen `['cheap', 'standard', 'powerful']` and deep-equals the Router adapter's `ROUTER_TIERS`; `MAX_CLASSIFY_PROMPT_CHARS`, `MAX_CLASSIFY_PROMPT_BYTES` and `MAX_CLASSIFY_ANSWER_BYTES` equal the adapter's `MAX_ROUTER_INPUT_CHARS`, `MAX_ROUTER_INPUT_BYTES` and `MAX_ROUTER_RESPONSE_BYTES`, so the numbers cannot drift. A route may carry an optional `tiers` array of distinct known tiers; an unknown tier, an empty array, a duplicate, a non-array, a sparse array or an accessor-backed entry fails as `invalid-policy`, and `describe()` reports each route's `tiers` (an untagged route reports `[]`). With the policy `[standard-tagged A, powerful-tagged B, untagged C]`, `chat(cls, { taskId, messages, tier: 'powerful' })` contacts B first and reports `routedBy { taskClass, provider: B, model: B, tier: 'powerful' }`; when B fails with a retryable error the next attempt is C (untagged before other-tagged) and then A, each group in policy order; with `tier: 'cheap'` (no matching tag) and with no `tier` the chain is exactly the policy order; an all-untagged class ignores the hint; the hint never reaches a provider (no provider call carries a `tier` field) and the reservation still covers every policy route, so a hint cannot shrink the reservation. An unknown tier string, a non-string tier, or a `tier` on an embeddings request fails as `invalid-request` before any provider call. `classify()` without a configured `difficulty` adapter fails `no-difficulty-provider` with no budget change; with a configured adapter it validates `taskId` and the prompt (non-empty, well-formed, no NUL, at most 32768 characters and 131072 UTF-8 bytes) before dispatch, reserves `CLASSIFY_FRAMING_TOKENS` plus the prompt's JSON-escaped UTF-8 bytes plus `MAX_CLASSIFY_ANSWER_BYTES` against the task and day, calls the adapter once, and settles with the answer's positive integer `usage.totalTokens` or the reservation otherwise, so `status(taskId)` shows the charge; an exhausted budget fails before the adapter is called (a counting adapter proves zero calls); an adapter that throws releases the reservation; a malformed, accessor-backed or out-of-range answer fails `invalid-response` and keeps the conservative charge. The returned classification is a frozen copy of only the documented fields (tier, difficulty, confidence, meetsThreshold, needsInfo, fallback, code, status), never an adapter-supplied extra field, and `describe()` reports whether a difficulty adapter is configured.
- Out of scope: calling the Router automatically for every chat turn (M6 agent-host wiring), caching, batching or deduplicating classifications, pricing in currency, sticky cross-turn routing (B4), changing the Morph adapter's own contract, path or response cap, new provider or model rows, Bifrost supervision, live provider calls or keys in tests, and any dependency.

- Delivered: `provider-router.mjs` now exports the frozen `DIFFICULTY_TIERS` (`['cheap', 'standard', 'powerful']`, asserted equal to the Router adapter's `ROUTER_TIERS`), the three prompt/answer caps and `CLASSIFY_FRAMING_TOKENS`, validates an optional per-route `tiers` array, and preorders each task class's chain once at policy construction. `chat()` accepts a `tier` hint on chat requests only, resolves `plans[taskClass][tier ?? 'default']`, strips the field before it can reach a provider, and reports `routedBy { taskClass, provider, model, tier }` with `tier: null` when no hint was given. A hint only reorders the class's own routes - matching tags, then untagged, then other-tagged, each group in policy order - so an unmatched hint changes nothing and the reservation still covers every policy route. A new `classify()` validates the prompt against the mirrored caps (well-formed, no NUL, non-empty, `MAX_CLASSIFY_PROMPT_CHARS` checked before the scan), reserves framing tokens plus the prompt's JSON-escaped UTF-8 bytes plus `MAX_CLASSIFY_ANSWER_BYTES` against the task and UTC day, calls the configured `difficulty` adapter once, and settles with a positive safe-integer `usage.totalTokens` that fits the reservation, otherwise keeping the conservative charge; a throw releases it and a malformed answer keeps it. `usageTokens()` reads `answer.usage` and `usage.totalTokens` through descriptor-safe `ownData` inside `try/catch`, so an accessor or Proxy getter is never invoked and anything but a non-negative safe integer degrades to "no usage"; `classify()` additionally ignores a count above its own reservation. `describe()` reports each route's `tiers` and whether a difficulty adapter is configured. Docs reconciled: `design/m2-provider-routing.md` gains the tier-ordering and classification-charge sections and the new `createProviderRouter` signature, `design/m2-morph-router.md` and the `ROADMAP.md` M2 cell mark the B3 wiring delivered, and `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` and this file follow.

- Tooling note for the loop: `gh pr create` fails in this runner with `failed to run git: 'merge' is not recognized...` because `git` resolves to the goal-runner's `git.cmd` guard shim, so Go runs it through `cmd.exe`, which splits gh's unescaped `|` in the `git config --get-regexp ^branch\.<branch>\.(remote|merge|pushremote|gh-merge-base)$` probe. Prepending `C:\Program Files\Git\cmd` to `PATH` for the gh invocation (real `git.exe`, no shim) fixes it; pushing and the other gh subcommands are unaffected.

### 2026-11-27 - M3 cross-process install lock and descriptor-bound archive read

- Goal / must-haves: close the last two gates ADR-0011 decision 7 records for enabling the installer by default or bundling the binary - "a cross-process lock and a descriptor-bound part-file read" - so two application instances cannot install into one runtime directory and a local writer that swaps the part path cannot feed bytes other than the ones the transfer wrote into extraction. The signed/authenticated manifest stays the owner-parked optional hardening route, and the wired product path that supplies the installer-owned application-data root stays M6/M12.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs`, `tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0011-nightly-llama-cpp-intake.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, plus the living-doc reconciliations their owners run in the chain (`docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` with no row change expected).
- Files forbidden: locked docs; `vibe/sidecars.json` and `vibe/models/catalog.json`; the `llama-server` supervisor, model downloader, hardware probe, `app-data`, `ollama`, `local-provider` and third-party notices modules and their tests; the first-run wizard/panel and their tests; gateway and provider modules; build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure; new dependencies; live network access in tests beyond the existing loopback fixture; process access in tests except short-lived local `node` children (one that exits immediately to supply a dead pid and one at a time that holds the install lock); M0/M1 owner-parked work and all M2/M4/M5/M6 work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar-intake.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `installSidecar` serializes one target directory across processes through an exclusive-create lock file in the target directory (`.vibecodium-install.lock`) whose record holds only the writing pid, the creation time and a random token; a lock held by a live foreign process makes a second install fail `busy` after a bounded wait with zero fetch requests, no extraction and no installed file (the target directory may be created), while a lock whose recorded pid is dead is broken and the install proceeds and removes it, and a lock record older than the hard stale bound is broken even when its recorded pid is live. A malformed, oversized or symlinked lock record is never followed, never crashes the installer and is treated as held until the age bound; a record that names this process is treated as a leftover and broken; the in-process `busy` guard stays in front of it. After a successful, a checksum-failed and an aborted install no `.lock`, `.part`, `.install-*` or temporary file remains, and a second install succeeds. A real second process that is inside `installSidecar` (fetch entered) makes the parent's install fail `busy`; after that child is killed, the parent breaks the dead-pid lock and installs. The archive is read back through the same descriptor that received the transfer (`readSync` at explicit offsets) instead of by path: a fake fetch that, after its last chunk, unlinks the part path and writes a different valid ZIP at that path still installs exactly the pinned bytes (on a host that cannot replace an open part file the swap assertion is replaced by a diagnostic; it ran for real here), and the read is bounded by the pinned archive size. The lock record and every error stay free of the URL, path, digest and archive body.
- Out of scope: a signed or authenticated manifest, a kernel-level file lock (`flock`/`LockFileEx`) or a lockfile directory service, a resumable download, making `ownedRoot` mandatory, the packaged catalog copy, signing, the Microsoft VC++ runtime, the first-run wizard/panel and host wiring, any pin, digest, model or catalog change, and any dependency.
- Parked cross-scope note: the signed-manifest route (needs an owner-owned signing key), the wired application-data root (M6/M12), the packaged catalog copy and the clean-machine live smoke stay open as recorded; no owner decision is needed and no other cross-lane work was noticed.
- Branch: `vibe/m3/install-lock` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs` now serializes installs into one target directory across processes and reads the transfer back through the descriptor that received it. A new lock block (`delay`, `isProcessAlive`, `readLockRecord`, `readLockState`, `acquireInstallLock`, `releaseInstallLock`) creates `.vibecodium-install.lock` with `openSync(..., 'wx', 0o600)` and writes only `{ pid, createdAt, token }` (token 16 hex); the loser re-reads the record under a deadline checked at the top of each loop and fails `busy` ('this sidecar directory is already installing') after about `LOCK_WAIT_MS` (1 s plus one retry), before any fetch, extraction or installed file (the directory may already be created). A record is read through `lstatSync`, then one read-only handle opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`/`O_NONBLOCK` where the platform has them, `fstatSync`ed, read at most 1024 bytes and closed in `finally`, so an oversized, non-regular or (where supported) symlinked record is held until the age bound instead of followed; a malformed record stays held. A record is stale when its pid is dead or this process, or when both its `createdAt` and the file mtime are older than `LOCK_STALE_MS` (2 h, `Math.max` of the two); a stale record is broken by one `renameSync` to `<lock>.break-<token>` (random suffix when the token is unreadable), the renamed file is re-read and removed only when it is still the same record (type plus token, or mtime when there is no token), and a record that changed in the gap is restored with `linkSync`/unlink unless another instance created the lock path first. `releaseInstallLock` removes the lock only when the on-disk token matches. The verified-install skip still runs before the lock, and a process that waited re-inspects and adopts a runtime another process just installed. `downloadArchive` now creates the `.part` file `wx+`, writes the response in bounded chunks through one descriptor, checks size and read-back, and returns the bytes read by offset from that same handle (1-byte overrun probe), so the digest and ZIP parse cannot be fed by a swapped path; `readArchive` enforces the pinned size. No new dependency, file, pin or CI change.
- Tests: `node --check src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs` clean; `node --test tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar-intake.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs` PASS (90/90: sidecar 58 including 11 new, intake 14, wizard 18); `node vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs` PASS (`ok      llama-server b12426 published 2026-11-15, eligible 2026-11-22`); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (6/6 steps, 921 tests: 911 pass, 10 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `git diff --check` clean. The new sidecar tests cover a dead-pid break with no `.vibecodium*` residue, a live foreign holder failing `busy` with zero fetch, a live-pid record older than the hard bound, malformed/oversized/symlinked records held, token-mismatch release, the skip before the lock, post-lock adoption, a real second process entering the installer, the descriptor-bound archive read with a path swap (it ran for real here, no diagnostic fired), and no lock, part or `.break-` file left after the outcome.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS (independently re-ran the acceptance set 90/90, verify 6/6, docs 0/0 and 13/13 locks, probed the lock states and ordering, and confirmed the new suite fails on the branch point; its non-blocking findings - an overstated test comment, the lock mode and the descriptor-swap diagnostic - were fixed). drift-sentinel PASS after one FAIL round: it caught a stale design sentence that still said cross-process installs were not serialized (blocking), inconsistent decision-7 gate accounting in ROADMAP/ADR, and the "fresh record is never deleted" and wait wording; all were corrected and the re-audit passed with no new contradiction. security-reviewer PASS on the third pass: the first round's unbounded path read, the lstat/open check race, the read-then-unlink stale break and the spurious `busy` were fixed by the bounded descriptor read, the single-winner rename break, the missing-lock retry and the token gate; its residual notes (Windows has no `O_NOFOLLOW`, so a swap is bounded and stat'ed but can point at another regular file; pid reuse; wall-clock jumps; a write-capable local process can still delete a live lock) are recorded in SECURITY. docs-keeper PASS (docs-check 0/0 after removing one stale sentence from the intake entry; no locked doc). governance-verifier not applicable (no locked path, 13/13 locks, no approval record needed); tech-scout not applicable (no dependency change).

```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

1. Read AGENTS.md, vibe/goal/GOAL.md, docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md, docs/vibe/STATUS.md and docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md.
   If this file ends with a `## LANE SCOPE` section, that section is binding: work only on the milestones it names, do not start work outside it, and record any cross-scope work you notice under "Parked for owner" in STATUS.md instead of doing it.
2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Morph tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.

## LANE SCOPE

This lane owns milestone M3 only: the local runtime sidecar (llama-server), the model downloader, and the first-run wizard.

Do not start M2 items (lane A owns M2 completion) and do not start M4/M5/M6 work. If you find work outside M3 that matters, record it under "Parked for owner" in docs/vibe/STATUS.md and continue with M3.
```

### 2026-11-27 - M3 embedding batch flag and profile-config isolation

- Goal / must-haves: close the two recorded local-runtime posture follow-ups that need no owner decision. The delivered embedding plan hands the supervisor `--embedding --pooling last -ub 8192`, but at the pinned `b12426` build the default logical batch of 2048 clamps the effective ubatch, so the plan batches at 2048 despite the `-ub` value; add the matching `-b 8192` to the same frozen constant so no caller rebuilds the flags. The supervisor's child environment is an allowlist, but `b12426` applies `%PROGRAMDATA%\llama.cpp\config.ini` and `%APPDATA%\llama.cpp\config.ini` before argv (and `/etc/llama.cpp/config.ini` and `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/llama.cpp/config.ini` elsewhere), and the Windows paths are resolved from environment names a caller `env` override can add, so a config file could still set a remote model or projector source past the remote-model argument denials. Refuse those profile names in `env` overrides so no caller `env` can redirect the delivered Windows child at a profile config, and record that the pin has no config-disable switch or variable (the POSIX `/etc` and passwd-home config paths stay recorded as the residual).
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs`, `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs`, `tests/vibe/first-run-embedding.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs` (it gains only an `envKeys` field so the child environment can be proved rather than assumed), `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-hardware-probe.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, plus the living-doc reconciliations the chain's owners run: `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (the runtime row records the `-b`/config-root facts and the POSIX residual) and `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs; the sidecar manifest and installer, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware-probe module, the `ollama` module, the third-party notices module and generator, `app-data.mjs`, the first-run panel `.cjs` and its service, the gateway and provider modules, extension host wiring, build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure, new dependencies, live network or real `llama-server` access in tests (the local fixture gains only the `envKeys` field), M0/M1 owner-parked work and all M2/M4/M5/M6 work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/first-run-embedding.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `EMBEDDING_SERVER_ARGS` is the frozen `['--embedding', '--pooling', 'last', '-b', '8192', '-ub', '8192']` (logical batch ahead of the ubatch), and `embeddingPlan()`, the view's embedding section and `embeddingRuntimeOptions()` all carry that same frozen array, with the hand-off still accepted unchanged by `createLlamaServer`; the panel renders `--embedding --pooling last -b 8192 -ub 8192` escaped. `createLlamaServer` refuses a caller `env` override naming `APPDATA`, `PROGRAMDATA`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `HOME` in any casing as `invalid-env` without echoing the value, while an ordinary override still passes and reaches the child; a started child's environment never carries any of those names.
- Out of scope: pinning a llama.cpp build with a config-disable switch or `--no-config`, shipping a private `APPDATA` to isolate the user profile, any real `config.ini` test, the live loopback smoke (owner-parked on the Microsoft VC++ runtime), changing the supervisor's `-b` handling for chat (a trusted caller flag), refusing the router-mode `--models-dir`/`--models-preset` INI indirection (delivered in the 2026-11-28 entry), sidecar manifest signing, packaging and the installed-notice/catalog copies, and any dependency.

- Original prompt (verbatim):

```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

1. Read AGENTS.md, vibe/goal/GOAL.md, docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md, docs/vibe/STATUS.md and docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md.
   If this file ends with a `## LANE SCOPE` section, that section is binding: work only on the milestones it names, do not start work outside it, and record any cross-scope work you notice under "Parked for owner" in STATUS.md instead of doing it.
2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Morph tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.
## LANE SCOPE

This lane owns milestone M3 only: the local runtime sidecar (llama-server), the model downloader, and the first-run wizard.

Do not start M2 items (lane A owns M2 completion) and do not start M4/M5/M6 work. If you find work outside M3 that matters, record it under "Parked for owner" in docs/vibe/STATUS.md and continue with M3.
```

### 2026-11-27 - M3 supervisor remote-model flag spellings

- Goal / must-haves: close the short-alias gap the M3 embedding-plan security review recorded against the delivered supervisor. llama.cpp `b12426` registers `-hfr` as a second short alias of `--hf-repo` beside `-hf`, and `-hff`/`--hf-file` and `-hft`/`--hf-token` as the file and token switches, while the supervisor's denied set names only `-hf` and the `--hf-repo` long name. A caller-supplied prefix argument could therefore point the child at a remote GGUF repository, which the local single-model posture refuses. The security-reviewer then found the same pin's eleven value-less preset switches also write a remote repository and the server downloads it in place of the validated local model. Add every such spelling to the denied set without refusing legitimate flags, and reconcile the design note that recorded the gap.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs`, `tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, plus the living-doc reconciliations the chain's owners run: `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (the runtime row records the `-b`/config-root facts and the POSIX residual), `docs/vibe/README.md` and `docs/vibe/design/m3-local-provider.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs; the installer, model downloader and catalog, hardware probe, ollama module, third-party notices, the first-run module and panel (consumed as-is), the gateway and provider modules, extension host wiring, build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure, new dependencies, live network or process access in tests, M0/M1 owner-parked work and all M2/M4/M5/M6 work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `createLlamaServer` refuses `-hfr <repo>`, `-hff <file>`, `-hft <token>`, `-hfd <file>`, `-hfrd <repo>`, `-mmu <url>`, `--hf-file <file>`, `--hf-token <token>`, `--spec-draft-hf <repo>`, `--hf-repo-draft <repo>`, `--mmproj-url <url>` and each of the eleven value-less remote presets (`--embd-gemma-default`, `--fim-qwen-1.5b-default`, `--fim-qwen-3b-default`, `--fim-qwen-7b-default`, `--fim-qwen-30b-default`, `--fim-qwen-7b-spec`, `--fim-qwen-14b-spec`, `--gpt-oss-20b-default`, `--gpt-oss-120b-default`, `--vision-gemma-4b-default`, `--vision-gemma-12b-default`) with case and underscore spellings, and the `=` form of each long switch that takes a value, as `invalid-command-args`, alongside the existing `-hf`/`--hf-repo`/`-mu`/`-dr`/`-ag` cases, while a legitimate flag such as `-b`, `-ub`, `--chat_template`, `--keep`, `-t`, `-c`, `-ngl`, `-md`, `--spec-draft-threads`, `--spec-draft-ngl` or `--spec-default` is still accepted and passed literally; the pinned `b12426` source registers `-hf`/`-hfr` on `--hf-repo`, `-hff`/`-hft` on the file and token switches, `-hfd`/`-hfrd` on `--spec-draft-hf`, `-mmu` on `--mmproj-url`, and the eleven presets as the other writers of `params.model.hf_repo`/`params.speculative.draft.mparams.hf_repo`; the rejection never echoes the argument value. (The qa-verifier widened the matrix to the draft and projector sources and the security-reviewer found the preset family; the overseer accepted both extensions, checked each spelling against the downloaded `b12426` `common/arg.cpp`, and amended this card.)
- Out of scope: a live install/start smoke (owner-parked on the Microsoft VC++ decision); the embedding `-b 8192` clamp recorded as its own M3 follow-up; the `--path` static-file surface; the pinned build's `config.ini` remote-source surface (recorded as an M3 follow-up in `design/m3-llama-server.md` because `b12426` has no config-disable switch); any pin, digest, catalog, installer, wizard or panel change; any dependency.

- Result: the supervisor's `commandArgs` refusal now names every `b12426` switch that sets a remote model, draft or projector source, so a caller prefix can no longer download a remote GGUF, draft or projector past the validated local model. The fix widens `DENIED_SERVER_ARGS` with the short aliases (`-hfr`, `-hff`, `-hft`, `-hfd`, `-hfrd`, `-mmu`) and the eleven value-less presets (`--embd-gemma-default`, the four `--fim-qwen-*-default` presets, `--fim-qwen-7b-spec`, `--fim-qwen-14b-spec`, `--gpt-oss-20b-default`, `--gpt-oss-120b-default`, `--vision-gemma-4b-default`, `--vision-gemma-12b-default`), and adds `--hf-file`, `--hf-token`, `--spec-draft-hf` and `--mmproj-url` to `DENIED_SERVER_ARG_PREFIXES` (`--hf-repo` already covers `--hf-repo-draft`). The local `-md` draft and the ngram-only `--spec-default` stay accepted, and the fail-closed error still never echoes the value. The docs now describe the exact set, and the pin's `config.ini` surface is recorded as a follow-up rather than implied closed.

The same binary had passed the whole-repository run at `59f32a4`; the detector is statistical, so the file is ASCII now rather than tuned to a size.

### 2026-11-27 - M3 sidecar intake gates (source-to-URL binding and the enforced seven-day rule)

- Goal / must-haves: close the two gaps that left ADR-0011 decision 7's manifest gate unenforced and that the sidecar design note records - the digest protects only one URL, and `publishedAt`/`source` are validated for shape but never enforced - so the manifest loader binds every remote archive URL to the recorded `source` and `version`, the installer and a new `verify` step refuse a pin that has not been public for seven days, and a re-pin without a review fails closed in the product instead of relying on the radar row. The signed-manifest hardening route and the cross-process lock/descriptor-bound part-file read stay open.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs`, `vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs` (new), `vibe/tools/verify.mjs`, `tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/sidecar-intake.test.mjs` (new), `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0011-nightly-llama-cpp-intake.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, plus the living-doc reconciliations their owners run in the chain (`docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` with no row change expected).
- Files forbidden: locked docs; `vibe/sidecars.json` and `vibe/models/catalog.json`; the `llama-server` supervisor, model downloader, hardware probe, `ollama` and third-party notices modules and their tests; `app-data.mjs`; the first-run wizard/panel and their tests; gateway and provider modules; build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure; new dependencies; live network or process access in tests; M0/M1 owner-parked work and all M2/M4/M5/M6 work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar-intake.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `validateSidecarManifest`/`loadSidecarManifestFile` accept a remote archive URL only when it is exactly `https://github.com/<source>/releases/download/<version>/<file>.zip` with a plain `.zip` asset name: another host, owner, repository, tag, an extra path segment, a non-`.zip` asset, a missing or percent-encoded name, an explicit `:443`, a non-canonical host spelling, a trailing slash and a non-release path each fail `invalid-manifest` with the fixed message `target url is not bound to the sidecar source and version` that never echoes the URL; a loopback `http` pin is exempt (the local test seam) while an `https` loopback pin is not, and a `ftp:`/non-loopback `http:` URL still fails before the binding. `assertIntakePolicy(manifest, { now })` validates the options and `now` first, then the whole manifest, and refuses a pin younger than `INTAKE_MIN_PUBLIC_DAYS` (7) at the exact UTC-midnight boundary (`intake-too-recent`, message naming id and version only), refuses a pin dated in the future with the same code, and fails `invalid-options` for a non-`Date`, an invalid `Date`, an unknown key or an accessor-backed `now` that is never invoked; a malformed manifest still fails `invalid-manifest`. `installSidecar` refuses a too-recent pin before any option read, fetch or write (fetch counter 0, target directory not created), while `verifySidecarInstall` on an installed or missing runtime is independent of the window. `node vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs` passes on the shipped pin, fails with `--now 2026-11-21` and passes with `--now 2026-11-22`, and refuses `2026-02-30`, `2026-1-1`, `not-a-date` and a value-less `--now` as `invalid-options`; `verify` runs the check as its own step.
- Residual risk: the `sidecar-intake` `verify` step reads the host clock, so it fails on a host clocked before a pin's eligible date and, after each re-pin, gates slices unrelated to the runtime for seven days; that is the intended consequence of ADR-0011 decision 2, and `--now YYYY-MM-DD` replays a recorded date. The manifest remains unauthenticated: a swapped manifest can still name another canonical asset with a rewritten digest, a residual risk that a signed manifest would close.
- Out of scope: an authenticated/signed manifest, the cross-process lock and the descriptor-bound part-file read, making the installer enable-by-default or bundling the binary, changing the pin (`b12426`), any digest, catalog, model or sidecar JSON change, the first-run wizard/panel, host wiring, signing, packaging, the Microsoft VC++ runtime decision, any dependency, and any live network or process smoke.
- Parked cross-scope note: the signed-manifest route, the cross-process lock with its descriptor-bound part-file read, the host wiring of the installer-owned application-data root, the packaged catalog copy and the clean-machine live smoke stay open M3/M6/M12 work as recorded; no owner decision is needed and no other cross-lane work was noticed.
- Branch: `vibe/m3/intake-gate` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs` exports `INTAKE_MIN_PUBLIC_DAYS` (7) and `MAX_ZIP_ENTRIES` (4096), binds each remote archive URL to the pin and enforces the public window. `assertRemoteUrl` now returns the parsed URL; the new `assertReleaseBinding(parsed, value, source, version)` leaves a loopback `http:` pin alone, requires the last path segment to match `RELEASE_FILE` (`^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*\.zip$`) and the whole URL to equal `'https://github.com/' + source + '/releases/download/' + version + '/' + file`, and otherwise fails `invalid-manifest` with the fixed `target url is not bound to the sidecar source and version`; `validateTarget` takes `source` and `version` from `validateSidecar`, so the check runs on the load path (`validateSidecarManifest`/`loadSidecarManifestFile`) for every platform target. `eligibleAt(sidecar)` is `publishedAt` midnight UTC plus the window; the new `assertPinEligible(sidecar, now)` fails `intake-too-recent` (message names id and version only, built from the constant) and the same code covers a future date; the new `assertIntakePolicy(value, options)` reads `now` from own data descriptors, refuses a non-`Date`/invalid `Date`/unknown key/accessor with `invalid-options` without invoking a getter, then validates the manifest and applies the window per pin, returning a frozen manifest. `installSidecar` calls `assertPinEligible(validated, new Date())` immediately after `validateSidecar`, before any option read, fetch or write, and `verifySidecarInstall` stays clock-independent. New `vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs` (`checkSidecarIntake(root, { now })`, `parseNow`) runs the same policy over `vibe/sidecars.json`, prints `ok      <id> <version> published <date>, eligible <date>` per pin, accepts `--now YYYY-MM-DD` (rejects `2026-02-30`, `2026-1-1`, `not-a-date` and a value-less flag as `invalid-options`) and fails the run with `intake-too-recent`; `vibe/tools/verify.mjs` runs it as its own `sidecar-intake` step between `notices` and `tests`. `tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs` replaces the placeholder host with `PINNED_URL`/`PINNED_SECRET_URL` pinned-release URLs (a fragment test and the URL-rejection cases keep their standalone URLs), and the new `tests/vibe/sidecar-intake.test.mjs` covers the binding matrix, the loopback exemption and its `https` boundary, the exact seven-day boundary at `2026-11-22T00:00:00.000Z`, a future date, invalid and accessor-backed `now`, the shipped pin, install refusal before any fetch or directory creation, clock-independent `verifySidecarInstall`, and the CLI pass/fail/`--now` paths. Docs reconciled: `design/m3-sidecar-binary.md` (binding is a loader-side validator, window is a policy/installer check, invalid or early clock fails closed, forward skew is a residual, the host-clock `verify` gate, next steps), `adr/0011` (decision 2 and decision 7 amended), `SECURITY.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `README.md` and this entry. The pin, catalog, digests, first-run wizard and every forbidden module are untouched. Patch budget: 10 tracked files plus 2 new files, about 540 touched lines (product delta `+57/-4` in `sidecar.mjs` and `+1` in `verify.mjs`, the rest tests and docs); over the rough 400-line budget, but the product change is a single bounded module and the new suite carries it, so no follow-up split is needed.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar-intake.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs` PASS (79/79, 0 fail; sidecar 47, intake 14, wizard 18); `node vibe/tools/sidecar-intake.mjs` PASS (`ok      llama-server b12426 published 2026-11-15, eligible 2026-11-22`); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (6/6 steps, 895 tests: 885 pass, 10 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok and `check` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS (it re-ran the acceptance set, verify 6/6, docs 0/0, 13/13 locks, and probed 58 hostile shapes independently - tag-prefix, dot segments, `%2e`, backslash, `:443`, `GitHub.com`, trailing space, `.ZIP`, leading-hyphen name, non-release path - plus loopback narrowness, unusable clocks, accessor options, install ordering and the mutation check that the new suite fails on the branch point; its findings were non-blocking: the message now interpolates `INTAKE_MIN_PUBLIC_DAYS` instead of hardcoding "7-day", and three doc-honesty misattributions were fixed by docs-keeper). drift-sentinel PASS (exactly the card paths, no locked file, no dependency, pin, catalog, CI or governance record; it flagged the loader/policy conflation in the ADR, design note and SECURITY plus the forward-skew overclaim, all fixed, and recorded the patch-budget overage and the wall-clock `verify` consequence now in this entry). security-reviewer PASS (no URL, path or secret echoed by the fixed binding message, `assertRemoteUrl` messages, the intake message or the CLI; fail-closed on every clock and option shape probe; hostile owner/repo/tag/host/port/encoded-segment/backslash/userinfo shapes refused; the loopback exemption is `http:` on `127.0.0.1`/`[::1]` only and `https:` loopback fails; the URL basename never becomes a file name and archive entries keep their existing validator; no new host, dependency, license or CI change; residual risks - unauthenticated manifest swap, loopback seam, wall-clock dependence - are documented). docs-keeper PASS (docs-check 0/0, verify 6/6) after fixing the ADR, design note and SECURITY attributions, adding the wall-clock and residual notes, tightening the gate-count wording in ROADMAP, CHANGELOG and this entry, and clearing a stale loader claim in a source comment and a test header; no locked doc proposal was needed. governance-verifier not applicable (no locked path changed: 13/13 locks, `governance check` 0 errors, no approval record written). tech-scout not applicable (no dependency, lockfile, pin, digest, binary or catalog change; the radar row and the 2026-12-03 review date are untouched). release-manager not applicable (in-milestone; no version, artifact, feed, signing or publication change; the CHANGELOG bullet stays under `[Unreleased]`).
- Next step: the next M3 slice is the host wiring that passes `runtime.auth().apiKey` into the B1 `llama-cpp` client and one live loopback install/start smoke with the pinned `b12426` binary; keep the signed/authenticated manifest, the wired installer-owned application-data root, the packaged catalog copy and the clean-machine smoke open for M3/M6/M12, and re-evaluate the pin at the 2026-12-03 radar review.

CI on `408e350` passed both `verify` runs, `ecformat`, both `zizmor` runs, both `compile` jobs, the Devin review and the RISCV64, LOONG64 and ARM32 build legs; `build (macos-14, arm64)` failed in 1 s on its unsupported runner label (the known infrastructure limitation) and `reh_linux (X64)` failed while installing the GitHub CLI after repeated `429` responses (an external rate limit, not a slice failure). ### 2026-11-27 - M3 local provider host bridge

- Goal / must-haves: finish the M3 step the loopback-auth slice recorded as next - hand the supervisor's `runtime.auth().apiKey` to the B1 `llama-cpp` client - so the host can start the local runtime and get a ready client in one call. A narrow bridge starts the delivered supervisor from the first-run plan (or adopts a selected Ollama daemon), reads the key through the one accessor that exposes it, and returns a frozen B1 client, with a bounded failure model and no key in any snapshot, error or serialization. The pinned start plan also gains the catalog chat entry's `id` as the B1 model name, so the host does not have to map the installed file back to a provider model.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/local-provider.mjs` (new), `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs`, `tests/vibe/local-provider.test.mjs` (new), `tests/vibe/first-run.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-local-provider.md` (new), `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0010-local-runtime-llama-server-supervisor.md` (docs-keeper reconciliation), `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs; `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs` and its tests (consumed as-is), the sidecar manifest and installer, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware probe, the `ollama` module, the third-party notices module and generator, `provider-client.mjs` and the other gateway modules (consumed as-is), the extension panel and service, extension host wiring, build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure, new dependencies, live network or process access in tests, M0/M1 owner-parked work and all M2/M4/M5/M6 work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/local-provider.test.mjs`, `node --test tests/vibe/first-run.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `createLocalProvider({ wizard, policy })` returns a frozen controller with `start`/`stop`/`dispose`/`status`/`describe`/`client`; a blocked wizard fails `runtime-unavailable` with a bounded reason and spawns nothing; an unknown or hostile blocked reason becomes `unavailable`; a non-plain or accessor-backed wizard fails `invalid-options` without invoking the accessor; a malformed hand-off or plan fails closed before any spawn; a throwing wizard reports `handoff-failed` without its message; a pinned plan starts the supervisor and hands a working authenticated `llama-cpp` client whose chat, streaming, tool calls and embeddings pass through the loopback fixture while `status()`, `describe()` and their JSON serialization contain no key; concurrent starts spawn one child; a supervisor failure reports its bounded code and leaves nothing running; a stop during a start ends `stopped` with no failure and a later start succeeds reusing the runtime and key; `dispose()` stops the child and refuses a later start; a selected Ollama daemon becomes an `ollama` client without spawning or reading the supervisor plan; a daemon hand-off with a hostile base URL or model fails closed; a private-LAN or remote daemon is refused even with a permissive caller policy and an IPv6 loopback daemon is accepted; a caller policy is snapshotted so a later array mutation cannot widen egress; `resolveRuntimeApiKey` validates the supervisor key without echoing it. The pinned `startPlan()` now carries `model: chatEntry.id`.
- Out of scope: a live install/start smoke with the pinned binary; packaging and the packaged catalog copy; signing; the extension panel and host wiring; provider setup and the other M2 modules; any dependency.
- Original prompt (verbatim):

```text
The previous request exceeded the provider's size limit due to large media attachments. The conversation was compacted and media files were removed from context. If the user was asking about attached images or files, explain that the attachments were too large to process and suggest they try again with smaller or fewer files.

Continue if you have next steps, or stop and ask for clarification if you are unsure how to proceed.
```

- Branch: `vibe/m3/local-provider` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/local-provider.mjs` is the host bridge. `createLocalProvider({ wizard, policy? })` reads only its two option keys through own data descriptors (a live Proxy is read only through prototype/descriptor operations, so a `get` trap and a getter are never invoked; an unknown key fails `invalid-options`), snapshots `policy` into a deep-frozen bag (own data properties only; `allowHosts`/`allowEndpoints` are copied and frozen so a later `push` cannot widen egress), and returns a frozen controller with a single-flight `start()`: an unavailable `runtimeOptions()` hand-off fails `runtime-unavailable` with a bounded reason (`not-ready`, `runtime-not-bundled`, `runtime-install-failed`, `disposed`, `no-embedding-model`, else `unavailable`); a `kind: 'ollama'` hand-off builds an `ollama` client only when the preset-validated `baseUrl` also classifies as a loopback `local-model` endpoint (`localhost`/`*.localhost`, any `127.0.0.0/8` address, `::1`) and the `model` is valid, so a private-LAN or remote daemon is refused even with a permissive caller policy; a pinned hand-off reads `startPlan()`'s B1 `model`, starts (or reuses) the supervisor with the plan's supervisor options, reads `server.auth()` through `resolveRuntimeApiKey` (own data descriptors, the supervisor's 32..128 length bound, no control or bidi character), and builds a `llama-cpp` client with `baseUrl`, `model`, `apiKey`, `policy` and an empty `env`. `stop()` is idempotent and safe during a start, keeps the supervisor controller so a later start reuses the same runtime and key, and ends `stopped` with `failure: null`; `dispose()` stops once and refuses later starts. `status()`/`describe()` share one frozen snapshot (`version`, `state`, `kind`, `id`, `label`, `baseUrl`, `model`, `port`, `pid`, `failure`) and never carry the key; `client()` is the only way to reach the client. A failed start stops any child it started, clears the client/port/pid and ends `failed` with a bounded code (a supervisor code that matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,63}$`, else `start-failed`). `first-run.mjs` `startPlan()` now returns `model: chat.entry.id`; the plan deepEquals in `first-run.test.mjs` and `first-run-sidecar.test.mjs` were updated. `isPlainObject` catches a revoked Proxy on `Array.isArray`; the deepEqual is key-free. Docs: new `design/m3-local-provider.md`, `design/m3-llama-server.md` (host hand-off), `design/m3-first-run-wizard.md` (plan `model`), `README.md` index, `ROADMAP.md` M3 row, `CHANGELOG.md`, `SECURITY.md` and this entry.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/local-provider.test.mjs` PASS (15/15); `node --test tests/vibe/first-run.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs ...` PASS (the first-run, sidecar, supervisor and provider suites: 148 pass, 1 environment-conditional skip); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (5/5 steps, 879 tests, 0 fail; 869 pass / 10 environment-conditional skips on the last run, and 868 / 11 on an earlier one - the split follows whether the host has a logon session for the Credential Manager round-trip); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings).
- Guardians: `qa-verifier` PASS on the first implementation (acceptance 13/13, cross-suite 79 pass / 1 skip, `verify` 877 tests); `drift-sentinel` PASS (exactly the card-allowed paths, no locked, dependency or CI change, no docs overclaim); `security-reviewer` FAIL -> fixed -> PASS (two MEDIUM: the policy freeze was shallow so a caller array could widen egress, and a daemon `baseUrl` was not constrained to loopback; both reproduced as attacks, closed by `snapshotPolicy` and the `classifyAIEndpoint` + loopback-host check, and re-reviewed PASS); `docs-keeper` PASS (reconciled ADR-0010, the CHANGELOG loopback-auth bullet and the first-run `runtimeOptions()` wording; `docs-check` 0 errors / 0 warnings). `governance-verifier` and `tech-scout` not required: no locked doc changed and no dependency added.
- Next step: a live loopback smoke with the pinned `b12426` binary (needs the ~1.7 GB weights + sidecar install and the owner's VC++ redistributable decision).
- Status: done.
- Delivery: PR #92 (`https://github.com/ynotfins/vibecodium/pull/92`), commits `664ae1c` and `5763af4` (the `ecformat` bullet-continuation fix) plus the `origin/vibe/main` merge `756bdc1` (which kept both the loopback-auth/owned-runtime and local-provider text in `STATUS`, `CHANGELOG`, `ROADMAP` and `README`). CI on `756bdc1` passed both `verify` runs, both `compile` jobs, `ecformat`, both `zizmor` runs and the Devin review; `build (macos-14, arm64)` was cancelled in 0 s on its unsupported runner label (the same infrastructure limitation PR #88 and PR #91 recorded), and the long prebuilt-binary `build` legs for the remaining architectures were still running when the PR merged, so no artifact claim is made. ### 2026-11-27 - M3 local runtime loopback authentication

- Goal / must-haves: close the residual risk ADR-0010 records for the M3 local runtime - "readiness accepts a 200 from any listener on the chosen port" and "upstream `llama-server` defaults to no API key and permissive CORS, and this slice adds neither, so the loopback port is reachable by local processes and web pages" - so the supervisor starts `llama-server` with an API key it owns for the controller's lifetime, the web UI off and CORS same-origin only, accepts readiness only after an authenticated probe that a listener rejecting the key cannot answer (a listener that answers 200 on both endpoints is a recorded residual race), and exposes the key to the host process (never to a panel or snapshot) for the B1 client. Because the runtime now rejects unauthenticated requests, the `llama-cpp` preset's `auth` mode moves from `none` to `bearer` (no key is required, so an unkeyed plain server still works) and a client given the key sends it; the host wiring that hands `runtime.auth().apiKey` to that client is recorded as the next M3 slice.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs`, `src/stable/gateway/provider-presets.mjs`, `tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0010-local-runtime-llama-server-supervisor.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs; `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs`, `first-run.mjs`, `model-downloader.mjs`, `hardware-probe.mjs`, `ollama.mjs`, `third-party-notices.mjs` and their tests; the extension panel; `provider-client.mjs`, `provider-setup.mjs` and the other gateway modules; `vibe/sidecars.json` and `vibe/models/catalog.json`; build scripts, CI, packaging and release infrastructure; new dependencies; a live network or process smoke.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `node --test tests/vibe/first-run-restart.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: a started runtime carries a controller-owned key of 43 URL-safe characters (or a validated caller key) that is returned only by `auth()` and appears in no snapshot, argument list, error or child-output tail; the child receives it as `LLAMA_API_KEY` in its allowlisted environment and the fixed flags include `--no-webui` and `--cors-origins localhost`; a caller-supplied key is validated (length, printable ASCII, no comma, quote, backslash or whitespace, no control or bidi character) and rejected as `invalid-api-key` otherwise (an accessor-backed option is rejected as `invalid-options`), while `commandArgs` cannot set `-m`, `--api-key`, `--api-key-file`, `--model`, the UI flags, the CORS flags or `--allow-unauthenticated-tools` and an `env` override cannot set `LLAMA_API_KEY`; `start()` accepts readiness only when `/health` returns 200 and the authenticated `/props` probe returns 200 with the key, fails `readiness-mismatch` when a listener answers `/health` but rejects the key, and stops the child on that failure; `health()` reports the authenticated result; the B1 `llama-cpp` client passes chat, streaming, tool calls and embeddings with the key and a request without it gets 401 from the fixture; the existing restart, timeout, early-exit, stop and environment tests still pass.
- Out of scope: spawning, installing, updating or signing the binary; the sidecar installer and its manifest; the first-run wizard and panel (their `runtimeOptions()` contract is unchanged); provider presets and client wiring; a live install/start smoke; packaging; any dependency.
- Original prompt (verbatim):

```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

1. Read AGENTS.md, vibe/goal/GOAL.md, docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md, docs/vibe/STATUS.md and docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md.
   If this file ends with a `## LANE SCOPE` section, that section is binding: work only on the milestones it names, do not start work outside it, and record any cross-scope work you notice under "Parked for owner" in STATUS.md instead of doing it.
2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Morph tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.

## LANE SCOPE

This lane owns milestone M3 only: the local runtime sidecar (llama-server), the model downloader, and the first-run wizard.

Do not start M2 items (lane A owns M2 completion) and do not start M4/M5/M6 work. If you find work outside M3 that matters, record it under "Parked for owner" in docs/vibe/STATUS.md and continue with M3.
```

- Branch: `vibe/m3/loopback-auth` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs` now authenticates the local runtime end to end. `RUNTIME_MODULE_VERSION` is 2; `MIN_API_KEY_CHARS` (32) and `MAX_API_KEY_CHARS` (128) bound a key whose validator (`invalid-api-key`, message without the value) accepts only printable ASCII with no comma, quote, backslash, whitespace, control, format or bidi character (llama.cpp parses the value as a CSV key list, so a comma or quote could split one caller key into several); an absent key is generated from `randomBytes(32)` as 43 base64url characters and reused for the controller's lifetime; `apiKey` is an option key read through own data descriptors (an accessor fails as `invalid-options` without being invoked). `llama_api_key` joins `DENIED_ENV_KEYS`, and `childEnvironment(overrides, apiKey)` writes the key last and deletes case variants on Windows, so no caller override survives. `commandArgs` can no longer set `-m`, `--api-key`, `--api-key-file`, `--model`, `--webui`, `--ui`, `--no-ui`, `--no-webui`, the CORS flags or `--allow-unauthenticated-tools`, in the separate-value or `=` spelling (`RESERVED_SERVER_ARG_PREFIXES` covers `--host=`, `--port=`, `--api-key`, `--model`, `--cors-origins`, `--cors-methods`, `--cors-headers` and the `=` form of each boolean UI/CORS flag). `buildLlamaServerArgs` appends `--no-webui --cors-origins localhost`. The old health probe became a shared `request(port, pathname, headers)` with `probe()` (`/health`) and `probeAuthenticated()` (`/props` with `Authorization: Bearer <key>`); a start now requires both, treats a 401/403 from `/props` as a foreign listener, reaps the child, clears the port and fails as `readiness-mismatch`, and `health()` uses the same authenticated probe. The controller exposes a frozen `auth()` returning `{ apiKey }`; `status()`, `describe()`, `config` and every error and retained output tail stay key-free. Because the runtime now rejects unauthenticated requests, the card was amended to allow `src/stable/gateway/provider-presets.mjs` and its test, and the B1 `llama-cpp` preset moved from `auth: 'none'` to `auth: 'bearer'` (still `apiKeyRequired: false` and `local: true`, with a comment), so a client given the key sends it while a plain unkeyed `llama-server` still works. `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs` enforces the llama.cpp rule (public `/health`, `Bearer` or `X-Api-Key` elsewhere, 401 with the documented body), logs only `HAS_API_KEY: present|absent`, and serves `GET /props`. `tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs` gained three cases (a caller key is validated and used on every request and survives a stop; an unusable key fails closed without echoing it; a listener that answers `/health` but rejects the key fails `readiness-mismatch`, is reaped, and the probe is proved to send the bearer header), extended the option matrix with every newly reserved flag and environment spelling, and updated the health, restart and B1 client cases to pass `apiKey`. A security-reviewer follow-up then closed an underscore bypass: the reserved/denied comparison now normalises `_` to `-` on `--`-prefixed arguments the same way llama.cpp does (`common/arg.cpp`), so `--cors_origins`, `--api_key`, `--no_webui`, `--allow_unauthenticated_tools` and the other underscore spellings are refused exactly like their hyphenated forms, while a non-reserved flag such as `--chat_template` still passes in either spelling (positive cases added so the fix cannot over-refuse). The same review round added the remote-model sources `-mu`/`--model-url`, `-hf`/`--hf-repo`, `-dr`/`--docker-repo` and the peer tunnel `--connect` to the denied set, so caller prefix arguments cannot point the child at a remote GGUF or a peer, and the test matrix covers representative short, long, underscore and `=` spellings of each (the prefix and exact-name rules refuse every form). Docs: `design/m3-llama-server.md` (contract, options, flags, two-step readiness, environment, failure codes, security boundary, residual risks, verification), `adr/0010-local-runtime-llama-server-supervisor.md` (decision and consequences), `SECURITY.md`, `TECH_RADAR.md` (llama.cpp row and `b12426` auth-surface re-verification), `ROADMAP.md` (M3 row and next slice), `design/m2-provider-presets.md` (the `llama-cpp` auth row), the `docs/vibe/README.md` index, `CHANGELOG.md` and this entry.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs` PASS (34 tests: 33 pass, 1 environment-conditional skip for `SIGTERM` on Windows, 0 fail, ~16 s); `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-setup.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-restart.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs` PASS (146 tests: 145 pass, 1 environment-conditional skip, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (5/5 steps; 856 tests, 0 fail, 10 or 11 environment-conditional skips depending on the host); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs check` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings). One earlier run of the seven-file set failed the environment-dependent real Credential Manager round-trip in `provider-setup.test.mjs` (a fixed target returned `null` under heavy parallel PowerShell load); the same file passes 12/12 alone and the seven other files passed in the same invocation and inside `verify`, so it is recorded as a pre-existing M2 flake below rather than a slice regression. After the `origin/vibe/main` merge (PRs #87 and #89) the suite grew to 864 tests: the first whole-suite run was 853 pass, 1 fail and 10 skips, the only failure being the untouched `tests/vibe/automation.test.mjs` lane-scope case under parallel load, and an immediate re-run was 854 pass, 0 fail and 10 skips with `verify --strict` 5/5 and `docs-check --strict` 0/0.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS twice. The first pass raised one finding (the `=` spelling of a boolean UI/CORS flag was accepted), fixed in `0cc1904`; the final pass on `9c5b170` ran the eight-file suite (146: 145 pass, 1 skip), `verify --strict` (856, 0 fail), `docs-check --strict` (0/0), 13/13 locks, an independent 17/17 spot-check (underscore/remote flags refused, `--chat_template`/`--keep` accepted, 43-char differing keys, no key in `status`/`describe`/`config`/error, `runtimeOptions` keys unchanged), and a mutation test proving the underscore assertions load-bearing with the worktree clean. drift-sentinel PASS (its findings - the `=` spellings, historical wording and low notes - all fixed; the diff is exactly the card paths plus docs-keeper reconciliations, no locked path, dependency, pin, binary, CI or governance record). security-reviewer FAIL then PASS: the FAIL was a MEDIUM underscore bypass (`--cors_origins` etc. slipped the reserved-flag check because llama.cpp rewrites `_` to `-`); the fix normalises `_` to `-` on `--` args (`5983dd1`) and the final PASS independently verified every reserved/denied spelling in both the separate-value and `=` forms, confirmed upstream `common/arg.cpp@b12426` only normalises `--` args, extracted all 442 registered `--` names (none contain `_`, so no legitimate flag is newly refused), and re-confirmed the broader boundary (key never in argv/snapshot/config/errors, authenticated `/props`, same-origin CORS, `--no-webui`, loopback bind); its optional defense-in-depth note led to the remote-model/peer-connect denials (`9c5b170`). docs-keeper PASS after reconciling seven points (the Tests count, the parked flake wording, `m2-provider-presets.md` auth row, the README index, the CHANGELOG bullet, the design verification wording and two review dates). governance-verifier not applicable (no locked path changed: 13/13 locks, `governance check` 0 errors, no approval record). tech-scout PASS: no dependency, lockfile, pin, binary or catalog change in `868ba30..HEAD`; it re-verified the live `b12426` pin (25,209,516 bytes, matching digest, 12 days public), the `b12426` auth surface, and the two model digests, refreshed the radar's latest-nightly clause to `b12818`, and left the pin unchanged (re-pin not due). release-manager not applicable (in-milestone; no version, artifact, feed, signing or publication change).
