### 2026-11-27 - M3 installer-owned runtime directory under the application data root

- Goal / must-haves: close the M3 gate recorded in `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md` - "an installer-owned `targetDir` under the application data root, never a webview-supplied path" - as code the host can call, without weakening the delivered installer: a new dependency-free module defines the application data root (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Vibecodium` with an `%APPDATA%` fallback on Windows, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/vibecodium` or `~/.local/share/vibecodium` on Linux, `~/Library/Application Support/Vibecodium` on macOS), derives exactly one runtime directory per pinned target (`<root>/runtime/<platformKey>/<id>-<version>`), and proves containment without following a symlink or junction; `installSidecar` and `verifySidecarInstall` accept an `ownedRoot` option that derives the directory themselves and refuse a caller `targetDir` alongside it; and the first-run wizard's host-owned `sidecar` bag accepts `appDataRoot`, refuses `targetDir` with it, and hands the installer the derived owned directory instead of a webview-supplied path.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/app-data.mjs` (new), `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs`, `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs`, `tests/vibe/app-data.test.mjs` (new), `tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0011-nightly-llama-cpp-intake.md`, plus the living-doc reconciliations the chain's owners run: `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md` and `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (the runtime row records the `-b`/config-root facts and the POSIX residual).
- Files forbidden: locked docs; the `llama-server` supervisor and its tests, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware probe, the `ollama` module, the third-party notices module and its generator, the first-run panel `.cjs` and its tests, the gateway and provider modules, the provider setup panel and service, extension host wiring, build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure, new dependencies, live network or process access in tests, M0/M1 owner-parked work and all M2/M4/M5/M6 work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/app-data.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `resolveAppDataRoot({ env, platform, home? })` returns a frozen `{ platform, root, source }`: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Vibecodium` as `local-appdata`, the `%APPDATA%` fallback as `roaming-appdata`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/vibecodium` as `xdg-data-home`, `~/.local/share/vibecodium` (from `HOME` or the `home` option) as `home-xdg`, and `~/Library/Application Support/Vibecodium` on macOS as `home-library`; a missing root variable fails `missing-app-data-root`; a relative, UNC, drive-relative, traversal, control/bidi, drive-root or filesystem-root value fails `invalid-app-data-root`; an unsupported platform fails `unsupported-platform`; an unknown or reserved option key, an accessor (never invoked) and a revoked Proxy fail `invalid-options`; the injected environment is read through own data descriptors only. `ownedRuntimeDirectory(root, { id, version, platformKey })` returns the frozen `<root>/runtime/<platformKey>/<id>-<version>` for a validated absolute local root and a lowercase slug id, a bounded version and a `<platform>-<arch>` key; an id or version that could leave the directory, an unknown key, an accessor and a relative or drive-root root fail `invalid-options`/`invalid-app-data-root`. `assertOwnedRuntimeDirectory(root, dir)` returns the resolved directory when it is strictly inside the root, compares case-insensitively on Windows, and fails `outside-app-data` for a sibling, the root itself, a `..` escape and an existing ancestor that is a symlink or junction pointing outside the root, without creating anything. `installSidecar`/`verifySidecarInstall` with `ownedRoot` and no `targetDir` derive and use that directory (missing before, verified after), a `verified` result whose executable sits inside it; `ownedRoot` together with `targetDir` fails `invalid-options`; a relative, drive-root, control-bearing or junction-escaped root fails `invalid-destination`; the injected `fetchImpl` still sees only the pinned URL. `createFirstRunWizard` accepts `sidecar: { manifest, appDataRoot }` and derives the owned target directory, refuses `targetDir` alongside it as `invalid-sidecar`, passes `ownedRoot` (never `targetDir`) to the injected `installSidecar` and `verifySidecar`, installs once and returns the pinned plan, and keeps the existing `targetDir`/`<destinationDir>/runtime` behaviour when no root is supplied. `docs-check --strict` is 0/0 and `verify --strict` passes.
- Out of scope: making `ownedRoot` mandatory (the delivered `targetDir` path stays for hosts that pass an installed runtime directory), the model `destinationDir` ownership, a cross-process lock, a descriptor-bound part-file read, an authenticated manifest, signing, packaging, the Microsoft VC++ runtime, placing the notice in the packaged application, the first-run panel or extension-host wiring, any pin, digest, model or catalog change, any dependency.
- Parked cross-scope note: the extension host that will pass the real application data root is M6/M12 wiring and stays there; the signing route, the Microsoft redistributable decision and the packaged catalog copy stay owner-parked or M12 as recorded. No other cross-lane work was noticed.
- Branch: `vibe/m3/owned-runtime-dir` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/app-data.mjs` adds the installer-owned runtime directory capability as a dependency-free module: `resolveAppDataRoot()` returns a frozen `{ platform, root, source }` from own data descriptors (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Vibecodium` as `local-appdata`, `%APPDATA%` as `roaming-appdata`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/vibecodium`, `~/.local/share/vibecodium`, `~/Library/Application Support/Vibecodium`), failing `missing-app-data-root`/`invalid-app-data-root`/`unsupported-platform` for a missing, relative, UNC, drive-relative, traversal, control/bidi, drive-root or filesystem-root value; `ownedRuntimeDirectory()` derives the frozen `<root>/runtime/<platformKey>/<id>-<version>`; `assertOwnedRuntimeDirectory()` canonicalizes only the existing prefix and proves strict containment (case-insensitive on Windows) without creating anything, failing `outside-app-data` for a sibling, the root itself, a `..` escape or a junction/symlink escape. `installSidecar`/`verifySidecarInstall` accept `ownedRoot` (exclusive with `targetDir`) and derive the directory themselves, mapping an unownable root or escape to `invalid-destination` without echoing the path; `createFirstRunWizard` accepts `sidecar: { manifest, appDataRoot }`, refuses `targetDir` alongside it, and hands both effects `{ ownedRoot, platformKey }` (the installer also gets its `signal`/`onProgress`), so no caller-supplied install path reaches a write, while the legacy `<destinationDir>/runtime` default is unchanged for a host that owns a runtime. Host wiring of the real root and the Windows location reconciliation with locked `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 4 remain M6/M12; the authenticated-manifest gate, signing, the packaged catalog copy and the live smoke stay open.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/app-data.test.mjs tests/vibe/sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs` PASS (76/76; the slice adds 11 app-data tests, 4 sidecar owned-root tests and 2 wizard `appDataRoot` tests plus the refused-bag cases, and the suite also passes on WSL Ubuntu); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (5/5 steps, 864 pass, 11 skipped); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locked paths ok and `check` PASS.
- Guardians: qa-verifier FAIL then PASS (it found that POSIX `/` was accepted as an owned root; both owned functions now validate the root before canonicalizing, regression-tested on Windows and Linux, and `first-run-sidecar.test.mjs` gained the required-`platformKey` and refused-root cases); drift-sentinel FAIL twice then PASS (doc overclaims that the owned root was in effect by default in the user guide, README design row and CHANGELOG, a dropped SECURITY gate, and the missing accepted-root cases were fixed; the scope now matches the card, including ADR-0011 added to the allowed list); security-reviewer PASS (containment, canonicalize boundary, 8.3/device/UNC/ADS probes and non-echoing errors independently reproduced; low notes only: `toLowerCase` folds wider than the NTFS upcase table - documented as a residual risk and unreachable from the derived path - a regular-file ancestor failing closed at `mkdir`, the user-owned root junction, verify creating empty directories under the owned root, and case-sensitive env keys for a synthetic env object); docs-keeper PASS (ADR-0011 review date, SECURITY "accepts in place of" wording; no locked-doc proposal filed); governance-verifier PASS (no locked path changed, no approval record written, hard stops intact, the ARCHITECTURE section 4 divergence parked for M12); tech-scout PASS (no dependency, pin, digest or notice change, built-ins only, `realpathSync.native`/`lstat`/`path.relative` current on Node 22+; recommends keeping env-based resolution and not using Electron's `appData`/`userData` for the runtime at M6 wiring); release-manager PASS (no artifact, signing, feed or version change, `[Unreleased]` bullet accurate; uninstall inventory, old-pin pruning and repair behaviour tracked for M3/M12 exit).
- Next step: wire the host-supplied root into the product path (M6/M12) and reconcile the Windows location with `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 4 through a governance proposal then; keep the authenticated-manifest gate, signing, the packaged catalog copy and the clean-machine live smoke open for M3/M12.

### 2026-11-26 - M3 third-party notices ledger and generator

- Goal / must-haves: make the third-party licensing of the pinned runtime honest and checkable. Ship the verbatim license texts the pins need, a ledger that ties every license to its text by SHA-256, a generator that produces one notice document from the shipped pins, and a repository check that fails when a pinned component's license has no notice, when a text drifts, or when the checked-in document is stale. The follow-up explicitly recorded after #77 and #79 is "add a third-party notices file for llama.cpp's MIT text and the LLVM/OpenMP notice".
- Must-have IDs: F6 (license review for commercial shipping); the M3 remaining item "third-party notices" recorded in the next-steps and remaining-work text of `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md` and `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/third-party-notices.mjs` (new), `vibe/tools/notices.mjs` (new), `vibe/notices.json` (new), `vibe/licenses/mit.txt`, `vibe/licenses/apache-2.0.txt`, `vibe/licenses/apache-2.0-with-llvm-exception.txt` (new, verbatim upstream texts), `docs/vibe/legal/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (new, generated), `tests/vibe/third-party-notices.test.mjs` (new), `vibe/tools/verify.mjs` (one added step), `docs/vibe/design/m3-third-party-notices.md` (new), `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-model-downloader.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, the sidecar installer and its tests, the supervisor, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware probe, the first-run wizard and panel, the Ollama module, the gateway modules and provider presets, B5 provider setup, build scripts, CI workflows, packaging and release infrastructure, new dependencies, any change to a pin or a digest, live network access in tests, M0/M1 owner-parked work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/third-party-notices.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/notices.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `vibe/notices.json` validates through own data descriptors and fails closed as `invalid-ledger` for a trap-throwing Proxy, a revoked Proxy, an accessor (never invoked), an unknown or reserved key, a wrong version, an invalid or future review date, an empty or oversized license list, a duplicate id, an identifier that is not lowercase, a name with a control or bidi character, a non-HTTPS URL, a URL with credentials, a text path that is absolute, drive-lettered, backslashed, traversing, outside `vibe/licenses/` or nested, and a digest that is not 64 lowercase hex characters. `collectThirdPartyComponents` projects the shipped pins (a `binary` per sidecar with its version, source and license ids, a `model` per catalog entry with its abbreviated revision, repository and license), sorts them by name, refuses a duplicate name, an empty projection or a malformed version/source/license id, and returns frozen components. `buildThirdPartyNotices` fails as `missing-license-text` when a component's license has no ledger entry, `missing-license-body`/`invalid-license-body` for a missing, empty, non-string or oversized text, `license-digest-mismatch` when a text no longer matches the ledger, and `invalid-components`/`document-too-large` on bad input; otherwise it returns a deterministic document whose header is `Doc class: generated` owned by `docs-keeper`, whose table escapes a `|` in a cell, and which embeds every used text verbatim. A CRLF checkout still matches the pinned digest. `checkNotices` passes on the real root, reports a missing or stale document and regenerates idempotently with `--write`, and the LLVM/OpenMP text digest equals the pinned `LICENSE-LLVM-OpenMP` digest in `vibe/sidecars.json` (19,741 bytes, `fdad1758...`).
- Out of scope: placing the notice in the installer or beside the installed files (M12 packaging), signing anything, the Microsoft VC++ runtime, a change to any pin, digest, model, installer or supervisor, a hosted or per-model license fetch at build time, and any dependency. Node built-ins only.

### 2026-11-26 - M3 nightly llama.cpp intake ADR

- Goal / must-haves: record, as a decision, the rule the product uses to accept a llama.cpp nightly as the bundled local runtime - tag shape, the seven-day wait, CPU-first platform matrix, digest and size verification, the installed allowlist and its notice obligation, the Microsoft VC++ runtime dependency, the no-auto-update rule, and the gates that still block a bundled release (signing, notices, installer-owned directory) - so the pin is a reviewed policy rather than an implicit build choice.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local runtime). The "ADR before bundling the nightly" item left open by the delivered sidecar pin and install.
- Files allowed: `docs/vibe/adr/0011-nightly-llama-cpp-intake.md` (new), `docs/vibe/adr/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, code, tests, the manifest and installer, design docs, CHANGELOG (no user-visible change), user guide, build scripts, CI, release infrastructure, new dependencies, any live network access.
- Acceptance commands: `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: the new ADR is classed `living`, carries a status and date, and is linked from `docs/vibe/adr/README.md`; every relative link it uses resolves; it states the tag shapes it accepts and the floating references it refuses, the seven-day rule as a review-time process control whose evidence lives in the radar row (and that the loader only format-checks the date), the CPU-first matrix, the digest and exact-size requirement, the allowlist and the llama.cpp MIT plus LLVM OpenMP notice obligation, the Microsoft VC++ 2015-2022 x64 dependency as a separate pin, the no-auto-update rule, the alternatives it rejected, and that signing, the notices file, the Microsoft runtime and the installer-owned directory remain open with signing parked for the owner; it does not approve a binary version (the manifest and `TECH_RADAR.md` do); `docs-check --strict` is 0/0 and `verify --strict` passes.
- Out of scope: changing the pin, the manifest, the installer, the wizard or any code; bundling, signing, publishing or packaging; the notices file itself; the Microsoft runtime pin; a GPU backend pin; the 2026-12-03 re-pin review.
- Parked cross-scope note: signing remains owner-parked; no other cross-lane work was noticed.
- Delivered: `docs/vibe/adr/0011-nightly-llama-cpp-intake.md` records the intake policy in seven decisions (pin a nightly and never a floating reference; choose a nightly at least seven days public at review, preferring the build a recent stable release names, and record the seven-day evidence in the radar row because the loader does not enforce it; ship CPU builds first and add a GPU backend only with its own pin and review; install only the allowlisted runtime files and ship the llama.cpp MIT text plus the archive LLVM OpenMP notice; track the Microsoft VC++ 2015-2022 x64 runtime as a separate M12 pin; re-pin only at a review and never in-product; require signing, notices and an installer-owned directory before a bundled release), the four alternatives it rejected (waiting for stable binaries, building from source, relying on an installed Ollama daemon, pinning a current nightly), and the residual risks (an upstream prerelease build with no upstream code signature, the extracted directory must be product-owned). The ADR index links it and the ROADMAP M3 row records it instead of listing it as open.
- Tests: `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps); ecformat (pinned CI 0.2.0) 0 errors; `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `git diff --check` clean; the new ADR is ASCII, LF, no tabs, final newline.
- Guardians: qa-verifier FAIL twice then PASS: it caught that the first draft claimed the seven-day rule was enforced in the manifest data (it is a review-time control; the loader only format-checks `publishedAt`), that the manifest has no tree or review-date field, that the loader does not refuse floating tags, and that `b12426` is not the v1.0.0 build (`b12359` is, and `b12774` is the v1.1.0 pointer); the ADR, the card and the milestone summary were rewritten to match the code, the duplicated consequence sentence and the pre-recorded verdict were removed, and the final runs are docs 0/0, verify 4/4 (821 tests: 811 pass, 10 environment-conditional skips) and 10/10 agents-and-docs. drift-sentinel PASS: exactly the four card paths, no locked path, code, dependency or governance-record change, governance 13/13, every ADR claim verified against the module and the manifest, and the ROADMAP no longer lists the ADR as open. docs-keeper PASS: header, status, index row and links verified, the ADR index review date and a STATUS typo and blank line corrected, no locked doc touched. governance-verifier, tech-scout and release-manager not applicable (no locked path, no dependency or component change, in-milestone); security-reviewer was recorded as not applicable when the slice merged, but a later review of the merged ADR found one blocking gate-wording issue, closed by the 2026-11-26 gate-reconciliation entry above.

### 2026-11-26 - M3 sidecar install in the first-run start action

- Goal / must-haves: let the first-run setup provision its own pinned runtime instead of waiting for the host to pass an installed `runtimePath`. The wizard accepts the shipped sidecar manifest (validated once at construction), detects an already installed runtime without the network on every refresh, and installs the pinned `llama-server` through the delivered `installSidecar` only when the start action is used, then returns the pinned start plan with the validated executable path. The wizard still never spawns, signs, updates or executes the binary, never opens a socket itself (the default installer is the only network effect and the tests inject it), and never reads a secret. This slice also repairs the two living docs the #78 merge left with unmerged conflict markers (`ROADMAP.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`) and adds an unmerged-conflict-marker rule to `docs-check` so a conflicted doc cannot pass again.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local llama.cpp preset / local runtime); the M3 remaining item "wire `installSidecar` into the first-run start action" recorded in the next-steps sections of `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md` and `m3-sidecar-binary.md`.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/first-run.cjs`, `tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs` (new), `tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/agents-and-docs.test.mjs`, `vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-sidecar-binary.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files allowed addition (2026-11-26): cross-reference-only corrections in `docs/vibe/design/m3-hardware-probe.md` and `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, both of which still described the pinned-runtime install as future work. No decision, contract or scope change; the hardware probe module and its tests stay forbidden.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs` and its tests (consumed as-is; every helper it needs is already exported), the supervisor and its tests, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware probe, the Ollama module, the gateway modules and provider presets, the B5 provider setup panel or its service (M2/lane A), the extension host wiring, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, new dependencies or vendored binaries, live network or process access in tests, copying the installed files or the catalog into a packaged application, manifest signing, third-party notices, the Microsoft VC++ runtime, M0/M1 owner-parked work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/first-run-sidecar.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs`, `node --test tests/vibe/agents-and-docs.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `createFirstRunWizard` accepts an optional `sidecar` option bag (`manifest`, optional `id`, optional `targetDir`, optional `platformKey`) read through own data descriptors and injected `installSidecar`/`verifySidecar` effects. A Proxy, an accessor, an unknown or reserved key, a malformed manifest, an unknown id, an unsupported platform key, a relative or unsafe target directory, or a non-function effect fails closed as `invalid-options`/`invalid-sidecar` without invoking a getter. With a sidecar configured, `refresh()` runs the offline verifier and adopts a verified executable path without a download; a throwing or unverified check leaves the runtime missing and never fails the refresh. `start()` is single-flight, returns `not-ready` while models are missing, `insufficient` on a low-memory machine, `busy` while a model download runs and `disposed` after disposal without calling the installer; with models ready it calls the installer exactly once with the configured target directory and platform key, forwards bounded progress into `view().runtime.progress`, validates the returned executable path with the supervisor's `validateLlamaExecutablePath`, flips `view().runtime.installed`, and returns the same frozen pinned start plan `startPlan()` now returns without a second install. A failed, aborted, non-verified, accessor-backed or hostile installer result yields a bounded `runtime-install-failed` (with a safe `code`) and no executable path, and a later retry can succeed. A daemon-selected wizard returns the Ollama plan and never installs. The panel enables **Start local runtime** when a sidecar is configured and the phase is ready although the plan still reports `runtime-not-bundled`, says the runtime will be downloaded, calls `start()` when the wizard exposes it (falling back to `startPlan()`), reports the install failure with a bounded message and keeps every dynamic value escaped. `docs-check` reports an unmerged conflict marker in any `docs/vibe` Markdown file as an error, and the shipped docs carry none.
- Out of scope: spawning, supervising, stopping, signing, updating or executing `llama-server`; bundling the installed files or the catalog into a packaged application; third-party notices; the Microsoft VC++ runtime; an authenticated manifest or an update path; a GPU backend pin; altering the installer, the supervisor or the catalog; provider switching or config writes; a live install/start smoke; an ADR for the nightly intake; any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Parked cross-scope note: none. No `## LANE SCOPE` section was present this session; the clone's established M3 lane was continued. Copying the installed files and the catalog into the packaged application, signing the manifest, third-party notices, the Microsoft VC++ runtime, the live install/start smoke, the nightly ADR and offering the daemon in the B5 provider setup panel stay open or parked as recorded.

### 2026-11-26 - M3 llama.cpp sidecar pin and verified installer (delivery)

- Original prompt (verbatim, no `## LANE SCOPE` section this session):

```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

1. Read AGENTS.md, vibe/goal/GOAL.md, docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md, docs/vibe/STATUS.md and docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md.
   If this file ends with a `## LANE SCOPE` section, that section is binding: work only on the milestones it names, do not start work outside it, and record any cross-scope work you notice under "Parked for owner" in STATUS.md instead of doing it.
2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Cursor tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.
```

- Branch: `vibe/m3/sidecar-pin` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered (build commit `77f3da0`): `vibe/sidecars.json` pins one `llama-server` sidecar (llama.cpp nightly `b12426`, MIT plus the Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception OpenMP runtime, published 2026-11-15, `ggml-org/llama.cpp`), its `win32-x64` CPU archive `llama-b12426-bin-win-cpu-x64.zip` (25,209,516 bytes, SHA-256 `61145645c26cf8d7b3ed76435812192ae42f51c4c51e895936adc61dc1eb0410`) and 23 allowlisted runtime files. `src/stable/runtime/sidecar.mjs` validates the pin through own data descriptors, refuses an accessor, a trap-failing or revoked Proxy, unsafe or reserved paths, a duplicate id or path, a non-permissive license and an unsafe URL, then downloads under an idle and total deadline, refuses an insecure redirect, enforces declared and streamed size, parses a pointer-free ZIP with no ZIP64, multi-disk or encrypted entry, verifies every extracted file against its own size and digest before writing anything, installs each verified file atomically (per file, not transactionally: a failed rename leaves the earlier verified files), verifies the result offline and, after a fresh install, returns an executable path the supervisor's `validateLlamaExecutablePath` accepts. It never spawns, signs, updates or executes the binary, and a target that already verifies is skipped without a request (the skip path trusts the offline inspection and does not run the supervisor validator). The delivery run adds two hardening fixes raised by security-reviewer: the final-URL guard now also refuses the literal IPv6 forms that embed an internal IPv4 address (IPv4-compatible `::a.b.c.d`, IPv4-mapped and SIIT `::ffff:*`, NAT64 `64:ff9b::/96` and `64:ff9b:1::/48`, 6to4 `2002::/16`) and the `fe80::/10`, `fc00::/7` and `ff00::/8` ranges through an 8-group expansion instead of prefix string tests, and the pin now records `licenses` (`mit` plus `apache-2.0-with-llvm-exception` for the bundled `libomp.dll`) as a validated 1-4 entry unique list instead of a single `license` field. Two portability defects that only a Linux run exposes were fixed in the same pass: the two download-deadline timers no longer call `unref()`, because a pending transfer whose only pending handle is that timer let the process exit and cancelled the awaiting test, and every test call now passes the explicit `platformKey: 'win32-x64'` instead of relying on the host platform, so the suite is host-independent.

#### Original prompt (verbatim)

```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

1. Read AGENTS.md, vibe/goal/GOAL.md, docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md, docs/vibe/STATUS.md and docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md.
   If this file ends with a `## LANE SCOPE` section, that section is binding: work only on the milestones it names, do not start work outside it, and record any cross-scope work you notice under "Parked for owner" in STATUS.md instead of doing it.
2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Morph tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.

## LANE SCOPE

This lane owns milestone M3 only: the local runtime sidecar (llama-server), the model downloader, and the first-run wizard.

Do not start M2 items (lane A owns M2 completion) and do not start M4/M5/M6 work. If you find work outside M3 that matters, record it under "Parked for owner" in docs/vibe/STATUS.md and continue with M3.
```

- Goal / must-haves: wire the delivered Ollama discovery into the first-run setup flow so the wizard can tell an already-installed local daemon apart from the pinned llama.cpp path, report its local chat and embedding models and hand the caller the exact B1 `ollama` preset overrides (`{ baseUrl, model }`), without a download, without a secret, without changing the pinned plan and without adopting the daemon on its own.

- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/first-run.cjs`, `tests/vibe/first-run-ollama.test.mjs` (new), `tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/extension-contract.test.cjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-ollama.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.

- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/first-run-ollama.test.mjs tests/vibe/first-run-panel.test.mjs`, `node --test src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/extension-contract.test.cjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `createFirstRunWizard` accepts an optional `discoverOllama` effect (default the delivered `discoverOllama`; an accessor, Proxy option bag, unknown key or non-function fails closed without invoking a getter), exposes `checkOllama()` and keeps `view().ollama === null` until it is called; `checkOllama()` is single-flight (at most one effect call in flight, and callable again after it settles), never changes the phase, error, models, progress or start plan, never downloads or probes, and returns a frozen summary `{ checked, available, reason, baseUrl, chatModel, embeddingModel, modelCount, notices, providerOptions }` that is also stored as `view().ollama`; a documented available result (`available: true`, a local chat model, an embedding model and `ollama-remote-models`) yields `providerOptions` exactly `{ baseUrl: 'http://127.0.0.1:<port>/v1', model: <local chat model> }` through `toOllamaProviderOptions`, `reason: null`, the delivered notices and the model count, while an available daemon with no local chat model or a forged, remote, embedding-only, wrong-version or wrong-base-URL result yields `providerOptions: null` and a bounded `no-chat-model` or `invalid-result` reason; an unavailable result keeps its bounded `unreachable`/`timeout`/`http-error`/`invalid-response`/`policy-denied` reason and its notice; a thrown effect, a non-object answer, an accessor-backed answer, a revoked or throwing Proxy, an unknown reason string, a non-array model list and an oversized or accessor-backed notice list fail closed as `check-failed`/`invalid-result` with bounded notices and never throw, echo a URL, model text, body or error message, or leak the injected error; a disposed wizard returns `{ checked: false, reason: 'disposed', ... }` and does not store; the panel renders the not-checked, available (base URL, chat and embedding model, and the provider-setup note that activation is not wired yet) and unavailable states with every value escaped, offers a `Check for Ollama` button, forwards `{ type: 'ollama' }` only through the existing single-data-property message rule, calls the wizard once, reports the result in its status line and returns `kind: 'ollama'`, and `open()` performs one automatic check after the first refresh; the extension contract still activates and forwards the new message.
- Out of scope: starting, installing, stopping or updating Ollama; pulling models; switching the active B1 provider or writing provider config (the B5 provider setup panel is M2 lane work and is parked below); probing any port other than the delivered loopback default; changing the pinned catalog, downloader, hardware plan, supervisor or start plan; a live daemon in tests; bundling the llama.cpp binary; copying the catalog into a packaged application; restart/back-off; any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Parked cross-scope note: wiring the same discovery into the B5 provider setup panel (so a user can activate the `ollama` preset from that panel) is M2/lane A work and is recorded under "Parked for owner" instead of being done here.
- Original prompt: the same goal-runner prompt recorded verbatim under the 2026-11-26 M3 Ollama entry below; its `## LANE SCOPE` section binds this lane to M3 only.
- Branch: `vibe/m3/ollama-wizard` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs` takes an optional `discoverOllama` effect (default the delivered probe) and adds a read-only, single-flight `checkOllama()`. The answer is read through own data descriptors with Proxies rejected up front; notices are capped at 8 with a bounded iteration and model count at 256; reasons are normalized to a fixed vocabulary; `no-chat-model` is reported when an available daemon has no local chat model; a thrown effect, malformed answer, accessor, revoked Proxy or oversized field list degrades to `check-failed`/`invalid-result` with a bounded notice and no URL, model text, body or error echo. Provider overrides are published only after the reviewed `toOllamaProviderOptions` re-validates the whole answer, so a forged, stale, remote, embedding-only or wrong-base result yields none; a disposed call returns `{ checked: false, reason: 'disposed', ... }` and stores nothing. `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/first-run.cjs` adds an escaped Ollama section, a **Check for Ollama** button, one automatic check after the first refresh, bounded descriptor reads for the summary and for the public error code, and forwards `{ type: 'ollama' }` through the existing exact-one-data-`type` rule; the controller now requires `checkOllama` on its wizard. ### 2026-11-26 - M3 llama-server restart and back-off

- Goal / must-haves: give the local runtime supervisor a bounded automatic restart/back-off policy, so a `llama-server` child that dies while serving is restarted on the same loopback port with exponential back-off, a small restart budget and a stability reset, and a permanently failing server ends in a bounded `failed` state instead of a crash loop or a silently dead endpoint. The policy is opt-in: a caller passes a validated `restart` object, and an omitted policy keeps the previous exit behaviour (`exited`, `restarts: 0`, no spawn); the status snapshot gains `restarts` and `failure`.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local llama.cpp preset / local runtime).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs`, `tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0010-local-runtime-llama-server-supervisor.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, plus cross-reference-only corrections in `docs/vibe/design/m3-first-run-wizard.md` and `docs/vibe/design/m3-hardware-probe.md` (both still listed restart/back-off as future work).
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware probe, the first-run wizard, the Ollama discovery, the gateway modules and provider presets, Bifrost or sidecar supervision, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, new dependencies or vendored binaries, live network access in tests, `vibe/sidecars.json`, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: the `restart` policy is validated through own data descriptors, so a revoked or trap-throwing Proxy, an accessor, an unknown key, a non-integer, an out-of-range value and a `maxDelayMs` below `baseDelayMs` fail closed as `invalid-restart-policy` without spawning a child; an omitted policy keeps the existing behaviour, where a child that exits while running leaves the controller `exited` with `restarts: 0` and no restart. With a policy, a child that exits while running puts the controller in `restarting`, then a fresh child is started after the back-off delay on the same port, `/health` is awaited again, the controller returns to `running` with a new pid and `restarts` incremented, and the B1 client answers through the new child. The delay grows exponentially from `baseDelayMs` and is capped by `maxDelayMs`; after `maxRestarts` restarts without a stable run the controller ends as `failed` with `failure: 'restarts-exhausted'`, `port: null`, no live child, and further exits schedule nothing. A run that stays healthy for `stableMs` resets the streak, so a later crash can restart again within the same budget, a `stableMs` of 0 never resets, and a non-zero window below 1,000 ms is refused so the policy cannot become a tight respawn loop. `stop()` during the back-off window cancels the pending restart, leaves the controller `stopped`, and spawns nothing; a crash while `starting` through an explicit `start()` still rejects with `server-exited` and never auto-restarts; the snapshot stays frozen and `health()` reports not-ready while `restarting` or `failed`.
- Out of scope: process-tree or job-object supervision, parent-death signals, restarting after an explicit `stop()`, changing the readiness or health contract, a UI switch, wiring the policy into the first-run wizard, a persistent restart log, and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Parked cross-scope note: none expected.

- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs` gains an opt-in, validated `restart` policy and a real restart state machine. `validateRestart` reads the policy through own data descriptors (`maxRestarts` 1-10, `baseDelayMs` 50-300,000 ms default 1,000, `maxDelayMs` default the larger of `baseDelayMs` and 30,000 with `maxDelayMs >= baseDelayMs` and a 300,000 cap, `stableMs` 0 or 1,000-600,000 ms default 60,000), refuses reserved and unknown keys, and fails closed as `invalid-restart-policy` before any spawn. An unexpected exit observed while `running` sets `restarting`, keeps the last port as the target and schedules `start()` after `baseDelayMs * 2 ** attempts` capped at `maxDelayMs`; the restart re-validates both paths, re-runs the `/health` gate, reuses the same port and reports a new pid, and each attempted restart spends one of `maxRestarts`. A failed automatic attempt also spends the budget and schedules the next one. Once the budget is spent the controller ends as `failed` with `failure: 'restarts-exhausted'`, `port: null` and no child, and schedules nothing further. A run healthy for `stableMs` clears the streak (a non-zero window below 1,000 ms is refused so the policy cannot become a tight loop, and `stableMs: 0` never resets). `stop()` cancels the back-off timer and clears the streak; a crash during an explicit `start()` still rejects `server-exited` and never auto-restarts; the per-child exit handler now settles once and each child owns its exit resolver; timers are `unref()`ed. The snapshot gains `restarts` and `failure` and stays frozen, and `health()` reports not-ready in `restarting` and `failed`. `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs` gains `FAKE_LLAMA_STARTS` boot counting, a configurable crash delay and the `crash-once`, `crash-twice`, `crash-after` and `crash-once-then-exit` modes.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs` PASS (28 tests: 27 pass, 1 environment-conditional skip); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 682 tests: 671 pass, 11 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `node vibe/tools/sync-agents.mjs --check` PASS; `git diff --check` clean; the 12 changed files are LF, final-newline, no tabs, with 3-space Markdown list continuations (README and SECURITY keep their pre-existing non-ASCII header separators; all added text is ASCII).
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS (focused 28/28 after it added three tests and four assertions: the `maxDelayMs` cap, a restart attempt that never becomes ready, the documented defaults, a `getOwnPropertyDescriptor`-throwing Proxy, and a frozen snapshot while restarting; it proved seven mutations load-bearing - no exponential growth, no cap, no budget guard, no stop cancellation, no stability reset, no zero guard, no attempt increment - and restored the module byte-for-byte). drift-sentinel PASS (exactly the 12 card paths, 13/13 locks, no dependency, patch, binary or governance-record drift) with five wording corrections applied: a revoked or trap-throwing Proxy in SECURITY and the card, the explicit-start versus failed-automatic-attempt nuance in the design and ROADMAP, the card's opt-in sentence now names the new snapshot fields, the design header date, and the fixture-mode list. security-reviewer PASS with three non-blocking items, two fixed here: a 1,000 ms floor on a non-zero `stableMs` (a 1 ms window could reset the streak on every boot) and a `.catch(() => failRestarts())` on the restart timer, while the benign-Proxy acceptance is documented by design; the third (a descriptor-trap Proxy test) is added. It confirmed no secret, environment, argument, license or loopback/no-tools surface changed and that a stop always wins. docs-keeper PASS after correcting the restart contract and defaults, the snapshot fields, the exhaustion trigger and validation list in the design doc, and the ADR's stale "downloading weights and detecting Ollama are later" sentence. governance-verifier PASS, not applicable (no locked path or governance record changed; 13/13 locks; `governance check` 0 errors). tech-scout PASS: no dependency, binary or catalog change, and live checks on 2026-11-26 confirm llama.cpp `v1.1.0` (published today, so the seven-day rule defers adoption to the row's 2026-12-03 review), nightly `b12774`, and that `GET /health` (503 `Loading model`, then 200 `{"status":"ok"}`) and all six supervisor flags are still documented in `v1.1.0`; it recommends keeping the ~100 in-tree lines over pm2 (AGPL-3.0), forever, nodemon or concurrently. A radar row correction is deferred to the next sweep (see Follow-up). release-manager PASS, not applicable (M3 stays in progress; no version, installer, feed, signing or publication change; only the CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` bullet is release-facing).
- Follow-up: the remaining M3 slices are pinning and bundling the `llama-server` binary, copying `vibe/models/catalog.json` into the packaged application, and wiring the Ollama discovery plus the restart policy into the first-run flow. `TECH_RADAR.md`'s `Local model runtime` row still names v1.0.0 as the latest stable; the next sweep (rows fall due 2026-11-27) should record v1.1.0 (2026-11-26, earliest adoption 2026-12-03 per the seven-day rule), nightly b12774, the re-verified `/health` and flag facts, and that this slice added an opt-in restart policy with Node built-ins only. A real extension-host smoke and a rebuilt-app screenshot remain M6/M12 work, and a process-tree/job-object supervisor and parent-death signal remain outside this slice. No cross-lane work was noticed.
- Delivery: PR #71 (`https://github.com/ynotfins/vibecodium/pull/71`), commit `e3cde60` (module, fixture, 28-test suite and living docs). A second docs commit records this entry before the merge.
- Branch: `vibe/m3/restart-backoff` -> PR #71 into `vibe/main`.
- Status: done.

7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.

## LANE SCOPE

This lane owns milestone M3 only: the local runtime sidecar (llama-server), the model downloader, and the first-run wizard.

Do not start M2 items (lane A owns M2 completion) and do not start M4/M5/M6 work. If you find work outside M3 that matters, record it under "Parked for owner" in docs/vibe/STATUS.md and continue with M3.
```

### 2026-11-26 - M2 B6 Morph Router difficulty adapter

- Goal / must-haves: deliver the last named B6 feature that can be built without a bundled binary or a live key, the direct provider's Router (prompt difficulty -> model tier), as a bounded, dependency-free adapter that asks the Morph Router to classify a prompt and returns one of three model tiers (with a conservative fallback tier when the Router is unavailable), so B3 can pick a cheap, standard or powerful model without guessing from prompt length.
- Must-have IDs: B6 (the Router feature of the direct-provider option); consumes the B1 `morph` preset's base URL/credential convention and the M1 endpoint policy. B6 stays open afterwards for the Morph-hosted model rows, the approval-gated abliterated model and live smoke tests; Bifrost supervision and the in-IDE history path also remain M2 work.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/morph-router.mjs`, `tests/vibe/morph-router.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-morph-router.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (the Morph agent-tools row, refreshed with the live Router facts read on 2026-11-26).
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, the B1 preset catalog and request client, B2 fallback, B3 router and budgets, B5 provider setup, the B6 direct policy, Fast Apply, WarpGrep, Compact, Morph-hosted model catalog entries and the approval-gated abliterated model, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, build scripts and CI workflows, release infrastructure, secret storage, new dependencies or vendored binaries, live provider calls in tests.

- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/morph-router.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `createMorphRouter(options)` validates every option through own data descriptors (a revoked or throwing Proxy, an accessor-backed option or policy, an unknown or reserved key, a sparse or accessor-backed `tierByDifficulty`, an invalid `defaultTier`, timeout, response cap, base URL with credentials, query, fragment or a non-loopback `http` scheme, and a missing API key fail closed; a live Proxy is read only through descriptors, so a get trap never runs); `describe()` returns only frozen non-secret configuration (version, classify path, tiers, default tier, timeout, caps) and never the API key, and the router object exposes no key or client. Against a local mock HTTP server, `classify('...')` sends exactly one `POST /v1/router/classify` carrying `{ input, classes: ['difficulty'] }`, no redirect, a bearer `Authorization` read once from the injected key or the configured environment variable, and a deadline that also covers the body read; a documented answer `{ classifications: { difficulty: { label: 'easy'|'medium'|'hard', confidence: 0..1, meets_threshold: true } } }` returns a frozen `{ tier, difficulty, confidence, meetsThreshold, needsInfo: false, fallback: false, code: null }` with the exact easy -> cheap, medium -> standard, hard -> powerful mapping, and the prompt and answer are never echoed outside that result. `meets_threshold: false`, a missing `meets_threshold`, and a documented `needs_info` signal return the configured default tier with `needsInfo: true` and `fallback: false` while keeping the reported difficulty where one exists; an unknown label, a non-object answer, a non-finite or out-of-range confidence, an accessor-backed answer and a malformed `classifications` fail closed as a bounded fallback result rather than throwing. A timeout, a network error, HTTP 408/429/500, a non-JSON body, an oversized declared or streamed body each return the default tier with `fallback: true` and a fixed `code` and never surface the URL, prompt, credential or body, while an oversized or empty prompt fails closed as `invalid-input` before any request; caller cancellation rejects as `aborted` and an unreachable external host is refused by the M1 endpoint policy before any fetch is made (a counted injected fetch proves zero calls), while a loopback mock is allowed by default and an external host is reachable only through `allowExternalProviders` scoped to the configured host or an explicit `allowHosts` entry. Input must be a non-empty string below the character and UTF-8 byte caps without lone surrogates or NUL, the router is configured once and frozen (a caller-supplied policy array is still shared), and unknown option or input fields fail closed without invoking a getter.
- Out of scope: the `/v1/router/multimodel` and legacy `/v1/router/{raw,provider}` endpoints, model selection itself (B3 maps a tier to a provider/model), the Morph-hosted model rows and the approval-gated abliterated model, Morph-hosted chat through the Router, Bifrost supervision, live provider calls or keys in tests, caching, retries beyond the single fallback result, streaming, telemetry and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.

### 2026-11-26 - M3 Ollama discovery and local preset wiring

- Goal / must-haves: let first-run and provider setup find an already running local Ollama daemon without the user typing a URL or model name: probe only `127.0.0.1:<port>` (`/api/tags` at port 11434 by default), read its installed models with bounded, hostile-input-safe parsing, choose a local chat model and an optional local embedding model (never a cloud/remote one), and hand the caller the two exact overrides the B1 `ollama` preset takes (`baseUrl`, `model`). The B1 `ollama` preset must pass chat, streaming, tool-call and embedding turns against a mock server.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (Providers: local llama.cpp / Ollama; each preset passes chat, streaming and tool-call tests against a mock server).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/ollama.mjs`, `tests/vibe/ollama.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-ollama.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, the llama-server supervisor, the model downloader and `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the hardware probe, the first-run controller or panel, the gateway modules and provider presets, Bifrost or sidecar supervision, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, new dependencies or vendored binaries, live network access in tests, `vibe/sidecars.json`, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/ollama.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `discoverOllama`, when the M1 policy allows it, asks exactly one `GET http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/tags` with `accept: application/json`, `redirect: 'error'` and no credentials, and returns a frozen view whose models are validated (plain object, a name in the provider model-name class, a bounded optional size, family, families, parameter size and quantization, no control or bidi characters), sorted by name; null optional metadata (size, family, families, parameter size, quantization) is treated as absent, `remote` is set for a non-null `remote_model`/`remote_host` or a `:cloud` id, `embedding` comes from the family/name heuristic (including `families` alone), an entry that carries only `model` is accepted, and unknown entry fields are ignored; the local chat model is the largest non-embedding non-remote model with a name tie-break and the embedding model the largest embedding one, and a missing chat or embedding model is a notice, not an error; an empty list is `available: true` with `ollama-no-models`, while a non-200 status, a non-JSON body or content type, a JSON body over the byte cap (an exact-cap body is accepted and one byte over is rejected), a body-less injected response read through `text()` and its cap, a non-bytes stream chunk, an oversized or malformed model list, and a body that stalls are reported as bounded `unavailable` reasons with no body, URL or error text, never thrown; a refused connection is `unreachable`, a request that ignores the abort signal still returns `timeout` within the probe deadline, a redirect is not followed, and the endpoint is evaluated by the M1 policy before the request (a denial, a missing decision and a throwing evaluator each return `policy-denied` with no fetch, while an allowed decision proceeds); options, results and the `toOllamaProviderOptions` input are read through own data descriptors, so a hostile Proxy, an accessor (never invoked), an unknown key, a port outside 1024-65535, an out-of-range timeout or byte cap, a non-function fetch or evaluate, and a host or URL override fail closed; `toOllamaProviderOptions` returns exactly `{ baseUrl, model }` for the discovered chat model or a caller-chosen discovered local chat model and rejects an unavailable result, an unknown, remote or embedding-only model and a forged or stale default that is absent, remote or embedding-only; and the B1 `ollama` preset created from that result passes plain chat, SSE streaming, tool-call and embedding turns against the same loopback mock server with no authorization header.
- Out of scope: starting, installing, stopping or updating Ollama; pulling models; the `/api/embeddings` endpoint; the Responses transport (`/v1/responses`); model capability discovery beyond the name/family heuristic; wiring the result into the first-run wizard or the provider setup panel (a later M3 slice); llama.cpp binary pinning or bundling; any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Parked cross-scope note: none expected.

- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/ollama.mjs` adds a dependency-free discovery of an already running local daemon. `discoverOllama({ port?, timeoutMs?, maxResponseBytes?, fetch?, evaluate? })` validates options through own data descriptors, evaluates the endpoint through the M1 policy core (a denial, a missing decision or a throwing evaluator is `policy-denied` with no request), and makes at most one `GET` to the loopback `/api/tags` endpoint with `accept: application/json`, `redirect: 'error'`, `credentials: 'omit'` and no credential. One deadline races the request and the body, so an injected fetch that ignores the abort signal still returns `timeout`; the body is read incrementally under a 256 KiB default cap (a caller may raise it to 4 MiB); only an injected fetch may return a body-less response, which is read with `text()` and capped; non-byte stream chunks are rejected; a non-200 or non-JSON failure aborts the request; a redirect is never followed. The answer is untrusted input: own-descriptor reads (accessors never invoked, hostile or revoked Proxies rejected), a model-name class that is a strict subset of the provider preset class, bounded metadata and families, a 256-model cap, control/bidi/ill-formed rejection, and an empty or chat-less list reported as notices with `available: true`. Each model is projected to `{ id, sizeBytes, parameterSize, quantization, family, embedding, remote }`, where `embedding` is the family/name heuristic and `remote` covers `remote_model`, `remote_host` and a `:cloud` tag; the list is name-sorted and the defaults are the largest local chat model and the largest local embedding model (size tie by name), with cloud entries never selected. Failures are frozen values with one bounded notice and the integer status where relevant, never an exception, a URL, a body or error text. `toOllamaProviderOptions(result, { model? })` returns exactly `{ baseUrl, model }` and re-validates the result version, the loopback `/v1` base URL and that the default chat/embedding model appears in the list with `remote: false` and the matching embedding flag. `docs/vibe/design/m3-ollama.md` records the live facts (verified 2026-11-26), contract, failure model, security boundary and residual risks.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/ollama.test.mjs` PASS (38/38); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 624 tests: 614 pass, 10 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail; after merging `origin/vibe/main` at #68 the same run is 671 tests: 660 pass, 11 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `git diff --check` clean; the 10 changed/untracked files are ASCII, LF, final-newline, no tabs, with 3-space Markdown list continuations.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS twice (the first pass grew 18 to 32 tests and proved nine mutations load-bearing with the module hash unchanged; the second pass on the hardened tree grew to 37 and proved the injected-only body path, non-byte chunk rejection, forged-default re-validation and content-type tolerance by mutation, with no experimental test flag left). drift-sentinel PASS after fixing the CHANGELOG/SECURITY template tokens, the design-doc indentation, the empty-list wording, the Proxy wording, the name-class wording, the `evaluate` signature and two design nuances; it confirmed exactly the card paths, 13/13 locks, no dependency or patch drift, no cross-scope work and no overstatement. security-reviewer PASS twice and closed all six findings: the body-less response is now injected-fetch-only, chunks must be ArrayBuffer views and are converted through their offset/length, a non-200/non-JSON failure aborts, the policy check and `decision.allowed` read are inside one try, the metadata taint contract is documented in the design and SECURITY, and the cap wording matches the caller-bounded 4 MiB ceiling; ```text
You are the vibe-overseer for the Vibecodium build, running unattended inside an autonomous goal loop. No human will answer questions during this session. The loop runs on our own models (see vibe/goal/models.json); every guardian is a sub-agent defined once in .vibe/agents/ (generated for the active harness).

2. If STATUS.md says "Goal state: complete" or "Goal state: blocked", verify that claim, print GOAL_STATE=<state> and stop.
3. Do exactly one task slice following the "One task slice" procedure in .vibe/agents/vibe-overseer.md. Launch each guardian in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 4 as a separate sub-agent (qa-verifier, drift-sentinel, security-reviewer, docs-keeper, governance-verifier, tech-scout, release-manager) and wait for its report.
4. Prefer the Morph tools: codebase search (WarpGrep) to find code, edit_file (Fast Apply) for edits to existing files, github_codebase_search to study other repositories.
5. Your task workspace must be a clone or worktree under <clone>.worktrees (the clone's sibling folder) - files there are writable; the trunk's own files may be read-only to you.
6. Never cross a hard stop in docs/vibe/GOVERNANCE.md section 5. Some hard-stop commands are blocked or outside your writable area; do not try to work around a refusal. Park business decisions in STATUS.md and continue with other unblocked work.
7. Before finishing: node vibe/tools/verify.mjs must pass on the trunk, the STATUS entry must be written, and the branch must be pushed with a PR opened into vibe/main (gh pr create --repo ynotfins/vibecodium --base vibe/main). Merge it when CI is green (gh pr merge --merge).
8. End your final message with one line: SLICE_RESULT=done|parked|failed and GOAL_STATE=active|complete|blocked.

## LANE SCOPE

This lane owns milestone M3 only: the local runtime sidecar (llama-server), the model downloader, and the first-run wizard.

Do not start M2 items (lane A owns M2 completion) and do not start M4/M5/M6 work. If you find work outside M3 that matters, record it under "Parked for owner" in docs/vibe/STATUS.md and continue with M3.
```



### 2026-11-25 - M2 B6 WarpGrep code-search agent tool

- Goal / must-haves: close B6's last tool acceptance item, "WarpGrep is available as an agent tool", with a bounded, read-only `codebase_search` tool that drives the `morph-warp-grep-v2.1` model through a short tool loop over one workspace and returns only file/line spans it has itself read.
- Must-have IDs: B6 (WarpGrep agent tool acceptance); consumes the B1 request client like the delivered Fast Apply tool.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/morph-warp-grep.mjs`, `tests/vibe/morph-warp-grep.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-warp-grep.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (added 2026-11-26 for the direct-API WarpGrep row).
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, changes to the B1 catalog/client, B2 fallback, B3 router/budgets, the B6 direct policy or Compact, Fast Apply, new dependencies or vendored binaries, build scripts and CI workflows, release infrastructure, secret storage, Morph-hosted models, the Router, the approval-gated abliterated model, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, live provider calls, the local runtime and the in-IDE history path.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/morph-warp-grep.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: the tool exposes the documented `codebase_search` function schema and, against a local mock provider endpoint through a real B1 client and a throwaway workspace, runs the trained loop shape: a first user turn carrying `<repo_structure>` and `<search_string>`, five read-only tools (`grep_search`, `glob`, `list_directory`, `read`, `finish`), a turn-counter user message after every tool round with a `finish` nudge on the penultimate turn, absolute in-workspace paths accepted and alternation patterns matched, and a scripted grep -> read -> finish run that returns the finished relative paths, their line ranges and their bounded content, frozen, with aggregated usage and no query, file body, credential, endpoint or response echo in an error or in `describe()`; every loop request carries no `tools` array (the model's five tools are built in, per the live WarpGrep docs) and no stream; a finish path outside the workspace, an absolute, drive, UNC, traversal, encoded, symlink, directory, missing, binary or oversized `read`/`glob` target, an excluded `.git`/dependency/build file or folder reached through the walk or an explicit path, and a target outside the workspace resolve fail closed without reading it; `.git` and `node_modules` are excluded by default and `node_modules` is searched only with `search_type: 'node_modules'`; a text-only answer, a finish with no files, a malformed or oversized finish, an oversized or repeated-range result, a provider error, a non-retryable HTTP status, an accessor-backed provider answer, a turn-limit run, a per-turn and a total tool-call overflow and a rejected `repo_path` fail closed with fixed codes and bounded status; and malformed options/input, unknown fields, accessors, sparse arrays, invalid `search_type`, oversized query and error-code shaping are covered without invoking a getter.
- Acceptance amendment (2026-11-26, owner-independent): the live WarpGrep docs (`https://docs.morphllm.com/sdk/components/warp-grep/direct` and `/guides/warp-grep-python`, read 2026-11-26) state the five search tools are built into the model and must not be sent as a `tools` array. The original card said each turn "carries the same five tools"; the delivered request now sends only `model` and `messages` (the B1 client adds `stream: false`), `temperature`/`max_tokens` are left at their documented defaults (0 and 2048), and the mock asserts `'tools' in request.body === false`. The executor still implements all five tools locally. `docs/vibe/design/m2-warp-grep.md` records the same fact.
- Files allowed addition (2026-11-26): `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` is added to the allowed files so the direct-API WarpGrep surface and its built-in-tools contract are recorded against the existing `@morphllm/morphmcp` row without waiting for a separate radar-only slice. No dependency, package or binary changes.
- Out of scope: the Router, Morph-hosted models, the approval-gated abliterated model, live provider calls, agent-host wiring (M6), streaming loop steps, remote GitHub repository search, remote context compaction, regular-expression patterns beyond a plain `a|b` alternation, the documented `temperature`/`max_tokens` sampling hints, write access of any kind, caching, a persistent index, Bifrost supervision and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/morph-warp-grep.test.mjs` PASS (47/47, including three read/glob/provider-leak cases added by the QA gates and four hardening cases for unsafe message characters, model reasoning text, an oversized tool-call argument and the glob output budget); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS on the final tree after the `vibe/main` merge (4/4 steps; 633 tests: 621-622 pass, 11 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail; the total includes the merged M3 hardware-probe suite); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `git diff --check` clean.
- Delivered: `src/stable/gateway/morph-warp-grep.mjs` adds the bounded `codebase_search` tool over `morph-warp-grep-v2.1` through the injected B1 client. The loop is at most six turns; the model has its five read-only tools built in, so no request sends a `tools` array, and the executor implements `grep_search`, `glob`, `list_directory`, `read` and `finish` locally; the first user turn carries a workspace-relative `<repo_structure>` (depth two, 200 entries, 32 KiB) and `<search_string>`, and each tool round is followed by a turn counter with a `finish` nudge on the penultimate turn. Relative paths are validated segment by segment, an absolute or drive path is accepted only when its canonical form stays inside the real root, and `.git`, dependency and build names plus a best-effort credential-name set are excluded from the walk, `glob`, `list_directory` and every explicit path, with Win32 trailing-dot/space aliases normalised. Files are opened `O_NOFOLLOW` where available with a realpath and device/inode check and strict UTF-8; binary, undecodable and files above 1 MiB are invisible. Grep matches a literal or plain `a|b` alternation (no regex), globs use a linear segment matcher, and every walk, grep, glob, listing, read, finish and loop is capped (8 MiB grep, 200 matches, 400 read lines, 32 files/32 KiB/256 KiB, 6 turns, 8 calls per turn, 24 total). `finish` budgets while selecting, `repo_path` must equal the root, unknown tool arguments are ignored while known fields are validated, the provider answer is read defensively and an accessor-backed answer fails closed, and no error carries the query, file body, endpoint or key. `docs/vibe/design/m2-warp-grep.md` records the contract and boundaries.
- CI fix (2026-11-26): the first PR run failed `ecformat` on three points. One trailing-space line in `morph-warp-grep.mjs` and the two 2-space `m2-warp-grep.md` indents are corrected to the 3-space Markdown indent. `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md` was converted to ASCII (`-` for the header separator and dashes, `section 7`, `->`) because ecformat's charset detector classified the longer non-ASCII file as latin1 against the `utf-8` EditorConfig; `docs-check` accepts the `-` header separator and the 14 milestone rows are unchanged in meaning. The same pass removed a doubled blank line in this log.
- Follow-up (later M2/M6 work): live provider smoke test (the loop is proven against a mock endpoint); regex patterns beyond a plain alternation and the documented `temperature`/`max_tokens` sampling hints; a `(truncated)` marker for a capped repo-structure listing and marker headroom for `list_directory`; control/bidi file names are still listed verbatim in a structure or listing (a spoofing, not containment, gap); a directory swapped for a junction after `readdir` can be walked outside the root (needs workspace write access); hard links inside the workspace are readable; a literal `.env ` name is an ordinary distinct file on case-sensitive (POSIX) filesystems; the tool caps are counted in characters, not UTF-8 bytes; agent-host wiring and the in-IDE history path remain M6.

Merged as `c1dbdb2` on 2026-11-26 by the owner account. - Acceptance test: `projectCatalogModels` validates the pinned catalog and projects only its chat and embedding entries to exactly `{ id, role, sizeBytes, contextTokens }`, rejects a catalog without a chat or an embedding role, and never mutates the input; `createFirstRunWizard` validates options through own data descriptors (a Proxy, an accessor, an unknown key, a bad destination directory, a malformed catalog or an invalid executable path fail closed without invoking a getter), probes once, recommends the plan from the projected models, verifies each pinned file without touching the network, and reports a frozen view whose `needs-download`, `ready`, `insufficient` and `failed` phases, `downloadBytes` and merged probe and plan notices are correct; `install()` downloads only the missing models chat first, reports progress, verifies every installed file before marking it installed, is single-flight, refuses an insufficient machine and an already ready one, honours cancellation by keeping a resumable partial, and on a failure keeps installed files and a safe `{ code, message }` with no path or body leak; `startPlan()` reports `not-ready` before installs finish, `runtime-not-bundled` without a packaged binary, and otherwise the chat model path plus the plan's context, threads and GPU layers; the webview panel renders the verdict, models, notices and progress, drives check/download/start only for a plain object with exactly one own enumerable data `type` property (rejecting a Proxy or revoked Proxy, an array, an unknown key, an accessor, a non-object and a non-string `type` as `invalid-message`, and a `type` over 64 KiB of UTF-8 as `message-too-large`), renders a CSP whose `style-src` and `script-src` nonces equal the nonce on the `<style>` and `<script>` tags (with `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'none'` and a valid fallback nonce), escapes every dynamic value, and reports a missing wizard safely; a synchronous downloader throw reports `install-failed` and leaves the wizard free for a retry that finishes the missing models.
- Out of scope: spawning or supervising `llama-server`, bundling, downloading or signing the llama.cpp binary, copying the catalog into a packaged application, restart/back-off, Ollama detection, live provider calls, model-ladder changes, NVML or per-GPU vendor libraries, and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.

- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/first-run.mjs` adds a dependency-free headless wizard. `projectCatalogModels` validates the pinned catalog and emits exactly `{ id, role, sizeBytes, contextTokens }` for the chat and embedding entries, skipping other roles and failing closed without a required role. `createFirstRunWizard` validates the catalog, destination, optional probe/downloader/verifier/runtime path and change callback through own data descriptors (Proxy, accessor, unknown key, traversal destination, malformed catalog and invalid executable fail closed), probes once, recommends the plan from the projection, verifies the pinned files without the network, and exposes a frozen view with the phase, verdict, acceleration, hardware summary, model rows, `downloadBytes`, merged probe and plan notices, progress and steps. `install()` downloads only missing models chat first, clamps and forwards progress, requires both the downloader's `verified` result and a fresh verifier pass before marking a model installed, is single-flight, refuses an insufficient or already-ready machine, keeps a resumable partial on abort, and returns a bounded code plus a fixed message after a failure so installed files are kept and a retry skips them; it starts on a microtask and assigns its promise before the change event, so a synchronously throwing injected downloader cannot leave the wizard busy. `startPlan()` is `not-ready` before the installs finish, `runtime-not-bundled` without a packaged binary, and otherwise returns the chat and embedding model paths plus the plan's context, threads and GPU layers without spawning anything. `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/first-run.cjs` adds the setup panel: it renders the phase, steps, verdict, models, notices, progress and start summary with every dynamic value escaped;
