### 2026-11-25 - M3 GPU/RAM hardware probe and local runtime recommendation

- Goal / must-haves: give the first-run wizard a dependency-free hardware probe (RAM, CPU count, Windows GPU list) and a pure policy that maps those facts plus the pinned model catalog to a frozen start plan for the local B1 provider (the model choice plus the context, thread and GPU-layer numbers its `llama-server` flags use), so an under-powered machine is told so instead of being started into an out-of-memory failure, and a capable machine gets GPU offload without guessing.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local llama.cpp preset, first-run side); third M3 slice, feeding the first-run wizard.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/hardware-probe.mjs`, `tests/vibe/hardware-probe.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-hardware-probe.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (header date only), plus cross-reference-only corrections in `docs/vibe/design/m3-model-downloader.md` and `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md` requested by docs-keeper (both wrongly described the probe as building on the supervisor or selecting a model ladder).
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, the llama-server supervisor, the model downloader or `vibe/models/catalog.json`, the first-run wizard UI or extension host code, restart/back-off, Ollama detection, Bifrost, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, new dependencies or vendored binaries, live network access in tests, `vibe/sidecars.json`, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/hardware-probe.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: `probeHardware` reports platform, arch, CPU model and logical core count, total and free RAM, and on Windows the GPU list from one fixed PowerShell query whose child is the absolute system `powershell.exe`, non-interactive, bounded by a timeout and an output cap; the GPU line parser accepts only base64 names with a decimal `AdapterRAM` and rejects a malformed, oversized or blank-name line, control or bidi names, an unknown field count, more than eight GPUs and invalid base64; a failed, timed-out, oversized or non-zero GPU query degrades to no GPUs with a bounded notice and never throws or echoes child output; a non-Windows probe returns no GPUs with a notice rather than shelling out; `recommendRuntime` maps the facts and the pinned models to a frozen plan (verdict, acceleration, chat and embedding ids, context size, threads, GPU layers, estimated memory, available memory and notice codes) and is covered for: a discrete GPU with at least 4 GiB offloads all layers, the WMI 4095 MiB cap counts as large, an integrated or software GPU gets zero layers, Apple silicon on arm64 gets full offload as unified memory, a machine without a GPU stays on the CPU, RAM below 8 GiB is `insufficient`, RAM below 16 GiB or a CPU-only machine is `tight` and a 16 GiB GPU on a machine with at least 16 GiB RAM is `ready`; the largest fitting chat model wins and a chat model larger than the budget falls back to the smallest with `insufficient`; threads leave two cores free and are capped at 16; a context above 8192 is capped with a notice; a GPU budget that cannot hold the smallest chat model falls back to CPU with a notice; a catalog with no chat or no embedding model, duplicate ids, unknown roles, oversized sizes or context, and a hardware descriptor with unknown keys, accessors, sparse arrays, class instances, prototype keys, control or bidi strings, a bad platform or arch, zero or oversized memory, more than eight GPUs or an out-of-range GPU memory fail closed without invoking a getter; and the plan is frozen and neither it nor the input is aliased (the input is not mutated).
- Out of scope: spawning or supervising `llama-server`, calling any provider, downloading anything, the first-run wizard UI, model-ladder adoption beyond the two pinned catalog entries, Ollama detection, restart/back-off, NVML or per-GPU vendor libraries, live network access, and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Parked cross-scope note: none expected.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/hardware-probe.mjs` adds a dependency-free probe and a pure recommendation policy. `probeHardware(options)` reports platform, arch, CPU model, logical cores, total and free memory and, on Windows, the display adapters from one fixed read-only `Win32_VideoController` CIM query run through the absolute system `%SystemRoot%\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe` with `-NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -EncodedCommand`, no shell, no stdin, `windowsHide`, a 5-second default timeout, a 64 KiB output cap, an allowlisted environment (`SystemRoot`, `windir`, `TEMP`, `TMP` and an empty `PATH`) and a probe-level deadline that also bounds an injected runner. Each adapter is emitted as base64 name, tab and decimal `AdapterRAM`; the parser accepts only canonical base64 with valid UTF-8, a name of 1 to 200 characters without control or bidi characters and a 1 to 15 digit size, rejects an interior blank line, a malformed or oversized answer or more than eight adapters, and returns a frozen list with vendor and kind. A spawn failure, timeout, non-zero exit, malformed answer or over-cap text degrades to no GPUs with `gpu-probe-failed` or `gpu-probe-invalid`, never throws and never surfaces child output; a non-Windows platform never shells out and reports `gpu-probe-unavailable`; a successful empty answer reports `gpu-probe-empty`. The OS CPU name is stripped of control and bidi characters, whitespace-collapsed and length-bounded. `recommendRuntime(hardware, models)` validates the descriptor and the model list through own data descriptors (rejecting Proxies before any trap runs and accessors without invoking them), requires chat and embedding roles, and returns a frozen plan: `verdict` (`ready`/`tight`/`insufficient`), `acceleration` (`gpu`/`unified`/`cpu`), `gpuLayers` (999 or 0), `threads` (cores minus two, 1 to 16), `contextSize` (chat context capped at 8192, embedding at 2048), chat and embedding model ids, estimated chat and embedding memory (`sizeBytes + 512 MiB + contextTokens * 16 KiB`), `availableMemoryBytes` (75% of RAM, or the chosen GPU's memory minus 512 MiB), total memory and de-duplicated ordered notice codes. A discrete GPU that holds the chosen chat model (512 MiB minimum usable, 4095 MiB to 4 GiB `AdapterRAM` cap marked `vram-capped`, 3583 MiB budget at the cap) offloads every layer; integrated, software, unknown or absent GPUs stay on the CPU; `darwin` + `arm64` uses unified memory; a GPU too small for the smallest chat model falls back to RAM with `model-exceeds-vram`; the largest fitting chat model wins, otherwise the smallest with `model-too-large`. `docs/vibe/design/m3-hardware-probe.md` records the contract, policy table, security boundary and residual risks, and the README index, ROADMAP M3 row, CHANGELOG, SECURITY, STATUS milestone line and TECH_RADAR rows 30/31 were reconciled. M3 remains in progress.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/hardware-probe.test.mjs` PASS (36/36, 0 skipped; includes the real read-only host probe, the real child runner's absolute path, non-interactive flags, empty inherited environment, timeout and output cap, and a never-settling injected runner deadline); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 562 tests: 552 pass, 10 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `node vibe/tools/sync-agents.mjs --check` PASS; `git diff --check` clean. ### 2026-11-25 - M3 local model downloader and pinned model catalog

- Goal / must-haves: give first-run provisioning a dependency-free downloader that installs the pinned GGUF weights from a shipped catalog, resumes an interrupted transfer, verifies SHA-256 and byte size before install, and never leaves a corrupt or unverified file under the final name.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local llama.cpp preset / local runtime); second M3 slice, feeding the first-run wizard.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/model-downloader.mjs`, `vibe/models/catalog.json`, `tests/vibe/model-downloader.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-model-downloader.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, llama-server supervision or process code, the first-run wizard UI, the hardware probe, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, new dependencies or vendored binaries, live network downloads in tests, `vibe/sidecars.json`, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/model-downloader.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: the catalog validates and pins every model by 40-hex revision, 64-hex SHA-256 and exact byte size; a completed download writes the verified bytes under the final name and leaves no partial file; a pre-existing `.part` resumes with a `Range` request and only transfers the remainder; a server that ignores `Range` restarts the transfer; a checksum, oversized or announced-size mismatch fails with a bounded code, installs nothing and removes the partial file; a short or dropped body fails as a transport error and keeps the partial file; an already verified file is returned without a request; an existing invalid file is replaced; caller cancellation keeps the partial file for resume and a second call finishes it; a stalled connection is timed out and keeps the partial file; HTTP errors do not leak the response body; non-loopback `http:`, userinfo, query and fragment URLs are refused; a redirect to a non-HTTPS URL, or to loopback `http` from an `https` pin, is refused; an insufficient disk space check fails before any request; one deadline covers both attempts; a junction above a missing destination and a reserved Win32 destination name are refused; and malformed catalogs (unknown version, duplicate ids, path traversal, branch revision, bad hash or size, disallowed license, reserved device names, accessors, prototype pollution, too many entries) fail closed.
- Out of scope: llama-server sidecar supervision and health checks, the GPU/RAM hardware probe, the first-run wizard UI, restart/back-off, Ollama auto-detection, Bifrost wiring, live model downloads in tests, and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/model-downloader.mjs` adds a dependency-free catalog validator and a resumable, digest-verified downloader. The catalog validates its version, root keys, the entry array and all twelve entry fields through own data descriptors, refuses duplicate ids and file names, branch revisions, non-GGUF or nested names, reserved Win32 device names, a disallowed license, sizes above the 12 GiB bound and a URL with credentials, query or fragment, and freezes the accepted catalog. `downloadModel(entry, options)` resolves the destination and creates it only when missing and only after the nearest existing ancestor has been proven to be a real, non-symlink directory; it checks free space for the whole file before the first request and streams the pinned URL into `<final>.part` under an idle deadline and one total deadline shared by both attempts. A resume sends `Range: bytes=<n>-`; a server that answers `200` instead restarts the transfer, a `206` must declare the pinned total, a `416` with a complete part installs it without a request, and any other status fails as `http-status` with an integer status and no body. The final response URL must be `https`, or loopback `http` only when the pinned URL is loopback, without credentials, query or fragment. Bytes are capped at the pinned size; a progress callback that throws is ignored; cancellation, a stall, the total deadline and a dropped or short body keep the partial file, while an oversized body, an announced size mismatch, a size mismatch and a digest mismatch remove it and never install anything. A finished part is checked for exact size, hashed in one streaming pass and renamed into place only on a match; a resumed attempt that fails verification is retried once from zero. An existing final file is hashed first and returned as `skipped` when correct or replaced when not. `verifyInstalledModel(entry, { destinationDir })` reports `{ verified, path, sizeBytes }` without touching the network, and a process-local guard refuses a second concurrent download of the same path. `vibe/models/catalog.json` pins `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B-Instruct-GGUF` (`q4_k_m`, about 1.04 GiB) and `Qwen/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B-GGUF` (`q8_0`, 610 MiB), both Apache-2.0, by 40-character revision, digest and exact byte size; both were re-checked against the live Hugging Face file listing and `resolve` URLs on 2026-11-25. `docs/vibe/design/m3-model-downloader.md` records the contract, transfer, verification, failure model, security boundary, residuals and verification; the README index, the ROADMAP M3 cell, CHANGELOG, SECURITY, TECH_RADAR (license-checked alternatives and the embedding `--pooling last` note) and the sibling supervisor scope sentence are updated. M3 remains in progress.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/model-downloader.test.mjs` PASS (34/34, 0 skipped; the junction and symlink cases run on this host); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 526 tests: 516 pass, 10 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `node vibe/tools/sync-agents.mjs --check` PASS; `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS on the final tree (focused 34/34, strict 526: 516 pass, 10 recorded skips, 0 fail) after it added a catalog fragment-URL case and a fragment final-URL case and proved both load-bearing. drift-sentinel PASS after six living-doc corrections (model size, the realpath mechanism, a non-existent live sanity check, the `accept` header wording, the STATUS milestone line and the "ships" wording). security-reviewer PASS then PASS again after the hardening rounds: the final-URL loopback allowance is keyed to the pinned scheme and host, `waitForDrain` settles on drain/error/close/abort, the writer close settles immediately when already closed, a missing destination is created only after its nearest ancestor is proven real, reserved Win32 device and colon segments are refused, one deadline covers both attempts, free space is checked for the whole file, the partial path is re-inspected immediately before the write, and the remaining redirect-hop and hash-to-rename residuals are documented. docs-keeper PASS with design corrections (catalog field table, the 416 path, `verifyInstalledModel` creating the destination, the sibling supervisor scope) and the radar, CHANGELOG, ROADMAP and SECURITY wording. governance-verifier PASS, not applicable (no locked path or governance record changed; 13/13 locks). tech-scout PASS (both pins verified live at their revisions with matching digest and size, licenses re-checked, no swap recommended; the radar rows now carry license-checked alternatives and the embedding server flags). release-manager PASS, not applicable (in-milestone; no version, artefact, signing, feed or publication change).
- Follow-up: the next M3 slices are the GPU/RAM hardware probe, the first-run wizard, the `llama-server` binary pinning and bundling, restart/back-off and Ollama detection; packaging must copy `vibe/models/catalog.json` into the built application, and the runtime wiring must pass `--embedding --pooling last -ub 8192` for the pinned embedding weights and add the port-ownership or per-start authenticated readiness check noted by the supervisor slice. No cross-lane work was noticed.
- Delivery: PR #63 (`https://github.com/ynotfins/vibecodium/pull/63`), commits `ef2aa35` (feature and tests) and `1b27a21` (docs); CI on `1b27a21` passed `verify` (both runs), `zizmor` (both), `ecformat` and both `compile` jobs, while `build (macos-14, arm64)` was cancelled on its runner label (the known infrastructure limitation) and the cross-platform Linux matrix was still queued at merge time. Merged as `7e59c5f`. One trunk run after the merge flaked in an unrelated suite (515 pass, 1 fail, name not captured) and two consecutive re-runs passed (516 pass, 0 fail).

- Status: done.

### 2026-11-25 - M3 local llama-server supervisor

- Goal / must-haves: give M3 a dependency-free supervisor for the local `llama-server` so the local B1 provider can start on a loopback-only ephemeral port, be health-checked, stop cleanly and never be spawned through a shell, escape its validated paths, inherit the parent secret environment or outlive a failed start.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (local llama.cpp preset); first M3 runtime slice, feeding the model downloader and the first-run wizard.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs`, `tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/0010-local-runtime-llama-server-supervisor.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/README.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (included verification fix: the Upstream editor row was due on 2026-11-25, which fails `docs-check --strict` from 2026-11-26 UTC, so its live facts and the local-runtime row are refreshed with the supervisor delivery).
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, the B1 catalog/client, B2/B3/B5/B6 modules, actual llama.cpp binaries, downloads or weights, `vibe/sidecars.json`, the model downloader, the hardware probe, the first-run wizard, Bifrost, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, secret storage, live network downloads, new dependencies or vendored binaries.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: a fake `llama-server` (a Node fixture launched through the supervisor with the real `spawn`) receives exactly `--model <path> --host [127.0.0.1] --port <port>` plus optional `--ctx-size`, `--threads` and `--n-gpu-layers` and no shell-metacharacter interpretation; `start()` resolves only after `/health` returns 200 and reports the port, base URL and pid; `status()` moves starting -> running -> stopped and clears the port and base URL on stop; `stop()` terminates the child and is idempotent; a never-ready child fails as `startup-timeout` and is killed; a child that exits early fails as `server-exited`; a non-loopback host (tested through the exported validator; the controller has no host option), relative, drive-relative, UNC, symlinked or missing model/executable (and a POSIX executable without an execute bit), bad port, context, thread, GPU-layer, timeout or command argument fails closed without spawning; a restart issued during a stop waits and comes up on a new pid; a SIGTERM-ignoring child is escalated to SIGKILL after the graceful wait (POSIX only); `health()` reports not-ready outside the running state and never throws; caller environment overrides are merged over a minimal allowlist, the parent `PATH` and any provider key are not passed, and loader/system names are refused; a rejected `commandArgs` value never echoes; and the controller, its argument list and its status cannot be mutated.
- Out of scope: downloading weights or the binary, `vibe/sidecars.json` pinning, the hardware probe, the first-run wizard UI, chat/embeddings proxy calls, restart/back-off supervision, process-tree management beyond the direct child, Windows service integration, metrics, Ollama auto-detection and any dependency change. Node built-ins only.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/llama-server.test.mjs` PASS (17 tests: 16 pass, 1 Windows skip for the POSIX-only SIGKILL escalation; independent QA also ran the file on WSL Ubuntu at 17/17); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 492 tests: 481-482 pass, 10-11 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail; the count varies by one conditional IDE/credential test); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok; `git diff --check` clean.
- Delivered: `src/stable/runtime/llama-server.mjs` adds the supervisor described above: absolute non-symlink executable/model paths (`.exe` on Windows, execute bit on POSIX), a loopback ephemeral port, exact `--model`/`--host`/`--port` flags plus bounded optional flags, `shell: false`, the executable directory as cwd, an allowlisted environment with an empty `PATH`, a re-validation of both paths at start, health-gated readiness, an idempotent `SIGTERM`-then-`SIGKILL` stop, and start/stop serialization so a restart issued during a stop waits. `tests/vibe/fixtures/fake-llama-server.mjs` serves `/health`, `/v1/chat/completions` (plain, SSE and tool calls), `/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/models` and `/infill`, and can hang, fail health, exit early or ignore SIGTERM, so the suite drives the real B1 `llama-cpp` client against a real spawned process. `docs/vibe/design/m3-llama-server.md` records the contract, environment, failure model, security boundary and residual risks; ADR-0010 records the decision. M3 remains in progress.
- Follow-up (next M3 wiring slice): before prompts flow through the supervised port, add an authenticated per-start readiness check or a listening-socket ownership check (a caller-supplied port has no ephemeral-race narrowing), restrict CORS origins and decide on a per-start API key, and add process-tree/parent-death handling. These came from the security review and are deliberately not part of this card. Tech-scout also found two llama.cpp v1.0.0 facts to settle then: real `/v1/embeddings` returns 501 until the server is started with `--embeddings` (the fixture simulates embeddings), so decide between one chat+embedding server and a dedicated embedding server; and upstream's default server port is 9931, while the llama-cpp preset's documented base URL still says 8080 (the supervisor always passes an explicit port, so this is a stale manual-server/docs fact, not a supervisor bug).
- Guardians: qa-verifier FAIL then PASS twice on the fixed tree (the first run found a POSIX executable gap, an untested SIGKILL escalation and the pre-existing overdue radar row; focused 17 tests: 16 pass + 1 Windows skip, real WSL Ubuntu 17/17, strict 492: 481-482 pass, 10-11 environment-conditional skips, 0 fail; non-vacuity probes for the readiness gate, empty PATH, parent secrecy, port clearing, realpath/junction, POSIX execute bit and SIGKILL). drift-sentinel PASS (exactly the 12 card paths, no locked/patch/dependency change; low doc wording fixes applied: fixture-only B1 claim, M3 open items, health-timeout and argument-prefix wording, `already-starting`, caller-supplied port). security-reviewer PASS then PASS again after the fixes (case-insensitive env denials plus `LD_AUDIT`, `DYLD_*` fallbacks, `GGML_BACKEND_PATH` and `LLAMA_ARG_*`; reserved-key and env size bounds; revoked/throwing proxy mapping to `LlamaServerError`; `-ag`/`--ui-agent`/`--ui-mcp-proxy` refusals; residual risks documented; no secret, dependency, license, CI or release change). docs-keeper PASS with four living-doc corrections (`DYLD_FRAMEWORK_PATH`, `start-aborted` or `server-exited` on stop-during-start, Node-added Windows identity variables, and the radar deadline rationale); docs-check 0/0. governance-verifier PASS, not applicable (no locked path or governance record changed; 13/13 locks; generated agents and the B1 surface intact). tech-scout PASS (no dependency or binary; live facts re-verified, row 14 kept at VS Code 1.148.0 published 2026-11-25 with adoption deferred to 2026-12-02, row 30 corrected to llama.cpp v1.0.0 plus latest nightly b12751 and the no-bundle posture; llama.cpp v1.0.0 still accepts all six supervisor flags and the four endpoints; the `--embeddings` 501 and upstream default port 9931 are recorded as follow-ups). release-manager PASS, not applicable (in-milestone; no version, artifact, signing, feed or publication change).
- Delivery: PR #62 (`https://github.com/ynotfins/vibecodium/pull/62`), commits `ebe3a47` (supervisor and docs) and `939b482` (ASCII header separators for ecformat). First CI run: `verify`, `zizmor`, `ecformat` (after the header fix), `docs-check` and the automated review pass; `build (macos-14, arm64)` fails in 1s on its runner label (the known infrastructure limitation) and the `compile` / `build (macos-15-intel, x64)` jobs were still queued at merge time. Merge follows this docs commit after the core checks re-run green.

- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/morph-fast-apply.mjs`, `tests/vibe/morph-fast-apply.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-fast-apply.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, changes to the B1 catalog/client, B2 fallback, B3 router/budgets, the B6 direct policy or Compact, new dependencies or vendored binaries, build scripts and CI workflows, release infrastructure, secret storage, WarpGrep, Router, Morph-hosted models, the approval-gated abliterated model, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, live provider calls and the in-IDE history path.

### 2026-11-25 - M2 B6 Compact history pruning

- Goal / must-haves: deliver B6's Compact feature as a dependency-free, line-preserving history pruner for the context engine, so an over-budget chat history can be reduced to a token budget while every surviving line stays byte-identical to its source.
- Must-have IDs: B6 (Compact acceptance only: "Compact output keeps every surviving line byte-identical"); consumes the D1 lossless history shape and composes with the D4 token budget.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/context-engine/compact.mjs`, `tests/vibe/compact.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-compact.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, changes to the B1 catalog/client, B2 fallback, B3 router/budgets or B6 direct policy, new dependencies or vendored binaries, build scripts and CI workflows, release infrastructure, secret storage, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, Fast Apply, WarpGrep, Router, Morph-hosted models, the approval-gated abliterated model and live provider calls.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/compact.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: given a mixed history (ASCII and non-ASCII text, CRLF and LF endings, tabs, trailing spaces, empty lines and one line larger than the whole budget), compaction to a tight budget keeps pinned system messages and the newest messages, drops whole older messages and drops only leading whole lines of the boundary message, and every surviving line is byte-identical to the original line in its original order; the reported token estimate is at or under the budget; the same input produces the same output; pinned content that alone exceeds the budget fails closed with `compact-overflow`, and a history above the aggregate character or line cap fails closed; malformed options, messages, roles, non-string content, ill-formed Unicode, unknown fields and accessor-backed values fail closed without invoking a getter, and no input object, buffer or credential is retained in the result.
- Out of scope: Fast Apply, WarpGrep, Router, Morph-hosted models, the approval-gated abliterated model, Bifrost supervision, live provider calls, model-backed summarization, tool-call pairing and the in-IDE history path. No dependency change.
- Delivered: `src/stable/context-engine/compact.mjs` adds a dependency-free `compactHistory` for the D1 chat-history shape. It reserves every `system` message and every explicitly pinned index, keeps pinned messages at their original positions, keeps the newest contiguous droppable tail, drops whole older messages and keeps only the longest whole-line suffix of the one boundary message after rechecking its whole content (keeping it unmarked when a later count fits and dropping it when not even its last line fits). A history is bounded as it is read and split - at most 10,000 messages, 1,000,000 characters per message, 8,000,000 characters and 1,000,000 lines in total - so an oversized history fails closed instead of allocating unbounded line arrays. Options, messages, roles, content, Unicode (including message names and tool ids), pinned indexes and `tool_calls` are read through own data descriptors; accessors, sparse arrays and revoked or throwing proxies fail closed without invoking a getter or echoing a value, and message names and tool ids reject control, format and bidi characters. Pinned content that alone exceeds the budget raises `compact-overflow` with integer token counts; a custom tokenizer must return 0 only for empty text, must not under-count the D4 UTF-8 byte floor, and is probed for monotonicity at several suffix positions, so an under-counting tokenizer, or one that fails a probe, fails closed (`compact-tokenizer`) rather than inflating the kept history; and the returned messages, report and index lists are frozen while the input is never mutated or aliased. Each retained suffix is charged the exact count that was compared for it, so `usedTokens` is the sum of the tokens actually charged and never exceeds the budget even for a non-deterministic tokenizer, and structural checks such as `Array.isArray` on the options object or a message element are wrapped so a revoked proxy becomes a `CompactError`. `docs/vibe/design/m2-compact.md` records the contract, algorithm, report and boundaries; the README index, ROADMAP M2 status, CHANGELOG and SECURITY are updated.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/compact.test.mjs` PASS (29/29: pinned and newest retention, whole-line boundary truncation over CRLF/LF/tabs/trailing spaces/empty lines/non-ASCII, an over-budget last line, a trailing empty line, empty content, tool metadata, determinism and frozen no-alias output, a caller tokenizer, a tokenizer that throws, exceeds the ceiling, returns a fraction, NaN or a string, returns 0 for text, returns a non-zero count for empty text, under-counts the D4 byte floor or is non-monotone, pinned overflow, malformed options/messages/budgets/roles/pinned lists, sparse input, the per-message character cap and the aggregate character and line caps, `tool_calls` rejection, control/bidi and ill-formed Unicode metadata rejection, revoked proxies (including as the options object or a message element), a non-deterministic tokenizer that must never report `usedTokens` above the budget, a non-deterministic tokenizer that keeps a whole message without reporting a truncation, and accessor non-invocation). `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 465 tests: 456 pass, 9 Windows skips, 0 fail). `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean; `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS on the final tree (focused 29/29; strict 465 tests: 456 pass, 9 Windows skips, 0 fail; docs 0/0; governance 13/13), with non-vacuity probes showing the shared-count charge, the `Array.isArray` guard, the byte floor, the aggregate character and line caps, the metadata surrogate checks, the proxy mapping and the monotonicity probes each load-bearing. security-reviewer PASS after two rounds: all five original findings plus a broken `usedTokens <= budget` invariant for a non-deterministic tokenizer and a raw `TypeError` for revoked proxies in every position were fixed; independent probing of 493 non-deterministic runs found no `usedTokens > budget`, every proxy position returned `CompactError`, and no secret, dependency, network or lock change was found. drift-sentinel PASS (exactly the 8 allowed paths, no locked or patch-budget change, Compact not overstated) with four doc findings fixed (D4 total wording, the in-IDE history path in the open-work lists, the CHANGELOG Compact reconciliation and the probe wording). docs-keeper PASS twice (design, STATUS, SECURITY and CHANGELOG corrections; its three out-of-card Last-reviewed bumps were reverted). governance-verifier PASS (no locked path changed, 13/13 locks, B6 must-haves intact). tech-scout PASS (no dependency change, no new radar row; it flagged the Upstream editor row as due today). release-manager PASS (in-milestone slice: no version, artifact, signing, feed or publication change).
- CI fixes (PR #59): ecformat flagged one continuation line in `compact.mjs` at odd indentation; it now matches the 6-space sibling indent. The draft STATUS text also carried a literal person-name placeholder token, now replaced with the tech radar wording ("Upstream editor"). No behaviour change; focused 29/29 and `verify.mjs --strict` (465 tests: 456 pass, 9 Windows skips, 0 fail) re-ran green, docs-check 0/0, 13/13 locks, `git diff --check` clean. CI on `1f28866`: `verify` (two runs), `ecformat` and `zizmor` PASS; the X64 Linux build job failed on an external `raw.githubusercontent.com` HTTP 429 while fetching Chromium clang tooling (upstream infrastructure, unrelated to this diff) and cannot be re-run with the current token scope, and the arm64 macOS build was cancelled as in earlier PRs.
- Follow-up: refresh the due Upstream editor row in `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md` (checked 2026-11-25, latest seen 1.148.0, next review 2026-12-02; VSCodium's `stable.json` is still 1.143.1) in a radar-only slice.
- Branch: `vibe/m2/compact` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Status: done.

### 2026-11-25 - M2 B6 Morph direct second option

### 2026-11-25 - M2 B5 provider setup panel and OS secret storage

- Goal / must-haves: let a user add a provider and switch the active provider from a panel without editing any file, pick the environment variable that holds its key, and keep every key in Windows Credential Manager or the process environment so no secret value reaches a settings file, panel HTML, error, log or commit.
- Must-have IDs: B5 (primary); consumes the B1 preset catalog and feeds the B1 request clients.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/secret-store.mjs`, `src/stable/gateway/provider-setup.mjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/provider-setup.cjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/extension.cjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/extension-contract.test.cjs`, `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/package.json`, `tests/vibe/provider-setup.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-setup-panel.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/graph-panel-extension.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/memory-review-extension.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-setup.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, the B1 catalog and request client, the B2 fallback chain, the B3 router and budgets, new dependencies or vendored binaries, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, M0 owner-parked packaging work, live provider calls.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-setup.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-setup-panel.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: a provider can be added and made active through panel messages and through the setup API with no file editing; the settings file on disk contains no secret value; the panel offers environment-variable names (never values) as the key picker and can import a selected variable into the secret store; listing, switching, clearing and validation fail closed on malformed input, accessors, unsafe ids and unknown providers; the Windows Credential Manager adapter round-trips through a fake credential backend, its PowerShell script parses, and its real-OS check skips with recorded evidence when the sandbox has no logon session; no secret value appears in settings, panel HTML, `view()`, `describe()`, errors or any file written under the data directory.
- Out of scope: live provider calls and key validation against a provider, Bifrost, routing-policy changes, the B6 direct-provider feature set, and a rebuilt-app screenshot (environment-limited, M12).
- Delivered: `src/stable/gateway/secret-store.mjs` adds a memory store for tests and the Windows Credential Manager adapter. The adapter runs one of three fixed PowerShell P/Invoke scripts (exported as `CREDENTIAL_MANAGER_SCRIPTS`); the target is `Vibecodium/Provider/<provider-id>` and the base64 secret reaches the child only on stdin, so the value never enters argv, the environment, a file or a log. A missing entry is `null` (or `false` for delete), a Win32 failure raises `secret-store-failed` with only the numeric status, and an unavailable host raises `secret-store-unavailable`. Ids and values are validated (control characters and surrounding whitespace rejected), and the child is the absolute system `powershell.exe` under `%SystemRoot%`, never a bare name that the current directory or PATH could shadow. `src/stable/gateway/provider-setup.mjs` owns the non-secret settings file at the caller's `settingsPath` (version, activeProvider, providers and secretProviders only), writes it atomically with a 0600 mode, rejects a definition containing a secret field on load and on add, rejects accessors, reserved keys, unknown fields and corrupt JSON, and exposes view/list, envNames (names only), addProvider, setSecret, importSecretFromEnvironment, clearSecret, hasSecret, selectProvider, removeProvider (built-ins refused), createClient, settingsForClient and describe. `createClient` reads the stored key, falls back to the provider's configured environment variable when none is stored, and constructs the B1 client internally, so no API returns a key. `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/provider-setup.cjs` adds a CSP + nonce webview panel (no inline handlers) with select/add/set/import/clear/remove/refresh messages, a 64 KiB message cap and bounded errors that never echo a secret; `extension.cjs` registers `vibecodium.providers.open` and `vibecodium.providers.setSetup` and exposes the panel API, and `package.json` declares the activation events, command and palette entry. `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-setup.md` records the contract and the sandbox limitation.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-setup.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-setup-panel.test.mjs` PASS (18 tests: 17 pass, 1 recorded skip, 0 fail). The skip is the real Credential Manager round-trip: the sandbox's restricted token has no logon session and `CredWrite`/`CredRead` return 1312 `ERROR_NO_SUCH_LOGON_SESSION`, while the adapter protocol is exercised through a fake backend and every PowerShell script is parsed. The suite covers add/switch/import/clear/remove, reload, an env-only key that reaches the Authorization header without being stored, accessor/malformed/unknown/corrupt rejection, the absolute-PowerShell path, and secret non-exposure in the settings file, `view()`, `describe()`, `settingsForClient()`, client config, panel HTML and errors. `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 432 tests: 423 pass, 9 Windows skips, 0 fail). `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean; `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok.
- CI: the first push failed the Linux `verify` job because the panel test called `path.isAbsolute`, which rejects a `C:\`-style fixture on non-Windows runners; the same push also failed the ARM32 Linux build (`apt-get update` got HTTP 520 from the Ubuntu mirror) and the X64 Linux build (the rust-cache step found no `Cargo.toml`), both upstream tooling. `de93493` switched the assertion to `path.win32.isAbsolute`; `verify`, `ecformat`, `zizmor`, `docs-check`, the focused suites and both builds then passed on that push. At merge the loong64 build job failed in upstream infrastructure - its container could not download the GitHub CLI (`TAG=null` on all five retries) - not on this diff; every other job that ran succeeded, `anylinux-appimage` is skipped because it depends on that build, and `build (macos-14, arm64)` was cancelled exactly as in PR #51.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS on the env-only revision and PASS again on the final tree after the PowerShell hardening (432 tests: 423 pass, 9 recorded skips, 0 fail; focused 18 tests: 17 pass, 1 recorded skip). drift-sentinel launch returned no report after repeated waits, so the overseer ran the checklist locally: all 17 changed paths are the card's 17 allowed paths, none outside and none unused; no `patches/vibe/` change, no locked doc or `.vibe/governance/` change, no dependency or radar row, and the living docs match the code - PASS. security-reviewer launch returned no report after two launches, so the overseer ran the checklist locally: the diff contains no real credential (only obviously fake `sk-test-...-do-not-leak` fixtures), no value reaches argv, a file, the panel HTML, `view()`, `describe()`, `settingsForClient()`, client config or an error, no dependency/license/CI/binary changed, and the endpoint policy still gates every client request. The local review found and fixed one hardening gap: the adapter spawned the bare `powershell.exe`, which CreateProcess resolves through the current directory before PATH, so a workspace could shadow it and receive the key on stdin; it now spawns the absolute system `%SystemRoot%\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe`, with a test - PASS after the fix. docs-keeper FAIL (the design note claimed a default settings path the code does not provide) then PASS after the note was corrected to say the caller supplies `settingsPath` and that `SETTINGS_FILE` is the conventional name; docs-check --strict is 0/0. governance-verifier PASS (not applicable: 13/13 locks ok, no locked path or governance record changed). tech-scout PASS (not applicable: no dependency, binary or component version changed; only activation events, a command and a menu entry were added, and `TECH_RADAR.md` is unchanged). release-manager PASS (not applicable: not a milestone exit or release build; no version, artifact, signing, feed or publication change). A re-review of the merged tree found two older CHANGELOG sentences that still called the provider setup UI future work and a CI line that said the first push failed only `verify` when the ARM32 and X64 builds had also failed on upstream apt and rust-cache errors; both were corrected in the docs follow-up, docs-check is 0/0, and a local re-check of both fixes passed.
- Branch: `vibe/m2/smart-routing` -> PR into `vibe/main`.

### 2026-11-25 - M2 B3 provider routing and budgets

- Goal / must-haves: route chat and embedding work by the six B3 task classes through a validated policy table, and enforce per-task and per-day token budgets before a provider call can exceed them.
- Must-have IDs: B3 (primary); consumes B1 clients and the B2 fallback chain.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/provider-router.mjs`, `src/stable/gateway/provider-client.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-router.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-routing.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-fallback.md`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-presets.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, changes to the B1 catalog or B2 fallback policy, provider UI, secret storage, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, new dependencies, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-router.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: a policy with all six task classes selects the configured provider and model for each class; chat and embeddings use the class route and report safe routing metadata; a primary failure reaches the class fallback; the router reserves an input-token upper bound plus the class output cap before dispatch and refuses a task or day budget that cannot cover it without calling a provider; successful usage reconciles the reservation; a provider cap is sent on both B1 transports; exhaustion is bounded and secret-free; malformed policy, unknown classes, unsafe task ids, accessor input, invalid budgets and clock rollover are covered.
- Out of scope: Bifrost integration and live interception, provider setup UI, secret storage, cost in currency, automatic task-class classification, cache-aware assembly, live provider smoke tests, and B4 sticky routing. No dependency change.
- Delivered: `src/stable/gateway/provider-router.mjs` adds a validated six-class policy (`plan`, `code-edit`, `quick-edit`, `review`, `summarize`, `embed`) with one to eight unique provider routes per class, policy-owned model and output cap, and a B2 fallback chain per class. Chat classes send the largest class cap and embedding routes send none. Request data is deep-snapshotted into frozen JSON-safe values before estimation and dispatch: accessor-backed arrays or objects, sparse arrays, functions, non-finite numbers, cycles, depth and size limits fail closed without invoking a getter, so a getter cannot change the request between reservation and dispatch. Input reserves the exact `JSON.stringify` UTF-8 byte length of messages or embedding input plus complete tool schemas and fixed allowances; output reserves the cap multiplied by the route count. The reservation is checked against both the per-task and UTC-day budget before any provider call, and an unaffordable request raises `budget-exhausted` with `scope`, `requiredTokens` and `remainingTokens` without dispatching. A positive `usage.totalTokens` reconciles the reservation, absent or zero usage keeps it, and failed calls release it. Settlement releases the reservation from its own UTC-day record and charges actual usage to the settlement day, so an in-flight call crossing midnight cannot subtract a reservation from a day that never held it; a clock failure during settlement falls back to the reservation day. The task map is capped at 10,000 entries and fails closed instead of evicting, and the public API has no `endTask`, so completed usage cannot be reset. `routedBy` reports only class, first provider and model; B2 supplies `servedBy` and attempt metadata. `src/stable/gateway/provider-client.mjs` exports `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` and accepts optional `maxTokens`, sending `max_tokens` on OpenAI Chat and `max_output_tokens` on OpenAI Responses. `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-routing.md` records the contract, fallback, budget and metadata boundaries.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-router.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs` PASS (30/30: all six classes, chat and embeddings routing, B2 failover, task/day hard stops, cap-only and JSON-heavy tool reservations, usage reconciliation, UTC rollover, both transport cap fields, malformed policy/provider/budget/request rejection, request and policy accessor non-invocation, completed-task persistence, the fail-closed task cap, client-secret non-exposure and caller model/cap override rejection). `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 413 tests: 405 pass, 8 Windows skips, 0 fail). `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS after two findings were fixed and independently re-verified (tool schemas were missing from the input reservation; an in-flight reservation could corrupt the next UTC day). drift-sentinel PASS after reconciling the STATUS milestone line, sharing `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, and updating the design for tool counting, midnight reservations, B4 sticky routing and in-memory persistence. security-reviewer FAIL then PASS: it found direct array-index reads that could invoke getters and race estimate against dispatch, an `endTask` reset of a completed task budget, and an input estimate that missed non-string JSON scalars, separators and escaping; the deep snapshot, exact JSON estimate, task-cap fail-closed removal of `endTask`, and non-invocation/JSON-schema/reset/cap regressions close all three. docs-keeper PASS after correcting the first-route model, chat-only cap forwarding, positive/absent usage reconciliation, metadata fields, and stale B2/B3 scope lines in the fallback and presets design notes. governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (13/13 locks; no locked path, governance record or generated agent edit; no secret in the diff). tech-scout PASS as not applicable (Node built-ins and existing modules only; no dependency, binary, manifest or radar change). release-manager PASS as not applicable (not a milestone exit or release build; no version, artifact, signing, feed or publication change; CHANGELOG only under `[Unreleased]`).
- CI: PR #51, 27 checks succeeded and the duplicate `build (macos-14, arm64)` check was cancelled (the same matrix behaviour as PR #50); `verify`, `ecformat`, `zizmor`, `compile`, both macOS builds, every Linux build/reh/alpine job and both AppImage jobs passed at `21a9e00`.
- Next step: B5 point-and-click provider setup is the next unblocked M2 slice, followed by B6 direct-provider parity; Bifrost supervision and live provider smoke tests remain M2 work.
- Branch/PR: `vibe/m2/provider-routing` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Status: done.

### 2026-11-24 - M2 B2 provider fallback

- Goal / must-haves: add an ordered provider fallback layer over the B1 request clients so a primary provider failure returns a complete answer from the next provider within the same turn, without retrying request, authentication, endpoint-policy or cancellation failures.
- Must-have IDs: B2 (primary); consumes B1 clients and prepares B3 routing.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/provider-fallback.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-fallback.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-fallback.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, changes to the B1 catalog or request client, new dependencies or vendored binaries, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, provider UI, budgets/routing policy, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-fallback.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: with two configured local mock providers, forced primary timeout, HTTP 429, HTTP 5xx and empty streaming responses each return the fallback provider's complete answer in the same `chat` call; streamed text and tool calls survive failover; attempt metadata identifies the failed and serving providers; a non-retryable HTTP 400, an endpoint-policy denial and caller cancellation never invoke the fallback; when every provider fails retryably the call rejects with a bounded `fallback-exhausted` error carrying only safe attempt summaries; custom fallback model selection, embeddings failover, malformed chain definitions and secret non-exposure are covered.
- Out of scope: automatic routing by task class, token/day budgets, cache-aware assembly, provider UI and secret storage, Bifrost sidecar supervision, live provider smoke tests, local runtime, and workbench request interception. No dependency change.
- Delivered: `src/stable/gateway/provider-fallback.mjs` adds an ordered chain of one to eight B1 clients. It retries only timeout, network error (including a connection reset while consuming a non-streaming body or SSE stream), HTTP 408/429/5xx and a chat answer with neither text nor a tool call; request/auth validation, further HTTP failures, endpoint-policy denial and caller cancellation propagate unchanged. The caller model applies to the first provider, each later provider uses its own optional model or its client default, and `servedBy` plus frozen attempt summaries report the provider that answered. Exhaustion raises `fallback-exhausted` with only id, label, outcome, error code and integer status, so bodies, prompts and credentials never enter errors or `describe()`. Unknown/duplicate/sparse entries, accessors, invalid labels/models and malformed chains fail closed. `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-fallback.md` records the contract, policy, metadata and boundaries.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-fallback.test.mjs` PASS (14/14: timeout, 429, 5xx, 408, network error and socket reset body/stream failover; empty stream; text/tool streaming; 400, policy and cancellation boundaries; exhaustion; model selection; embeddings; metadata allowlist; malformed/unknown fields). `node --test tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs` PASS (20/20). `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps; 397 tests: 389 pass, 8 Windows skips, 0 fail). `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.
- CI: the first run failed only on `ecformat` (wrong continuation indentation on two lines of the design note); fixed with a three-space continuation and no wording change, and `verify`, `zizmor`, the focused tests and `docs-check` were already green.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS on the final revision (the first pass found a mid-body/mid-stream reset gap; it was fixed and re-verified with discriminating tests); drift-sentinel PASS with no findings; security-reviewer PASS with no findings; docs-keeper PASS (one design wording correction); governance-verifier PASS (no locked path changed; 13/13 locks ok); tech-scout PASS (no dependency or radar row needed); release-manager PASS (N/A: no milestone exit or release artifact).
- Next step: keep M2 open for B3 task-class routing and budgets, B5 point-and-click provider setup, B6 direct-provider feature parity, Bifrost supervision and live smoke tests.
- Branch: `vibe/m2/provider-fallback` -> PR #50 into `vibe/main`.

### 2026-11-24 - M2 B1 provider presets and request client

- Goal / must-haves: give the product a dependency-free provider preset catalog and request client for every B1 provider (OmniRoute, Morph, kie.ai Responses, DeepInfra, Abliteration, OpenRouter, Requesty, any OpenAI-compatible endpoint, llama.cpp and Ollama), verified against a local mock server for chat, streaming and tool calls, with a settings-JSON-only path to add a provider and every request gated by the M1 deny-by-default endpoint policy.
- Must-have IDs: B1 (primary); advances B2/B5 groundwork by making provider configuration data-driven.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/gateway/provider-presets.mjs`, `src/stable/gateway/provider-client.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-presets.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files forbidden: locked docs, upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, new dependencies or vendored binaries, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, Bifrost binaries or sidecar supervision, M0 owner-parked packaging work.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/provider-presets.test.mjs tests/vibe/provider-client.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: the frozen catalog contains one preset for every B1 provider and validates identifiers, transports, capabilities, base URLs and env-var names; each preset and a provider added only through a settings JSON object passes chat, streaming and tool-call turns against a local mock OpenAI-compatible or Responses server, with token deltas and tool arguments assembled correctly; embeddings pass where the preset advertises them; the endpoint policy allows loopback mock hosts by default, denies a remote preset endpoint by default and allows it only through an explicit host or external-provider opt-in; an API key never appears in a returned config, error, or serialized client; malformed settings, URLs, headers and model names fail closed.
- Out of scope: the Bifrost sidecar supervisor and binary pinning, live provider smoke tests, fallback/budget/routing policy (B2/B3), the point-and-click provider UI (B5), local runtime (M3), live request interception in the workbench (M6), and any dependency or binary addition.
- Delivered: `src/stable/gateway/provider-presets.mjs` is a dependency-free, frozen ten-provider catalog (OmniRoute, Morph, kie.ai Responses, DeepInfra, Abliteration, OpenRouter, Requesty, OpenAI-compatible, llama.cpp, Ollama) whose built-ins and settings-JSON entries go through one validator for ids, transports, auth modes, capabilities, base URLs, environment names, labels and models; reserved keys, accessors, prototype pollution and malformed settings fail closed. `src/stable/gateway/provider-client.mjs` implements OpenAI Chat Completions and OpenAI Responses, non-streaming chat, streaming text, streamed and non-streamed tool calls, embeddings only where a preset advertises them, bounded SSE/event/byte/tool-call handling, a distinct `missing-model` error, safe environment reads that accept `process.env`, and one credential read that never reaches `config`, `describe()`, an error or a serialized client. Every request calls the M1 `assertAIEndpointAllowed()` first; local servers stay allowed, a remote preset is denied by default, and `allowExternalProviders` is applied only to endpoints the policy classifies as `external-provider` or `unknown`, scoped to the configured host, so it cannot reach Copilot, telemetry, GitHub or metadata hosts; requests refuse redirects, keep the deadline armed through body consumption, require async-iterable bodies under the byte cap, and reject control or bidi characters in a credential. `docs/vibe/design/m2-provider-presets.md` records the catalog, the settings schema, both transports, the policy boundary and the out-of-scope work.

### 2026-11-24 - M1 endpoint-policy option-handling hardening

- Goal / must-haves: harden `evaluateAIEndpointRequest()` option handling after PR #46 landed the endpoint policy. A caller-supplied option bag must not run accessors, throw out of prototype or key enumeration, or pick up `Object.prototype` pollution as a switch. Endpoint categories and defaults do not change. Advances A2's endpoint-audit prerequisite.
- Must-have IDs: A2 (endpoint-audit prerequisite).
- Files allowed: `src/stable/security/ai-endpoint-policy.mjs`, `tests/vibe/ai-endpoint-policy.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/design/m1-endpoint-audit.md`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/SECURITY.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source and patches, IDE or extension code, new dependencies or vendored binaries, build scripts, CI workflows, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/ai-endpoint-policy.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict`, `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict`.
- Acceptance test: the contract proves a getter is rejected without being invoked; throwing prototype, own-keys and property-descriptor traps plus revoked proxies return `invalid-options`; accessor, holey and revoked string arrays fail closed while a custom iterator never runs; a null-prototype bag remains usable; and `Object.prototype` pollution cannot flip a deny-by-default switch in either evaluation or the policy summary.
- Out of scope: live traffic interception or upstream fetch stubs (M2/Bifrost and M6), enabling the native AI surfaces, the weekly upstream-compile CI job (blocked by the parked `workflow` token scope), provider presets, endpoint inventory or category changes, and any reuse of Copilot endpoints.
- Delivered: hardened `validateOptions()` and the policy summary in the policy core. Accepted options are copied into a null-prototype map, values come from own property descriptors rather than accessor invocation, and accessor descriptors plus throwing prototype, own-key or property-descriptor traps and revoked proxies return `invalid-options`. String option arrays are read only through own data descriptors, so accessor indices, holes, throwing descriptors and revoked arrays fail closed and a custom `Symbol.iterator` never runs. `describeAIEndpointPolicy()` uses the same null-prototype fallback, so an invalid bag cannot surface an inherited switch. Unknown keys still fail closed, `AI_ENDPOINT_POLICY_VERSION` is `1.1.4`, and the design doc, CHANGELOG and SECURITY record the contract. PR #46 had already merged at `0509256`, so this hardening lands as a follow-up PR rather than an amendment to #46.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/ai-endpoint-policy.test.mjs` PASS (26/26); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs --strict` PASS (4/4 steps, 363 tests: 355 pass, 8 Windows skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean; `node vibe/tools/governance.mjs status` 13/13 locks ok. The summary-pollution regression failed before the fallback fix and passed after it.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS (26/26 focused; verify --strict 363 tests, 355 pass, 8 Windows skips, 0 fail; docs 0/0). drift-sentinel PASS after one medium finding: `describeAIEndpointPolicy()` fell back to `{}` and could reflect an inherited switch from an invalid bag; the null-prototype fallback and regression fixed it, and the final tree has no high drift. security-reviewer PASS on the final narrowed option-handling review; two earlier full-context attempts and the default fallback stalled at the provider and were shut down without a verdict. docs-keeper PASS after correcting CHANGELOG's `without running caller code` claim to accessor and iterator non-invocation with unsafe proxy structure failing closed; SECURITY and the design note use the same precise boundary; docs-check --strict 0/0; no locked doc touched. governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (13/13 locks; no locked path or governance record changed; no secret in the diff). tech-scout PASS as not applicable (Node built-ins only; no dependency, binary or radar change). release-manager PASS as not applicable (not a milestone exit or release build; no artifact, version or publication change).
- Next step: M2 wires `evaluateAIEndpointRequest` into the Bifrost fetch boundary and M6 into the agent host.

- Delivered: the D3 memory review panel core. `src/stable/extensions/vibecodium-ai/src/memory-review.cjs` validates every field of a `MemoryStore.review()` page (known fields only, safe ids, the page scope on every item, pending/approved/rejected status, a positive revision, the `untrusted-memory-content` trust marker, JSON-safe values with a 64,000-character aggregate budget, 50,000-node and depth-32 caps, and a page consistent with its own total, limit and `hasMore`). The model exposes a 2,000-character preview, kind, origin, timestamps and session id. The renderer uses a per-document CSP nonce, escapes keys, previews, metadata and notices, rejects bidi/format controls, and renders keyboard-accessible project/user scope tabs, status filters, paging and approve/reject/forget buttons carrying the rendered id and revision. `createMemoryReviewController` registers `vibecodium.memory.open` and `vibecodium.memory.setStore`, re-checks every action against the current page (`stale-view` for an unseen revision, `not-found` for an unrendered id), reports the store's `revision-changed` separately, reloads after every action, reports `no-store`/`load-failed` instead of a stale success when the store disappears, and never lets the webview set or reach the store. The `vibecodium-ai` manifest adds the memory command and exact activation events. Honest boundary: this is a panel core verified with unit tests and a fake `vscode` API; the full in-IDE binding and an Electron screenshot remain M6/M12 work. Future store hardening: a status transition does not bump the revision, so a panel that rendered a pending fact could still reject it after another session approved it at the same revision; the store file was out of scope here.

The acceptance test opens the panel with a real `MemoryStore`, approves at the rendered revision, then recalls the approved value from a new `MemoryStore` over the same root. Additional tests cover stale revisions, forget, filters, paging, an oversized page offset, hostile text, bidi controls, long timestamps, aggregate value budget, malformed pages, unsafe nonces, a disconnected store and the extension manifest.

### 2026-11-23 - M5 C3 cross-project route-to-caller graph

- Goal / must-haves: build the first cross-project graph edge layer over the multi-project registry so a client symbol and an API route definition become one explicit edge across two repositories. This closes the C3 acceptance check without waiting for the IDE graph panel. Must-have IDs: C3.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/cross-project-graph.mjs`, `tests/vibe/cross-project-graph.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, new dependencies or vendored binaries, index-engine source/provider changes, embeddings or vector store changes, graph panel (C4), Understand-Anything integration, docs-mcp, persisted graph tables, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/cross-project-graph.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: graph panel UI, semantic LSP/ast-grep edge extraction, live watcher-driven graph invalidation beyond rebuilding from current indexes, a database schema for graph tables, cross-language framework coverage beyond the documented JS/TS patterns and manifests, MCP gateway execution, IDE binding.
- Delivered: the standalone C3 cross-project edge layer. `src/stable/index-engine/cross-project-graph.mjs` rebuilds a versioned graph from the registered projects' current indexes plus bounded contained reads of JS/TS source, `package.json`, Dockerfile and compose files. It emits project, module, symbol, route, package, env, port, database-table, MCP-tool and container nodes and project-module, route-definition, import, package-dependency, env-use, exposes-port, database-table-use, mcp-tool, container-image and `http-route-caller` edges. `routeCallers(path, { method })` returns the route plus every matching caller edge, tagged with each caller project, so cross-project and in-project callers stay distinguishable; the API + web fixture proves `loadUser` -> `GET /api/users/:id`, `createUser` -> `POST /api/users`, and `healthCheck` -> `GET /health`. GET/POST stay distinct, template URLs are tagged `template`, comments and string-only calls are ignored, and the serialized graph keeps no source values, environment values or request URLs. File reads are project-relative, reject traversal/absolute keys and symlinks, revalidate containment plus device/inode, and are bounded per file, per project and in total. Nodes, edges, caller answers and the snapshot carry `sourceTrust: 'untrusted-repository-content'`; HTTP caller extraction is capped at 1,000 per file and 5,000 per graph, and route matching uses a method-keyed trie. Route indexing is also bounded to 64 segments per route and 200,000 trie nodes; HTTP caller candidates are capped at 20,000 per file and route ranges are merged so exclusion is a binary search; the import/export scan is length-bounded. The public `CrossProjectGraph` constructor validates a persisted snapshot field-by-field, rejecting unknown fields, duplicate ids and missing references, and forces `sourceTrust` on nodes and edges. Every trie node, including parameter nodes, counts against the 200,000-node budget; each read stops at 1 MB + 1 byte and re-checks the size; persisted node and edge ids are restricted to a safe identifier alphabet and edge ids are deduplicated; a saturated route-range table disables caller inference for that file rather than risking false matches. Import edges keep only a resolved project-relative target or package name. Malformed registries, index formats, snapshots, file keys, routes and nodes fail closed with `CrossProjectGraphError`.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/cross-project-graph.test.mjs` PASS (14/14). It covers the C3 route-to-caller acceptance edge from the web client to the API fixture (plus the POST and health cases), client.get/client.post callers, the remaining C3 edge families, template-route normalization, the `untrusted-repository-content` tag, the HTTP caller caps, no-source-value serialization including import tokens, comment and string-only call exclusion, method/path mismatches, malformed registry, index-snapshot, file-key, route and node inputs, per-file size and total-byte bounds, a junction that escapes the project root, a same-path GET/POST routes fixture that fails if the method key is dropped, import specifiers that would leak a URL, UNC path or drive path, a hostile 40,000-token import line, an over-long route path, the trie node budget with both literal and parameter nodes, forged snapshots with injected fields, unsafe or duplicate ids, a missing reference, a bad version and a spoofed trust marker, and a 4,097-range fixture proving caller inference is disabled once the range table saturates. `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps; 276 tests: 271 pass, 5 Windows skips, 0 fail). `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS (final 14/14 focused and 276-test verify; three rounds closed the bounded-read, method-keyed-route, import-sanitization and range-saturation coverage gaps); drift-sentinel PASS (changes limited to the card's seven paths; C3 only; C4 and M6 explicitly disclaimed); security-reviewer PASS (three rounds: bounded import regex; merged and binary route ranges with candidate caps; trie and parameter-node budgets; maxBytes+1 contained read with size recheck; safe persisted ids; duplicate edge-id rejection; snapshot allowlist, version and trust checks; no leakage found); docs-keeper PASS (docs-check 0 errors/0 warnings; no locked path); governance-verifier N/A (no locked path changed; all 13 locks ok); tech-scout N/A (Node built-ins only; no dependency or radar row); release-manager N/A (no milestone exit or release artifact).

### 2026-11-23 - M4 C1 LanceDB vector persistence

- Goal / must-haves: persist the standalone index's file embeddings to LanceDB behind a validated, caller-injected driver, so the vector store can outlive the JSON index and answer vector queries without the index engine taking a native dependency of its own; this closes the last item named in the M4 roadmap row.
- Must-have IDs: C1.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/lancedb-vector-store.mjs`, `src/stable/index-engine/index-engine.mjs`, `tests/vibe/lancedb-vector-store.test.mjs`, `.gitignore`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, bundled native binaries, an npm install inside this repository, embedding-provider or symbol-provider changes, ast-grep/LSP adapters, cross-project graph, graph panel, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/lancedb-vector-store.test.mjs`, `node --test tests/vibe/embedding-retrieval.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: downloading or vendoring `@lancedb/lancedb`, a package manager install in the loop, neural or hosted embeddings, reranking, cross-project vectors, IDE binding, a LanceDB migration for existing indexes, graph tables.
- Delivered: `src/stable/index-engine/lancedb-vector-store.mjs` adds an optional, driver-injected LanceDB store. The caller supplies `@lancedb/lancedb` 0.41.0 (Apache-2.0, published 2026-10-29); nothing is downloaded or bundled here. The store accepts only a local database URI, a short table name, a provider id and dimensions, then merge-inserts by file id, deletes removed files and answers cosine vector queries. `ProjectIndex` accepts a `vectorStore`, mirrors changed and removed files during `refresh()`, backfills a new store on `refresh({ full: true })`, upserts one file on `reindexFile()`, and exposes `searchVectors(query)`. Driver, connection, table and merge-insert builder shapes are validated; file names must be project-relative and free of control, format, bidi, colon and angle characters, trailing dots or spaces, and Windows device names; duplicate ids and unreadable row accessors are rejected; returned rows are checked for file, distance, score and hash. The index snapshot is verified before the store is synced and the store is synced before the index file is written, so a store failure rejects the refresh without committing the index; the two stores are not one transaction, and a failure after the store sync can leave newer vectors until the next refresh reconciles them, while the index stays authoritative for `searchHybrid()`.
- Review fixes: adopted the unpushed prior branch, removed a duplicated `.gitignore` line, hardened project-relative file names (control, format, bidi, colon and angle characters), row and result accessors, duplicate ids, store metadata accessors and returned-hit validation, and moved snapshot verification ahead of the store sync. The security pass then found that the database uri still accepted format and bidi characters, and that the merge-insert builder accessors were read outside the proxy-trap guards; both are fixed with regression tests, cosine distance is bounded to 0-2 instead of an arbitrary 1,000,000, and deletes are split into 200-id filter chunks so one filter cannot grow to tens of megabytes.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/lancedb-vector-store.test.mjs` PASS (17/17); `node --test tests/vibe/embedding-retrieval.test.mjs` PASS; `node --test tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs tests/vibe/structural-query.test.mjs tests/vibe/project-registry.test.mjs` PASS (89 tests: 84 pass, 5 Windows skips); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 262 tests: 257 pass, 5 Windows skips).
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS on the final code via the documented read-only fallback after the registered agent stalled (17/17 store tests; verify 4/4, 262 tests: 257 pass, 5 Windows skips; two independent runs); drift-sentinel PASS (9 changed paths all in scope, no locked path, no dependency, no IDE or release path); security-reviewer PASS after two rounds (round 1 FAIL: the database uri accepted format and bidi characters and the merge-insert builder accessors were read outside the proxy-trap guards; both fixed with regression tests, plus cosine distance bounded to 0-2, deletes chunked at 200 ids and Windows-aliasing file keys rejected; round 2 PASS); docs-keeper PASS via read-only fallback (docs-check --strict 0 errors and 0 warnings; CHANGELOG, ROADMAP, STATUS, TECH_RADAR and BUILD match the code and the stated honest limit); governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (13/13 locked files ok, no locked path changed); tech-scout PASS (radar row present and internally consistent; the live registry re-check was blocked by TLS interception on this host, so the row rests on the 2026-11-23 check recorded for this slice); release-manager PASS as not applicable (M4 in progress, no release build).
- CI: verify, compile, ecformat and zizmor are green. The Linux X64 packaging leg failed on an external GitHub HTTP 429 while fetching Chromium's `update.py` (`429: Too Many Requests` piped into python), and the macOS-14 arm64 leg was cancelled with no runner assigned; both are infrastructure, not this change, and the macOS-14 leg is red on already-merged PRs.
- Next step: continue M5 (cross-project graph and graph panel, memory review UI) and the remaining M0 owner-parked build smoke; loading the native LanceDB module inside the Electron app remains IDE/release work.

### 2026-11-23 - M4 C1 ast-grep structural pattern provider

- Goal / must-haves: close the ast-grep gap recorded in the M4 roadmap by adding an optional ast-grep structural pattern provider over source text, so later graph extraction (routes, env names, tables) and agents can find syntax-shaped matches locally without a new bundled dependency.
- Must-have IDs: C1.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/ast-grep-provider.mjs`, `tests/vibe/ast-grep-provider.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/mock-ast-grep.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, new dependencies or vendored binaries, index-engine symbol-extraction changes, AST-grep NAPI packages, LanceDB, embedding changes, IDE binding, graph panel, cross-project graph, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/ast-grep-provider.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: bundling or downloading ast-grep, a `sgconfig.yml` project scan, symbol-provider wiring, call/route/env/table extraction rules (C3), persistence, cross-project graph, UI.
- Delivered: `src/stable/index-engine/ast-grep-provider.mjs` adds an optional ast-grep structural pattern provider. The caller supplies a trusted local `sg` executable implementing the ast-grep 0.51.3 CLI contract (`sg run --stdin --lang <lang> --pattern <pattern> --json=stream --color never`). The provider writes the source to stdin, never scans a directory or writes a rule file, runs with shell execution disabled, drops secret-named and loader-hijack child variables, and bounds source (1 MB), stdout (4 MB), matches (500), match text (4,000 characters), meta variables (64 names, 32 items), time (5 s default, 60 s maximum) and errors. It converts ast-grep's zero-based UTF-8 positions to one-based positions, normalises its PascalCase language values, infers the language from the file extension, treats exit code 1 as no matches, and rejects malformed streams, wrong-language matches, prototype-polluting meta names and Windows `.cmd`/`.bat` launchers. Nothing is downloaded or bundled, no native NAPI module is loaded, and `ProjectIndex` does not use this provider yet. Returned match and meta-variable text replaces control, format and bidi characters, results carry `sourceTrust: 'untrusted-source-content'`, the child environment also drops URL, URI and DSN variables plus Node, Electron and Bash loader variables, and a timed-out child is escalated from SIGTERM to SIGKILL after 250 ms.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/ast-grep-provider.test.mjs` PASS (14/14); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 245 tests: 240 pass, 5 Windows skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.
