### 2026-11-23 - M4 C1 local embeddings and hybrid retrieval

- Objective / must-haves: complete the C1 embeddings clause and add hybrid retrieval over the standalone index, so an agent can find code by a natural-language query without a full-file dump or an external service.
- Must-have IDs: C1.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/index-engine.mjs`, `src/stable/index-engine/embedding-index.mjs`, `tests/vibe/embedding-retrieval.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, new dependencies, LanceDB or other native binaries, ast-grep/LSP adapters, model downloads, provider calls, cross-project graph, graph panel, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/embedding-retrieval.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: LanceDB persistence, neural or hosted embeddings, ast-grep/LSP adapters, cross-project retrieval, reranking, IDE binding, user-visible UI.
- Delivered: `src/stable/index-engine/embedding-index.mjs` adds a dependency-free `LocalHashEmbeddingProvider` that splits identifiers into lower-case subwords, feature-hashes them into a signed dense vector, and L2-normalizes it. The module validates vectors in place against `MAX_EMBEDDING_VALUE` (1,000,000), scales cosine similarity by each input's largest absolute component so bounded large finite values cannot overflow, drops non-finite hybrid scores, and exposes `hybridSearch`. `ProjectIndex` records embedding metadata in index format v4, persists one vector per file, rejects provider, dimension, format or vector mismatches on load, embeds only changed files, and exposes `searchHybrid(query)`. The default provider is local-only; no source text leaves the module and no network or provider call is made. The local provider requires a lexical anchor; vector-only candidates are opt-in for model-backed providers, so hash collisions cannot invent unrelated hits.
- Review fix: qa-verifier found that vector-only hash collisions made unrelated natural-language queries return hits at 64 dimensions. `allowsVectorOnly` now defaults to false, the local provider sets it false, and `hybridSearch` accepts a zero-lexical hit only when a provider explicitly opts in and its vector score clears `MIN_VECTOR_SCORE`. The no-invented-hits test now covers the five QA collision queries plus two existing unrelated queries, and provider validation rejects a non-boolean capability.
- Acceptance evidence: `node --test tests/vibe/embedding-retrieval.test.mjs` PASS (9/9: deterministic normalized vectors, natural-language top-three retrieval with `src/auth/middleware.ts` first, persisted reload, provider/dimension/format mismatch, malformed and non-finite vectors, forged `1e308` rejected on load, finite score bounds, incremental reindex, query/limit guards and the expanded unrelated-query battery); `node --test tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs` PASS (29/29, including the existing sub-2-second single-file reindex bound); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps; 222 tests: 217 pass, 5 Windows skips, 0 fail); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS after one FAIL/fix cycle (the first run found vector-only hash collisions returning hits for unrelated queries; the fix requires a lexical anchor and expands the unrelated-query battery); drift-sentinel PASS with one medium finding on this card's STATUS delivery wording (corrected here); security-reviewer PASS (no secrets, new dependency, network path or blocking finding); docs-keeper PASS (strict docs check 0/0; STATUS, ROADMAP, BUILD and CHANGELOG verified against the code; no proposal); governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (no locked path; 13 locks ok); tech-scout PASS as not applicable (no dependency or component change; no radar row due); release-manager PASS as not applicable (M4 remains in progress; no release build or publication).
- Accepted lows/follow-ups: the embedding is lexical rather than neural (as documented); `.vibe/index/` is not yet gitignored (outside this card); `__proto__`, `constructor` and `prototype` path segments are accepted but no pollution path was found; hybrid hits are untrusted repository-derived metadata and must be tagged or quarantined before a future IDE binding; a repo-supplied persisted index is structurally validated but not authenticated, so an untrusted checkout must not be trusted before IDE binding; per-query vector revalidation is bounded but could be cached later.
- Delivery status: the verified slice, QA fix and guardian updates are committed on `vibe/m4/embeddings`. The loop's Git HTTPS transport cannot authenticate, so this branch is mirrored by the GitHub Git Data API from the same verified tree and opened as a PR; the PR number and CI result are recorded on the PR.

### 2026-11-23 - M4 C5 bounded structural answers

- Goal / must-haves: give agents bounded structural answers (callers, impact and the path between symbols) over the standalone index instead of making them read whole files, closing the first unmet M4 acceptance check.
- Must-have IDs: C5.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/index-engine.mjs`, `src/stable/index-engine/structural-query.mjs`, `tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs`, `tests/vibe/structural-query.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, dependencies, tree-sitter/ast-grep/LSP binaries, embeddings provider calls, vector store, graph panel, cross-project graph, reranker, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/structural-query.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.

- Delivered: added `src/stable/index-engine/structural-query.mjs`, a bounded structural-query layer over the standalone index (`find`, `callers`, `callees`, `impact`, `path`, `describe`). `index-engine.mjs` now records call sites, and `INDEX_VERSION` is 2. A call resolves to the same file, then the same container, then a uniquely named symbol, then bounded ambiguous candidates; calls at brace depth 0 attach to a synthetic `<module>` node; unresolved calls are counted, never invented. Answers are capped at 200 nodes, 400 edges and 8 KiB of JSON, `counts.returned` is recomputed after trimming, `bytes` is exact, and `path` is atomic (fails closed rather than dropping hops). The index and answer layers reject malformed snapshots, unknown symbol kinds, non-identifier names and unsafe file keys.
- Review fixes: the security pass found a ReDoS in the original call regex and untrusted names/file keys reachable from a persisted index. `extractCalls` is now a single linear scan (a 256 KB identifier run completes in under 20 ms instead of about 62 s), and both `ProjectIndex.load()` and `StructuralQuery` reject non-identifier symbol, container, call and receiver names, unknown symbol kinds, and file keys containing control, format, line/paragraph-separator or markup characters. The dead `endLine` pop in `#callerFor` was removed; symbols never store an end line. Regression tests cover the linearity bound, generic call capture (`foo<Bar>(x)` -> `foo`), and forged names, kinds and file keys through both entry points.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/structural-query.test.mjs` PASS (17/17); `node --test tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs` PASS (29/29); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 203 tests: 198 pass, 5 Windows skips); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.

- Next step: continue M4 with a tree-sitter/ast-grep/LSP provider layer over this index, then embeddings and hybrid retrieval; the graph integration (C4) and embeddings/LanceDB remain outstanding.

### 2026-11-22 - M4 C2 multi-project registry and background watchers

- Goal / must-haves: add a standalone registry that indexes and watches several project roots at once, so three registered repositories stay current while their files are edited without an IDE build.
- Must-have IDs: C2.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/index-engine.mjs`, `src/stable/index-engine/project-registry.mjs`, `tests/vibe/project-registry.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, dependencies, tree-sitter/ast-grep/LSP binaries, embeddings provider calls, vector store, graph layer, reranker, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/project-registry.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: cross-project graph (C3), graph panel (C4), structural query answers (C5), semantic retrieval and LanceDB, IDE binding, LSP, embeddings model calls, persisted registry configuration, and replacing the existing per-project index semantics.
- Delivered: added `src/stable/index-engine/project-registry.mjs`, an in-memory multi-project registry for up to 32 project roots. Each project gets its own `ProjectIndex`, is refreshed in parallel, and is watched by a bounded per-directory watcher tree on Unix, while Windows uses reconciliation polling to avoid recursive-watch crashes and directory-handle locks. Registration installs watchers before a catch-up refresh, so edits made between the first scan and watcher startup are indexed. Ignored trees are excluded before watches are installed, atomically replaced files stay covered, a directory that cannot be listed falls back to polling instead of terminating the host, an index refresh that cannot read the tree fails closed and preserves the previous snapshot when one exists, and failed registration clears any polling timer and closes watchers before removing the project. Registration resolves and pins the real root plus its `dev`/`ino` identity, rejects duplicate roots and unsafe ids, refreshes, and revalidates the root before recording. Registry-owned indexes use `persist:false` and a frozen internal `expectedRoot`; `ProjectIndex` rechecks that identity around reads, re-hashes the candidate with `verifySnapshot()` before publishing, and `load()` verifies before assigning `this.current`. Failed refreshes restore the previous snapshot; a conflicting caller pin cannot override the stored pin; generated index-file and temporary events are ignored while source files in a custom index directory stay watched outside standard ignored directories; moving that directory out drops its stale entries and watches; ignored segments are case-insensitive; unsupported source names are skipped rather than blocking indexing; registration and refresh queues are bounded; rename reconciliation schedules a coalesced refresh after nested watches attach, so a file created in the attach gap is indexed once the registry settles; `close()` releases watchers, timers and lookups.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/project-registry.test.mjs` PASS (35 tests: 30 pass, 5 Windows-only skips; includes three-repo parallel isolation, root swaps, forged snapshots, pinned `load()` ordering, caller-pin conflict, watcher-creation, directory-listing and watcher-error fallback, Windows polling, catch-up after registration, atomic replacement, custom index directory moves, queue bounds, registration cleanup, the `index()` accessor and parallel `refreshAll()`; the Windows skips are the Unix native watcher tree, moved-directory discovery, the post-attachment reconciliation race, ignored-tree watch counts and POSIX filename cases); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 183 tests, 178 pass, 5 platform skips); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS; `git diff --check` clean. QA mutation controls fail without the constructor-copy, load-ordering, caller-pin, persist:false, swap-rejection, watcher-creation fallback, listing fallback, watcher-fallback bound, registration-ordering, polling-cleanup, isolation and queue-bound behaviours.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS at 0c547d6 (code/test tip; 35 registry tests: 30 pass, 5 Windows-only skips; 183 verify tests: 178 pass, 5 skips; deterministic red/green covered the rename-only mutation and the old contamination; all later commits are docs-only); drift-sentinel PASS at cb2594 (final scope check; all 7 task-owned paths are allowed by the card; later changes are docs-only; no high findings); security-reviewer PASS at 92829b8 (final tree; no blocking findings; seven residual low/informational findings recorded below); docs-keeper PASS on the final corrected worktree (strict docs check clean; STATUS ASCII; the seven-finding residual set, Guardians line and Audit trail reconciled; the commit recording this verdict is docs-only); governance-verifier not applicable (no locked path); tech-scout not applicable (Node built-ins only); release-manager not applicable (M4 in progress; no release build).
- Review fixes: stored a frozen copy of `expectedRoot` instead of the caller object; made pinned `load()` verify the snapshot before publishing `this.current`; added regression tests for the constructor pin, load ordering, a conflicting caller pin, `index()` and parallel `refreshAll()`; corrected CHANGELOG/BUILD wording (custom index directory, pinned-index reindex rejection). After PR review, replaced recursive watchers with bounded per-directory watches on Unix and reconciliation polling on Windows, installed watchers before the catch-up refresh, excluded ignored trees before watches are installed, and covered atomic replacement and custom-index moves. A later QA pass removed a Unix-only generated-index race and made the watcher-error and replacement tests fail against the prior watcher design.
- Residual hardening (non-blocking, accepted by security; seven low/informational findings): root identity is checked with path strings (`realpath`/`stat`), so when `dev`/`ino` both report `0` the pinned identity collapses to the resolved path and a same-user rename/junction swap-and-restore around the checks gains no identity information (true `openat`/`NtCreateFile` handle anchoring is outside C2); watchers attach by directory path after a symlink check, so a same-user symlink swap in that window could redirect a watch outside the pinned root (watches are read-only and the index refresh still enforces the pinned root); a polling timer keeps its record reference and checks only the project key, so a project map that is externally mutated and re-registered under the same key could let a stale callback refresh the old record; pinned `load()` re-hashes every file but still trusts the verified-hash JSON `symbols`/`tokens` metadata, so a same-user writer that preserves file hashes could inject symbols; `refreshAll()` starts one refresh per project, so a full 32-project registry can spike concurrent I/O; the registry's project maps and each project's `current` index stay publicly mutable; and `index()`/`symbols()`/`searchText()` expose index data before the verified IDE binding. None is a demonstrated outside-root or cross-project leak; registry indexes stay in memory, and rebuilding entries from the verified buffers or signing the index belongs with the verified IDE-binding work.
- Audit trail: `0c547d6` was labelled test-focused but also updated living STATUS and BUILD docs; the security residual list now records the union of the five findings accepted at `fb9e930` and the two added in `0c547d6`, and security-reviewer re-reviewed all seven at `92829b8`.
- CI fix: after the ecformat job reported `docs/vibe/STATUS.md` as latin1, the file's non-ASCII punctuation (em dash, arrow, middle dot, section sign, smart quotes) was normalized to ASCII without wording changes; the document is now pure ASCII, still valid UTF-8, removing the cause of the ecformat charset failure.
- Next step: continue M4 with tree-sitter/ast-grep/LSP adapters, embeddings, LanceDB and hybrid retrieval, then C5 structural queries and IDE binding; the residual index-metadata hardening is scheduled with the verified binding.
- Branch: `vibe/m4/project-registry` -> PR #28 into `vibe/main`.

### 2026-11-22 - M4 C1 project symbol and text index

- Goal / must-haves: build the standalone per-project code index for the first unmet C1 acceptance check, so a fixture repo indexes at least 95% of its known symbols and one changed file reindexes in under 2 seconds, without the IDE build.
- Must-have IDs: C1.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/index-engine/**`, `tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/repo-ts/**`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, dependencies, tree-sitter/ast-grep/LSP binaries, embeddings provider calls, vector store, graph layer, reranker, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: multi-project registry and watchers (C2), cross-project graph (C3), graph panel (C4), structural query answers (C5), semantic retrieval and LanceDB, IDE binding, LSP, embeddings model calls, incremental watch mode.
- Branch: `vibe/m4/index-engine` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/index-engine/index-engine.mjs` is a standalone per-project symbol and text index. `ProjectIndex(root, options)` refreshes a project, reuses SHA-256-unchanged files, reindexes one file, persists atomically to `.vibe/index/index.json`, reloads with version and count validation, lists symbols by file or kind and searches identifiers case-insensitively. Declarations cover functions, classes, interfaces, types, enums, namespaces, variables, methods, getters, setters and constructors from a deterministic built-in TypeScript/JavaScript parser; tree-sitter, ast-grep and LSP remain M4 work. Comments, strings, template literals and regex bodies are masked before tokenization, including control-flow regexes across comments and `for await`, postfix `++`/`--` division, member calls named like control words (`obj.if()`, `obj?.while()`), and a regex after a division operator. Paths are project-relative POSIX paths; absolute paths, drive prefixes, traversal segments, alternate-data-stream colons, control characters and Windows reserved names for `indexDir`/`indexFile` are rejected. Reads use no-follow where available and are revalidated by device and inode before and after, and index writes are revalidated before and after the atomic rename. Caps cover file size, file and directory counts, symbols, tokens and index bytes; a corrupt, oversized or inconsistent index is rejected on load and rebuilt.
- Review fixes: QA and security tightened the first implementation across several rounds: a quadratic backward parenthesis walk became a forward paren stack with explicit `lastSignificant`/`previousSignificant` token state; close-paren division, postfix division, regex-after-division, comments and `for await` now preserve or mask the right context; property-named control words no longer hide division; the persisted index stores only per-file entries and metadata, never a derived symbol array; and the new `a / /secret/.source` case is masked. Each fix has a regression test, and QA mutation probes confirmed the tests fail without the fixes.
- Accepted lows: regex recognition is lexical and may misclassify exotic valid JavaScript outside the tested contexts; `{} / objLitDiv` loses the operand because a closing brace is regex-ambiguous; template interpolation identifiers are masked (false negative, not a leak); single-character identifiers are skipped as a noise guard; `startsWith('..')` is conservative and can reject legal names beginning with `..`; `O_NOFOLLOW` and Windows ACLs are platform-dependent, so the post-open device and inode checks mitigate but do not eliminate a theoretical TOCTOU window; `readdir` materializes one directory before the `maxFiles` check; a directory junction swapped after validation can yield a discarded outside listing but no indexed entries; the parser is hand-written rather than tree-sitter or LSP.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/index-engine.test.mjs` PASS (25/25); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 143 tests); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings); `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS at the final code (25/25 index, verify 4/4 with 143 tests; independent probe 24/24 fixture symbols, 2.72 ms one-file reindex, path-safety and mutation checks); drift-sentinel PASS (scope unchanged, no locked doc contradicted, no dependency; the later masking fixes stayed inside the allowed files); security-reviewer PASS via the documented read-only fallback with the exact role checklist after the dedicated security-reviewer agents stalled on repo-scale scans (25/25, 143 tests, no secret, path, prototype or unbounded-scan finding); docs-keeper PASS (living docs accurate; no edits needed); governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (no locked path changed); tech-scout PASS as not applicable (first-party module, Node built-ins only); release-manager PASS as not applicable (M4 remains in progress; no release build).
- Next step: continue M4 with a tree-sitter/ast-grep/LSP provider layer over this index, then embeddings and hybrid retrieval; the M4-backed graph integration and the multi-project registry and watchers remain outstanding.

### 2026-11-22 - M5 optional AgentCore adapter

- Goal / must-haves: add the optional AgentCore adapter to the standalone Vibe Context Engine so a session transcript can be mirrored to `agentcore-memory` and hydrated, appended and closed without making the local store depend on the remote. D5.
- Must-have IDs: D5.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/context-engine/**`, `tests/vibe/agentcore-adapter.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, dependencies, real gateway or PostgreSQL clients, credentials, other context-engine modules, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/agentcore-adapter.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: live AgentCore transport and auth, retry/backoff policy, offline queue persistence, IDE binding, memory review UI, MCP, SQLite.
- Branch: `vibe/m5/agentcore-adapter` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/context-engine/agentcore-adapter.mjs` adds an optional `AgentCoreAdapter` for the standalone Vibe Context Engine. With a caller-supplied client it hydrates missing remote records into the local append-only store, mirrors only unmirrored records in batches of at most 1,000, flushes and closes the remote session, and keeps the local store authoritative when the remote is absent or fails. Without a client all three operations are local-only no-ops. Hydrate fully validates remote records, refuses a shorter or conflicting remote transcript, and labels its result `untrusted-session-content`. The payload walk builds a validated snapshot with an exact JSON byte budget before anything is stored; caps are derived from `SessionStore` (999,999 bytes/record, 49,900,000 bytes/session, 100,000 records, 32 nesting, 250,000 payload nodes). `session-store.mjs` now captures an own `__proto__` key as own data so the round trip is verbatim. Errors are fixed-text `AgentCoreAdapterError`s; remote payload text and raw client errors never cross the boundary.
- Review fixes: Security and QA rounds tightened the adapter from the first implementation: byte caps are charged during the payload walk before serialization; lone UTF-16 surrogates and the record envelope are counted exactly; array and own-object `toJSON` are rejected; the walk returns a validated snapshot and only that snapshot is stored; `__proto__` is preserved; record field and response accessors/proxies are wrapped as fixed adapter errors; the non-contiguous-sequence error no longer echoes the remote number. PR review (Devin and Codex) then found two hydration issues: a local append during the remote fetch could append a stale remote tail and advance the cursor, and a null-prototype remote payload was compared by prototype on repeat hydration. `SessionStore.appendIfUnchanged` now re-reads and verifies the expected prefix under the store write queue before writing, so the race fails closed; record comparison is content-based and ignores prototypes and key order. Regression tests cover both. A final security pass asked for the comparison to use the validated snapshot: hydrate now compares `remoteResult.kinds`/`payloads` rather than re-reading a remote accessor, and `appendIfUnchanged` pre-validates the whole batch so a rejected batch writes nothing.
- Accepted lows: Global `Object.prototype.toJSON` pollution can make `SessionStore` reject after the adapter accepts (the adapter snapshot is null-prototype and immune; the environment is caller-controlled). Hydrated records carry no durable per-record trust marker, so store consumers must treat session content as untrusted. The compare-and-append lock is in-process only, consistent with the store's single-writer assumption; an I/O failure after pre-validation can still leave an already-written valid prefix, and the unadvanced cursor makes a retry resume safely.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/agentcore-adapter.test.mjs` PASS (26/26); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 118 tests); `node vibe/tools/docs-check.mjs --strict` PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings).
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS at a3a0ef5 (26/26 adapter, verify 4/4 with 118 tests; the extra null-prototype-remote regression was added during this gate); drift-sentinel PASS at 473f628, confirmed at 218aa39, e619c76 and a3a0ef5; security-reviewer PASS at a3a0ef5 with the accepted lows above; docs-keeper PASS at a3a0ef5 (no edits needed); governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (no locked path changed); tech-scout PASS as not applicable (no dependency or component change); release-manager PASS as not applicable (M5 remains in progress; no release build).
- CI: the first run failed ecformat's charset check because the lengthened CHANGELOG.md was classified latin1. Fixed by making CHANGELOG.md ASCII-only: the header uses the approved ASCII `-` separator (approval `0007-ascii-docs-header`) and one existing D4 sentence uses `->` instead of the arrow. The macOS-14 arm64 build leg is cancelled in 1 s with no runner assigned; the same leg is red on already-merged PR #22, so it is a known infrastructure issue, not a slice failure.
- Next step: continue M5 with agent-host/IDE binding and the memory review panel; the M4-backed graph integration remains outstanding, and ADR-0003 SQLite/TypeScript alignment stays a separately scoped docs slice.

### 2026-11-22 - M5 token-budget context assembly

- Goal / must-haves: add cache-stable token-budget assembly to the standalone Vibe Context Engine so assembled context never exceeds the model window, uses the fixed section order, and reports exactly what was included, omitted or dropped. D4.
- Must-have IDs: D4.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/context-engine/**`, `tests/vibe/token-budget.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, dependencies, AgentCore adapter, memory integration or review UI, SQLite persistence, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/token-budget.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: binding assembly into an agent host or IDE, bundled model-specific tokenizers or provider calls, semantic caching, summarizer quality, AgentCore adapter, automatic compaction triggering, SQLite.
- Branch: `vibe/m5/token-budget` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/context-engine/token-budget.mjs` assembles system, rules, spec, repo map, summary blocks, and fresh-tail records in the fixed architecture order. It uses a conservative UTF-8 byte upper bound by default; a caller-supplied model tokenizer can only raise that count, never lower it, so an under-counting host tokenizer cannot break the window guarantee. It keeps required system, rules and provided spec sections fail-closed, omits an oversized optional repo map whole, includes whole summary blocks and a contiguous newest tail suffix while dropping the rest rather than truncating or rewriting them, and reports included and omitted sections, summary ids, tail sequences, total tokens, the estimator and a per-section sha256 prefix manifest that lets a caller reuse the unchanged prefix. It rejects shared-reference payload graphs and caps payload nodes, object keys, array items, string lengths and total tail/summary characters. In-band untrusted markers wrap the repo map, summary blocks and tail records, marker strings in content are escaped, and source-trust labels distinguish trusted configuration from untrusted repository and session content. A growing fresh tail changes only the tail hash; a revised summary block changes the cache from the summaries section onward.
- Review fixes: PR #22 review found that per-section counts can under-report after joining and that a caller-supplied tokenizer could loosen the window guarantee. The assembler now measures the fully joined prompt, including separators, with the bounded token count (the supplied tokenizer floored at the conservative UTF-8 byte upper bound) before reporting a fit; the fit decision always uses at least that byte bound, so a host tokenizer can only tighten the budget and an under-counting tokenizer cannot make the prompt exceed the window; whitespace-only rules, spec or repo map values now fail closed rather than being silently treated as absent. Exact trimming uses a bounded binary search rather than re-measuring once per dropped record.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/token-budget.test.mjs` PASS (24/24); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 92 tests).
- CI: transient external GitHub failures (the CLI release API returned `null` and a Windows compile runner got HTTP 403 fetching VSCode release assets) required fresh attempts; the merge gate is the latest full PR run, not a superseded attempt.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS at 487bbfc (92 tests, 24/24 token-budget; added repoMap whitespace coverage at e2574ea); drift-sentinel PASS at e2574ea with low-only notes (D4 module-versus-IDE wording, absent patches/vibe/, guardian line pending); security-reviewer PASS at e2574ea after independently reproducing the four prior findings as fixed (CJK under-count and 4096-byte probes rejected; 4,000-case fuzz found no over-budget result); docs-keeper PASS at 487bbfc (BUILD, STATUS and CHANGELOG aligned; out-of-scope SECURITY and ADR review-date edits reverted); governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (no locked path changed); tech-scout PASS as not applicable (no dependency or component change); release-manager PASS as not applicable (M5 remains in progress; no release build).
- Next step: bind assembly into the agent host and IDE context path, then continue M5 with the AgentCore adapter (D5). Keep the project/user memory review panel UI, the noted memory hardening and the ADR-0003 alignment visible; the M4-backed graph integration remains outstanding.

### 2026-11-22 - M5 project and user memory

- Goal / must-haves: add persistent, scoped project and user memory to the standalone Vibe Context Engine, with a review surface for pending facts and recall of approved facts across sessions. D3 storage and review contract; binding the review panel UI remains a separate IDE integration step. (The panel core and a real extension-host acceptance followed on 2026-11-23; full IDE binding remains M6.)
- Must-have IDs: D3.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/context-engine/**`, `tests/vibe/memory.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/vibe/README.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/TECH_RADAR.md`, `docs/vibe/adr/README.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`, `docs/vibe/user/README.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream source, IDE integration, dependencies, AgentCore adapter, token-budget assembly, SQLite persistence, release infrastructure, locked docs.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/memory.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: review panel UI, model-generated extraction, memory deduplication or ranking, AgentCore mirroring, token-budget assembly, SQLite.
- Branch: `vibe/m5/memory` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/context-engine/memory-store.mjs` persists global user facts and project-scoped facts in JSONL event logs. New and revised facts are `pending`, reviews gate recall, revisions preserve stable ids, and `approve`, `reject`, and `forget` require the exact reviewed revision so stale decisions cannot approve a newer value. `recall` returns only approved facts by default, `includePending` is explicit, and every returned entry carries `sourceTrust: untrusted-memory-content`. Project paths use a SHA-256 key derived from the exact id, preventing case-only collisions on Windows. The store rejects a new fact at the 10,000-entry cap while still permitting revisions, and compacts to current live state when the 20,000,000-byte log cannot fit the next event. It validates JSON-safe values and ids, rejects path traversal and Windows-reserved project ids, enforces nesting/key/value/query/page caps, and serializes writes per scope within a store instance. The review API was ready for the later IDE panel at this slice's close; the panel has since landed in the built-in extension and was accepted in a real VS Code extension host on 2026-11-23, with full IDE binding still M6.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/memory.test.mjs` PASS (11/11); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 68 tests); `git diff --check` clean.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS (added revision-validation and compaction-preservation coverage); drift-sentinel PASS using a read-only explorer fallback with the exact checklist after the dedicated role stalled; security-reviewer PASS with low residual notes (cross-instance delete race, Windows ACL hardening, raw helper exposure, wide-value capture cap, caller-supplied secret screening, lexical containment, and compaction durability); docs-keeper PASS after reconciling living docs; governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (no locked path); tech-scout PASS as not applicable (Node built-ins only); release-manager PASS as not applicable (M5 remains in progress and this is not a release build).

The actual `-p` packaging run remains parked.
- Attempts: three `bash vibe/dev/build-windows.sh -p` runs failed before compilation. (1) Git Bash used a non-executable winget `jq` shim, fixed by staging an official checksum-verified jq 1.7.1 locally. (2) node-gyp hit `EPERM` writing to the user Electron-header cache; setting `npm_config_devdir` fixed header download but the VS Code preinstall still hardcoded the user cache for custom-header overlay. (3) A retry with a slice-local cache failed because a Windows path contained an accidental tab; the build did not reach native compilation. No repository source or build script was changed in these attempts.
- Blocked artifact smoke: the three attempts stopped in environment/cache setup before compilation, so the `-p` build and this smoke remain unverified; no product-code dependency is known to be missing. The full acceptance still requires a successful `-p` build plus the packaging smoke command above.
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS after fixing a false-pass CLI guard and adding the nonzero-exit test; drift-sentinel PASS after removing build cache, enforcing packaged commit identity, and adding the "Parked for owner" row; security-reviewer PASS; docs-keeper PASS after correcting the smoke command to build-stamped values and documenting the 7-Zip prerequisite; governance-verifier PASS as not applicable (no locked path changed); tech-scout PASS as not applicable (no new dependency); release-manager PASS as not applicable (no release checkpoint).
- Final verdict: PASS for the delivered packaging-smoke tooling; the actual `-p` installer/zip smoke remains parked and must be rerun after a writable build-cache environment fix.
- Merge outcome: PR #14 merged at 2026-11-21T17:46:22Z as `59af95f9643f1e85c848edfb6f08256f10e6a428`. At the PR head, Windows/Linux compile, Windows/macOS x64 builds, lint, verify, and Devin Review were green or completed, but `build (macos-14, arm64)` was cancelled on the unsupported runner label and two Linux checks later failed on GitHub API 403 rate limits. The rerun was token-blocked; a post-merge correction is in progress.

### 2026-11-21 - M5 lossless session store

- Goal / must-haves: implement the standalone M5 context-engine session store so messages, tool calls, and tool results remain recoverable verbatim after compaction. This is the owner-priority standalone engine work that does not depend on the M0 IDE build.
- Must-have IDs: D1.
- Files allowed: `src/stable/context-engine/**`, `tests/vibe/context-engine.test.mjs`, `docs/vibe/STATUS.md`, `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md`, `docs/vibe/ROADMAP.md`.
- Files forbidden: upstream files, release infrastructure, locked docs, dependencies, installer assets.
- Acceptance commands: `node --test tests/vibe/context-engine.test.mjs`, `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs`.
- Out of scope: summary DAG construction, recall tools, embeddings, AgentCore adapter, UI, provider integration, Windows session-root ACL integration, ADR changes.
- Branch: `vibe/m5/lossless-session-store` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Delivered: `src/stable/context-engine/session-store.mjs` adds an append-only JSONL `SessionStore` for `user`, `assistant`, `system`, `tool_call` and `tool_result` records. Payloads are captured into a canonical JSON-safe snapshot; `Map`, `Date`, `undefined`, `NaN`, functions, symbols, cycles and `toJSON` are rejected. Appends are serialized per instance with contiguous sequence numbers, and the store enforces path containment plus file, record, payload and nesting limits. `compact({ tailCount })` returns the retained tail and removed count without rewriting the append-only log, so the raw transcript stays byte-identical.
- Tests: `node --test tests/vibe/context-engine.test.mjs` PASS (4/4); `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS (4/4 steps, 39 tests).
- Guardians: qa-verifier PASS; drift-sentinel PASS; security-reviewer PASS (Windows session-root ACL hardening deferred to product integration as a Low note); docs-keeper PASS; governance-verifier PASS (no locked path changed); tech-scout PASS (Node built-ins only; ADR-0003 product design remains separate); release-manager PASS (not a milestone exit or release build).
- Next step: continue M5 with the summary DAG, recall tools, cache-stable assembly, project/user memory, AgentCore adapter and docs-mcp.

### 2026-11-21 - I1: loop transport measured and hardened

- Branch: `vibe/f0/ssh-transport` -> PR into `vibe/main`.
- Problem: the loop's Codex session crashed `git-remote-https.exe` and raised a focus-stealing dialog.
- Measured inside the sandbox (the environment that fails): schannel TLS is unusable (`SEC_E_NO_CREDENTIALS`, curl 35, git 128); `http.sslBackend=openssl` clones, commits and pushes cleanly (all exit 0). SSH is unusable too - the sandbox user cannot read the private key. So OpenSSL stays, with a comment recording why, and nobody should "fix" a transport error by switching back.
- Also fixed: the runner now creates `<clone>.worktrees` before each session (the sandbox can write inside it but cannot create it - that broke the first transport test); `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` so a prompt can never block an unattended run.
- Dialog class suppressed at OS level for this user (`HKCU\...\Windows Error Reporting\DontShowUI=1`), outside the repo and reversible.
- Approval record 0009, verifier run on Codex BYOK; `node vibe/tools/verify.mjs` PASS.

## Parked for owner

| Item | Why it needs the owner | Default until decided |
|---|---|---|
| Code-signing route | Paid certificate vs SignPath Foundation (free for open source, needs an application) | Unsigned builds; Windows SmartScreen shows "More info -> Run anyway" |
| Public repository | `ynotfins/vibecodium` is a public fork; GitHub forks cannot be made private. A closed-source product needs a separate private repository | Continue in the public fork; no secrets or machine details in the repo |
| Product name and trademark check for "Vibecodium" | Legal decision | Working name stays "Vibecodium" |
| MSI installer | WiX v3 is archived; WiX v6+ binaries carry a maintenance-fee EULA for revenue-generating users | Ship Inno `.exe` and portable zip only |
| Complete M0 `-p` Windows artifact smoke | Three unattended build attempts failed before compilation on native-header cache permissions; a writable build cache needs an environment fix outside this task worktree | Retry after the build environment provides a writable node-gyp cache and rerun the packaging smoke command in `docs/vibe/dev/BUILD.md` |
| Upstream base | Stay on VSCodium's cadence or move ahead to newer VS Code | Stay on VSCodium's cadence |
| Loop-wide: GitHub `workflow` token scope and Git HTTPS transport | The loop's GitHub token lacks `workflow` scope, so any branch that edits `.github/workflows/*` is rejected on push ("refusing to allow a Personal Access Token to create or update workflow ... without `workflow` scope"). The completed M0 Windows CI parity slice is committed locally on `vibe/m0/ci-parity` but cannot be pushed as a PR; direct Git HTTPS also fails with Schannel `SEC_E_NO_CREDENTIALS` (the GitHub API and Node fetch work). | Owner supplies a repository-scoped token with Workflows read/write (or adds `workflow` to the classic PAT) and fixes the Git credential/TLS transport; no workflow change is merged until then. |
| Insider publish repository | The upstream insider publish workflows target `${repository}-insiders` (`ynotfins/vibecodium-insiders`), which does not exist; creating it is a public-repository business decision | Local `-i` builds target the same branded name, but nothing is published; wire or create it in M12 |
| Ollama activation in the B5 provider setup panel | The M3 lane may only surface a discovered daemon and hand out `{ baseUrl, model }`; switching the active B1 provider and writing provider config is M2/lane A's B5 provider setup work. Lane A should pick it up so a discovered daemon can be activated without typing a URL | The first-run panel reports the daemon and its provider overrides; nothing is activated automatically; the pinned llama.cpp path stays primary |
| Microsoft VC++ 2015-2022 x64 redistributable | The pinned llama.cpp CPU runtime needs `MSVCP140.dll`, `VCRUNTIME140.dll` and `VCRUNTIME140_1.dll`, which come from Microsoft redistributable. It is proprietary freeware, not a permissive open-source license, so bundling it needs a redistribution review and an explicit exception to `ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 8 through a governance proposal, not an owner note alone (ADR-0011 decision 5) | Nothing is bundled or downloaded; the runtime must already be installed on the machine, and the clean-machine live smoke waits for this decision |
| M2 provider-setup Credential Manager test flake | `tests/vibe/provider-setup.test.mjs` performs one real Windows Credential Manager round-trip against a fixed target (`vibe-probe`); one earlier run of seven test files in parallel saw the read return `null` while the file passed 12/12 alone and the same set passed on a retry and inside `verify`. Making it robust (unique target per run or a bounded retry) belongs to M2/B5 provider setup, outside this M3 lane | The test stays as written; a rare red run is re-run before it is treated as a regression. The M3 lane is unaffected because it never touches the credential store |
| Installed-runtime location vs `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 4 | The locked table records user data at `%APPDATA%/Vibecodium` and sidecar binaries under `resources/app/vibe/bin/<platform>`; the new app-data module prefers `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Vibecodium\runtime`, the standard per-user location for a ~200 MB installed binary that must not roam. A locked-doc change needs a governance proposal; any product caller that supplies this root (M6/M12), not only the packaged layout, must carry that proposal, and the M12 packaging decision must also settle the uninstall/upgrade inventory and pruning of old `<id>-<version>` directories | `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Vibecodium\runtime` for the first-run install with `%APPDATA%` as fallback; no bundled sidecar location changes; reconcile the wording and the final packaged location in M12 |
